is the polynomial algebra in the variables
{\bf z}_{\bf g}
,
{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}},i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
naturally extends to a linear map
{\rm Sym}[{\bf z}]\to{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]]
, and any continuous linear endomorphism
F^{*}
of the vector space
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
equipped with the formal power series algebra topology naturally induces a continuous linear endomorphism of the vector space
{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]]
. We denote these extensions by the same symbols.

For any
\beta,\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
and any linear map
F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
we can define linear maps
(F)_{\beta}^{\gamma}:W_{\beta}\to W_{\gamma}
by

(2.5.8)

(F^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\beta}=(F)_{\beta}^{\gamma}{\bf z}^{\beta}.

Now we obtain a triangularity property of the automorphisms
F_{x}^{*}
with respect to the homogeneity of multi–indices.
For this piurpose we note that from (
2.5.7
) one can infer

(2.5.9)

(F_{x})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\gamma}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}(-x)^{{\bf m}-{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}&{\rm if}~\gamma=\delta_{\bf n},~{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.,

where
{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}:W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}\to W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}
is the natural isomorphism.

From formula (
2.5.7
) we also obtain that

F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}}={\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|\end{array}}(F_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}{\bf z}^{\beta}

Together with (
2.5.3
) this implies by multiplicativity that for
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
one has

(2.5.10)

F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma}={\bf z}^{\gamma}+{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{l}\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|{\bf m}_{i}|\leq|\beta_{i}|,\\
i=1,\ldots,p\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt(F_{x})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{1}}\ldots(F_{x})_{\beta_{p}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{p}}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{p}},

where in each term of the sum at least one
\beta_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,p
satisfies
|{\bf m}_{i}|<|\beta_{i}|
. Therefore by linearity of the function
|\cdot|-\alpha
the homogeneity of each such term equal to
|l\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}|
satisfies

|l\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}|-\alpha=|l\delta_{\bf g}|-\alpha+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\beta_{i}|-\alpha)>|l\delta_{\bf g}|-\alpha+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|{\bf m}_{i}|-\alpha)=

(2.5.11)

=|l\delta_{\bf g}|-\alpha+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|-\alpha)=|\gamma|-\alpha,

i.e.
|l\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}|>|\gamma|
.

Thus for any monomial
Z\in\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]
of multi–index degree
\gamma
one has

(2.5.12)

(F_{x}^{*}Z-Z)_{\beta}=0~{\rm for}~|\beta|\leq|\gamma|.

Later we shall also need a similar property of the automomorphisms
F_{x}^{*}
with respect to the modified homogeneity
|\cdot|_{\prec}
defined by

(2.5.13)

|\beta|_{\prec}:=|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}([\beta]+1),~\beta\in{\bf Mult}.

Note that for
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
one has
[\beta]\geq-1
, and hence by the definition for such
\beta
one has
|\beta|_{\prec}\geq|\beta|
. Moreover, for any
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}

[\delta_{\bf n}]=-1
, and hence by the definition
|\delta_{\bf n}|_{\prec}=|\delta_{\bf n}|=|{\bf n}|
.

Using these observations we can immediately rewrite (
2.5.10
) in the form

F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma}={\bf z}^{\gamma}+{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{l}\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|{\bf m}_{i}|_{\prec}\leq|\beta_{i}|_{\prec},\\
i=1,\ldots,p\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt(F_{x})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{1}}\ldots(F_{x})_{\beta_{p}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{p}}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{p}},

where in each term of the sum at least one
\beta_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,p
satisfies
|{\bf m}_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}
. Therefore, similarly to (
2.5.11
), by linearity of the function
|\cdot|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2}
the modified homogeneity of each such term equal to
|l\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}|_{\prec}
satisfies

|l\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2}=|l\delta_{\bf g}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2})>

>|l\delta_{\bf g}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|{\bf m}_{i}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2})=|l\delta_{\bf g}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2})=|\gamma|_{\prec}-\alpha-\frac{d}{2},

i.e.
|l\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}|_{\prec}>|\gamma|_{\prec}
.

Thus for any monomial
Z\in\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]
of multi–index degree
\gamma
one has

(2.5.14)

(F_{x}^{*}Z-Z)_{\beta}=0~{\rm for}~|\beta|_{\prec}\leq|\gamma|_{\prec}.

In the next lemma we study triangularity properties of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
, similar to (
2.5.12
), (
2.5.14
), in a more general setting.

Lemma 2.5.1
.

(i) Let
\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]
be the polynomial algebra in the formal variables
{\bf z}_{\bf g}
,
{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}},i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
, and
F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
a linear map. Assume that for any monomial
Z\in\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]
of multi–index degree
\gamma

(2.5.15)

(F^{*}Z)_{\beta}=0~{\rm for}~|\beta|+N_{\beta}<|\gamma|,

where
N_{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}
may depend on
\beta
. Then
F^{*}
induces a continuous linear map
F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
and a linear map

(2.5.16)

F:\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}

defined by

(2.5.17)

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}F({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}F^{*}({\bf z}^{\gamma}),

where in the left hand side
F
acts on the coefficients of the polynomial
{\bf z}^{\beta}\in W_{\beta}[{\bf z}_{\bf g},\{{\bf z}^{j}_{{{\bf n}}_{i}}:j=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),i=1,\ldots,p\}]
which are elements of
W_{\beta}
,
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
.

Moreover, for any
\beta\in{\bf Mult}

(2.5.18)

F:W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|\leq|\beta|+N_{\beta}\end{array}}W_{\gamma},

and if for any
\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
we denote by
P_{\gamma}
the natural projection operator
P_{\gamma}:\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\to W_{\gamma}
then
(F)_{\beta}^{\gamma}=P_{\gamma}FP_{\beta}
.

(ii) If
F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
is a linear map such that for any monomial
Z\in\mathbb{R}[{\bf z}]
of multi–index degree
\gamma

(2.5.19)

(F^{*}Z-Z)_{\beta}=0~{\rm for}~|\beta|\leq|\gamma|

then
F^{*}
induces a continuous linear map
F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
and a linear map (
2.5.16
).

Moreover, one has

(2.5.20)

F:W_{\beta}\to W_{\beta}\oplus\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}W_{\gamma},

and

(2.5.21)

(F)_{\beta}^{\beta}={\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}},

i.e.

(2.5.22)

(F-{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}):W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}W_{\gamma}.

Also, if

(2.5.23)

(F^{*}Z-Z)_{\beta}=0~{\rm for}~|\beta|_{\prec}\leq|\gamma|_{\prec}

then

(2.5.24)

(F-{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}):W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}W_{\gamma}.

The maps
F,F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
are invertible, and
{F^{*}}^{-1}
is continuous.
Denote by
\rm Id
the identity linear endomorphism of
\bigoplus_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\gamma}
.
Then
F-{\rm Id}
is locally nilpotent, and the inverse to
F
can be defined by
F^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}({\rm Id}-F)^{n}
, where the series in the right hand side converges in the strong operator sense, so that
F^{-1}
satisfies (
2.5.22
), and, if (
2.5.23
) holds, it satisfies (
2.5.24
) as well.

(iii) Continuous linear endomorphisms
F^{*}
of
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
satisfying (
2.5.19
) form a group, and the corresponding linear maps
F
form a group as well.

(iv) The algebra automorphisms
F_{x}^{*}
induce linear maps defined in (
2.5.16
), (
2.5.17
) and they satisfy (
2.5.20
), (
2.5.21
), (
2.5.22
), and (
2.5.24
).

Proof.

(i) Note that the expression in the right hand side of (
2.5.17
) is well defined as

(2.5.25)

\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}(F^{*}({\bf z}^{\gamma}))_{\beta}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|+N_{\beta}\end{array}}F^{\gamma}_{\beta}{\bf z}^{\beta},

and by Lemma
2.3.1
the sum in the right hand side of the last expression is finite. This observation together with (
2.5.15
) immediately imply all statements in part (i).

The first claim in part (ii) and (
2.5.20
) follow from (i) with
N_{\beta}=0
, and (
2.5.19
) also determines
(F)_{\beta}^{\beta}
as described in (
2.5.21
). Property (
2.5.24
) follows from (
2.5.23
).

Identity (
2.5.22
) implies that
F-{\rm Id}
is locally nilpotent, and the inverse to
F
can be defined by
F^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}({\rm Id}-F)^{n}
, where the series in the right hand side converges in the strong operator sense, so that
F^{-1}
satisfies (
2.5.22
), and, if (
2.5.23
) holds,
F^{-1}
satisfies (
2.5.24
) as well. This property also implies that
F^{*}
is invertible and that
{F^{*}}^{-1}
is continuous by the first claim in part (ii) which is already proved.

Part (iii) immediately follows from (ii).

(iv) Since the algebra automorphisms
F_{x}^{*}
satisfy (
2.5.19
) and (
2.5.23
) (see (
2.5.12
) and (
2.5.14
)), by part (ii) they induce linear maps defined in (
2.5.16
), (
2.5.17
) which satisfy (
2.5.20
), (
2.5.21
), (
2.5.22
), and (
2.5.24
).

∎

Now we define an action of the automorphisms
F_{x}^{*}
on the canonical lift
{\bf\Pi}
to relate it to the recentered maps
\Pi_{x}
. We start by introducing some notation and by deriving some preliminary formulas.

For any
\Phi({\bf z})
as in (
2.5.2
) and any continuous linear endomorphism
F^{*}
of the vector space
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
we define

(2.5.26)

(F^{*}\Phi)({\bf z}):=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}(F^{*}\Phi)_{\gamma}({\bf z}^{\gamma}):=F^{*}(\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Phi_{\gamma}({\bf z}^{\gamma})),

where the coefficients of the formal power series in the last expression in the right hand side are obtained by applying
F^{*}
to the formal variables
{\bf z}_{\bf g}
, and
{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}},i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
in the expression
\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Phi_{\gamma}({\bf z}^{\gamma})\in\mathcal{O}(V)[[{\bf z}_{\bf g},\{{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}},i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\}]]
. By the definition of
F^{*}
the expression in the right hand side of (
2.5.26
) is well defined as a formal power series in the formal variables
{\bf z}_{\bf g}
,
{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}},i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
with coefficients in the vector space of
V
–valued functions on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

If a continuous linear map
F^{*}:\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]\to\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
satisfies (
2.5.15
), which is the case for the automorphisms
F_{x}^{*}
(see
2.5.12
), then by (
2.5.17
) and by Lemma
2.3.1
definition (
2.5.26
) can be rewritten in the form

(2.5.27)

(F^{*}\Phi)({\bf z})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}(F^{*}\Phi)_{\gamma}({\bf z}^{\gamma})=\hskip 142.26378pt

\hskip 56.9055pt=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Phi_{\gamma}(F^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})\in\mathcal{O}(V)[[{\bf z}_{\bf g},\{{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}},i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\}]],

where the coefficients of the formal power series in the last expression in the right hand side are obtained by evaluating
\Phi_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{O}_{\gamma}\simeq\mathcal{O}(V)\otimes W_{\gamma}^{*}\subset\mathcal{O}(V)\otimes{\rm Sym}^{*}
on the coefficients of the series
F^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma}\in{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]]
using natural map (
2.5.1
).

Now assume that
F^{*}
is an algebra endomorphism of
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
.
Let
\gamma,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in{\bf Mult}
be such that
\gamma=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}
. Using the multiplicative property
{\bf z}^{\gamma}={\bf z}^{\gamma_{1}}{\bf z}^{\gamma_{2}}
which follows from the definition of
{\bf z}^{\beta}
,
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
, definition (
2.5.8
), and the fact that
F^{*}
is an algebra endomorphism we have for any
\beta\in{\bf Mult}

(2.5.28)

(F)_{\beta}^{\gamma}{\bf z}^{\beta}=(F^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\beta}=(F^{*}({\bf z}^{\gamma_{1}}{\bf z}^{\gamma_{2}}))_{\beta}=(F^{*}({\bf z}^{\gamma_{1}})F^{*}({\bf z}^{\gamma_{2}}))_{\beta}=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\end{array}}(F^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma_{1}})_{\beta_{1}}(F^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma_{2}})_{\beta_{2}}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\end{array}}(F)_{\beta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}({\bf z}^{\beta_{1}})(F)_{\beta_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}}({\bf z}^{\beta_{2}}),

where in the second line we also used the multiplication rule in
{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]]
in the form

(ZW)_{\beta}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\end{array}}(Z)_{\beta_{1}}(W)_{\beta_{2}},~Z,W\in{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]],~\beta\in{\bf Mult}.

Recalling linear isomorphisms (
2.3.2
) we can rewrite (
2.5.28
) as follows

(2.5.29)

(F)_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\end{array}}(F)_{\beta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(F)_{\beta_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}},

where
(F)_{\beta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(F)_{\beta_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}}
is defined as the composition

W_{\beta}\xrightarrow{\sim}W_{\beta_{1}}\otimes W_{\beta_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F_{\beta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\otimes F_{\beta_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}}}}{{\rightarrow}}W_{\gamma_{1}}\otimes W_{\gamma_{2}}\xrightarrow{\sim}W_{\gamma},

and the first and the last maps in the composition are an isomorphism (
2.3.2
) and an inverse isomorphism, respectively.

Now we are in a position to state the main result of this subsection.
The relation (
2.5.33
) in the proposition below is an analogue in the formal power series approach of a similar relation between the canonical lift and the recentered maps in the original Hairer’s framework (see formula (8.26) in
[
27
]
or Proposition 2.1 in
[
16
]
).

Proposition 2.5.2
.

(i) For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, let

(2.5.30)

\Pi_{x}({\bf z})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\beta}({\bf z}^{\beta}),~\Pi_{x}^{-}({\bf z})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}({\bf z}^{\beta}),

be the formal power series defined as in (
2.5.2
), and

(2.5.31)

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}{\bf\Pi}_{\beta}(F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta}),~(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})({\bf z})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}{\bf\Pi}_{\beta}^{-}(F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta})

the formal power series defined as in (
2.5.27
). Then

(2.5.32)

\Pi_{x}({\bf z})=(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z}),~{\rm and}~\Pi_{x}^{-}({\bf z})=(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})({\bf z}).

(ii) For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
relations (
2.5.32
) are equivalent to

(2.5.33)

\Pi_{x}={\bf\Pi}F_{x},

where
\Pi_{x},{\bf\Pi}:{\bf T}\to\mathcal{O}
are defined by (
2.4.19
) and (
2.4.21
), respectively, and we denote by the same symbol
F_{x}
the natural extension of
F_{x}
defined by (
2.5.16
),

(2.5.34)

F_{x}:\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta},

to an automorphism of

(2.5.35)

{\bf T}=\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\simeq(\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta})\otimes V^{*}

acting as in (
2.5.34
) on both summands
\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}
in the last expression in (
2.5.35
), and as the identity transformation on the factor
V^{*}
of the tensor product in that expression.

Proof.

(i) Firstly, we rewrite (
2.5.32
) in terms of components using definitions (
2.5.31
) and recalling (
2.5.12
),

(2.5.36)

\Pi_{x\beta}({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|\leq|\beta|}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\beta})=(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta}({\bf z}^{\beta}),~\beta\in{\bf Mult},

(2.5.37)

\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|\leq|\beta|}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}^{-}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\beta})=(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})_{\beta}({\bf z}^{\beta}),~\beta\in{\bf Mult}.

If
\beta=\delta_{\bf n},~{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
is purely polynomial then we have by (
2.4.8
), (
2.4.13
)

\Pi_{x\delta_{{\bf n}}}(y)=(y-x)^{{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V},~{\bf\Pi}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}(y)=y^{{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}.

Thus from (
2.5.7
) we obtain for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
using the binomial formula that

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}(y)({\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|\leq|{\bf n}|}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})=

=\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf m}|\leq|{\bf n}|}{\bf\Pi}_{\delta_{\bf m}}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf m}}})_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf m}|\leq|{\bf n}|\end{array}}{{\bf n}\choose{\bf m}}y^{{\bf n}}(-x)^{{\bf n}-{\bf m}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}({\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}})=(y-x)^{{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}({\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}})=\Pi_{x\delta_{{\bf n}}}(y)({\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}})

which proves (
2.5.36
) for all polynomial
\beta
.

Similarly, by (
2.4.10
) and (
2.4.15
)

\Pi_{x\delta_{{\bf n}}}^{-}={\bf\Pi}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}^{-}=0.

Thus from (
2.5.7
) we obtain

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}({\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|\leq|{\bf n}|}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}^{-}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})=

=\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf m}|\leq|{\bf n}|}{\bf\Pi}_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{-}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf m}}})_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})=0=\Pi_{x\delta_{{\bf n}}}^{-}({\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}})

which proves (
2.5.37
) for all polynomial
\beta
.

For non–polynomial
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
we shall prove (
2.5.36
) and (
2.5.37
) by induction over
|\beta|
.

For
\beta=0
, which is the only multi–index with
|\beta|=\alpha
, (
2.5.36
) and (
2.5.37
) trivially hold as in this case in (
2.5.36
) and (
2.5.37
)
(F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{0}=0
for
\gamma\neq 0
by (
2.5.4
) and
(F_{x}^{*}1)_{0}=1
by (
2.5.3
), so in the right hand sides of (
2.5.36
) and (
2.5.37
) only the terms with
\gamma=0
are non–trivial, and by (
2.4.4
) these terms are equal to
\Pi_{x0}={\bf\Pi}_{0}=\mathcal{K}\xi^{\rho}
and
\Pi_{x0}^{-}={\bf\Pi}_{0}^{-}=\xi^{\rho}
, respectively.

Now assume that for some
\eta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
\eta\neq 0
identities (
2.5.36
) and (
2.5.37
) hold for all
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
such that
|\beta|<|\eta|
. Then by (
2.4.13
) and (
2.5.7
)

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\eta}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta})=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|\leq|\eta|}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\eta})=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult},\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|\leq|\eta|\end{array}}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\eta})+\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|\leq|\eta|}{\bf\Pi}_{\delta_{\bf n}}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{\bf n}})_{\eta})=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult},\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|\leq|\eta|\end{array}}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\eta})-

-\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|\leq|\eta|}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf m}|<|\eta|-|{\bf n}|\end{array}}\frac{y^{\bf n}(-x)^{\bf m}}{{\bf n}!{\bf m}!}\partial^{{\bf n}+{\bf m}}(\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-})(x)({\bf z}^{\eta})=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult},\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|\leq|\eta|\end{array}}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\eta})-\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|<|\eta|}\frac{(y-x)^{\bf n}}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{{\bf n}}(\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-})(x)({\bf z}^{\eta}),

where at the last step we also used the binomial formula.

Now we rewrite the first sum in the previous formula using (
2.4.14
) and the fact that
{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}^{-}=0
for
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
by (
2.4.15
). This yields

(2.5.38)

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\eta}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta})=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult},\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|\leq|\eta|\end{array}}\mathcal{K}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}^{-}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\eta})-\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|<|\eta|}\frac{(y-x)^{\bf n}}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{{\bf n}}(\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-})(x)({\bf z}^{\eta})=

=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|\leq|\eta|}\mathcal{K}{\bf\Pi}_{\gamma}^{-}(y)((F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\gamma})_{\eta})-\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|<|\eta|}\frac{(y-x)^{\bf n}}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{{\bf n}}(\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-})(x)({\bf z}^{\eta})=

=\mathcal{K}(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})_{\eta}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta})-\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|<|\eta|}\frac{(y-x)^{\bf n}}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{{\bf n}}(\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-})(x)({\bf z}^{\eta}),

where at the last step we also used the fact that
F_{x}^{*}
acts on the formal variables
{\bf z}_{\bf g}
,
{\bf z}^{i}_{{\bf n}}
,
i=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V)
,
{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
, and hence it commutes with the integral operator
\mathcal{K}
.

Now we show that (
2.5.37
) holds for
\beta=\eta
, i.e.
(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})_{\eta}=\Pi_{x\eta}^{-}
, and hence the first term in the right hand side of (
2.5.38
) coincides with
\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta})
. Indeed, evaluating (
2.4.5
) for
\Phi_{\beta}^{-}={\bf\Pi}_{\beta}^{-}
,
\Phi_{\beta_{i}}={\bf\Pi}_{\beta_{i}}
,
i=1,2,3
on
{\bf z}^{\beta}
, using the multiplicativity
{\bf z}^{\beta}={\bf z}_{\bf g}{\bf z}^{\beta_{1}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{2}}
in the first sum,
{\bf z}^{\beta}={\bf z}_{\bf g}^{2}{\bf z}^{\beta_{1}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{2}}{\bf z}^{\beta_{3}}
in the second sum, and
{\bf z}^{\beta}={\bf z}_{\bf g}^{k}{\bf z}^{\beta_{1}}
in the last sum, taking the sum over all
\beta\neq 0
, and adding identity (
2.4.4
) in the form
{\bf\Pi}_{0}^{-}=\xi^{\rho}
we obtain

{\bf\Pi}^{-}({\bf z})={\bf z}_{\bf g}\mathcal{A}({\bf\Pi}({\bf z}),{\bf\Pi}({\bf z}))+{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{2}\mathcal{B}({\bf\Pi}({\bf z}),{\bf\Pi}({\bf z}),{\bf\Pi}({\bf z}))+c({\bf z}_{\bf g}){\bf\Pi}({\bf z})+\xi^{\rho},

where
c({\bf z}_{\bf g})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{k}
, and the last sum is actually finite.

Applying
F_{x}^{*}
to this identity and recalling (
2.5.3
) we deduce using that
F_{x}^{*}
is an automorphism

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})({\bf z})={\bf z}_{\bf g}\mathcal{A}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z}),(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z}))+

+{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{2}\mathcal{B}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z}),(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z}),(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z}))+c({\bf z}_{\bf g})(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})({\bf z})+\xi^{\rho}.

Consider the
\eta
–component of this identity,

(2.5.39)

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})_{\eta}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\delta_{\bf g}=\eta\end{array}}\mathcal{A}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{1}},(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{2}})+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}+2\delta_{\bf g}=\eta\end{array}}\mathcal{B}((F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{1}},(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{2}},(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{3}})+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}k\in\{1,2,3,4\},\beta_{1}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
k\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}=\eta\end{array}}c_{k}(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{1}}.

By Lemma
2.4.1
(i) for all
\beta_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
in the right hand side we have
|\beta_{i}|<|\eta|
, and hence by the induction assumption
(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\beta_{i}}=\Pi_{x\beta_{i}}
, so (
2.5.39
) takes the form

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi}^{-})_{\eta}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\delta_{\bf g}=\eta\end{array}}\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{x\beta_{1}},\Pi_{x\beta_{2}})+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}+2\delta_{\bf g}=\eta\end{array}}\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{x\beta_{1}},\Pi_{x\beta_{2}},\Pi_{x\beta_{3}})+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}k\in\{1,2,3,4\},\beta_{1}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
k\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}=\eta\end{array}}c_{k}\Pi_{x\beta_{1}}=\Pi_{x\eta}^{-},

where at the last step we used definition (
2.4.5
) in the case of
\Pi_{x\eta}^{-}
. This establishes (
2.5.37
) for
\beta=\eta
, and hence (
2.5.38
) takes the form

(F_{x}^{*}{\bf\Pi})_{\eta}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta})=

=\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta})-\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|<|\eta|}\frac{(y-x)^{\bf n}}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{{\bf n}}(\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\eta}^{-})(x)({\bf z}^{\eta})=\Pi_{x\eta}(y)({\bf z}^{\eta}),

where at the last step we used (
2.4.9
).
This confirms (
2.5.36
) for
\beta=\eta
and completes the proof of the induction step.

(ii)
Consider, for instance, the first relation in (
2.5.32
) and expand its left hand side and right hand side using (
2.5.30
) and (
2.5.31
), respectively,

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\beta}({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}{\bf\Pi}_{\beta}(F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta}).

By the first relation in (
2.4.18
) the last displayed identity takes the form

(2.5.40)

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}})(\tau_{\beta})({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}({\bf\Pi}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}})(\tau_{\beta})(F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta}),

or, as
{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}}={\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}^{*}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}
is an identity transformation and
\tau_{\beta}={\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}^{*}}={\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V^{*}}
,

(2.5.41)

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}})(\tau_{\beta})({\bf z}^{\beta})=({\bf\Pi}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}(F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V^{*}})=

=({\bf\Pi}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})(\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta})\otimes{\rm Id}_{V^{*}},

where

F_{x}^{*}{\bf z}^{\beta}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V^{*}}\in W_{\beta}\otimes V^{*}\otimes V[[{\bf z}_{\bf g},\{{\bf z}^{j}_{{{\bf n}}}:j=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\}]]\simeq

\simeq V_{\beta}^{*}\otimes V[[{\bf z}_{\bf g},\{{\bf z}^{j}_{{{\bf n}}}:j=1,\ldots,{\rm dim}(V),{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\}]].

Recalling (
2.5.16
) and (
2.5.17
) and the definition of the extension
F_{x}:{\bf T}\to{\bf T}
in the statement of part (ii) of this proposition we can reduce relation (
2.5.41
) to

(2.5.42)

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}})(\tau_{\beta})({\bf z}^{\beta})=({\bf\Pi}F_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}({\bf z}^{\beta}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V^{*}}).

Applying the arguments which led from (
2.5.40
) to (
2.5.41
) in the opposite order we infer

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}})(\tau_{\beta})({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}({\bf\Pi}F_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}})(\tau_{\beta})({\bf z}^{\beta}).

Similarly one can obtain

\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}^{-}})(\tau_{\beta}^{-})({\bf z}^{\beta})=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}({\bf\Pi}F_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}^{-}})(\tau_{\beta}^{-})({\bf z}^{\beta}).

The last two relations are obviously equivalent to (
2.5.33
). This completes the proof of part (ii).

∎

2.6.
Structure group automorphisms and the structure group

Now we are in a position to define the last component of our smooth model, the structure group maps.

Following
[
30
]
, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
we define structure group automorphisms
\Gamma_{xy}
by

(2.6.1)

\Gamma_{xy}=F_{x}^{-1}F_{y}:\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta},

and denote by the same symbols their extensions to automorphisms of
\bf T

acting as in (
2.6.1
) on both summands
\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}
in the last expression in (
2.5.35
), and as the identity transformation on the factor
V^{*}
of the tensor product in that expression.

Using (
2.5.17
) with
F=\Gamma_{xy}
one can define automorphisms
\Gamma_{xy}^{*}
of the algebra
{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]]
which are actually induced by automorphisms of the subalgebra
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]\subset{\rm Sym}[[{\bf z}]]
by the definition of
F_{x}
and of
\Gamma_{xy}=F_{x}^{-1}F_{y}
.

The automorphisms
\Gamma_{xy}
have the following properties which are similar to properties of analogous automorphisms in
[
5
]
in the case of a single equation for real–valued functions.

Proposition 2.6.1
.

(i) For any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and any
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
one has

(2.6.2)

\Gamma_{xy}:W_{\beta}\to W_{\beta}\oplus\bigoplus_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|<|\beta|}W_{\gamma},

and

(2.6.3)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\beta}={\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}},

i.e

(\Gamma_{xy}-{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}):W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|<|\beta|}W_{\gamma}.

Also,

(2.6.4)

(\Gamma_{xy}-{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}):W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}}W_{\gamma}.

(ii) For any
x,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has

(2.6.5)

\Gamma_{xy}\Gamma_{yz}=\Gamma_{xz},

and
\Gamma_{xx}
is the identity transformation of
\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}
, so that the automorphisms
\Gamma_{xy}
form a group.

(iii) For any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has

(2.6.6)

\Pi_{y}=\Pi_{x}\Gamma_{xy},~\Pi_{y}({\bf z})=(\Gamma_{xy}^{*}\Pi_{x})({\bf z}),~\Pi_{y}^{-}({\bf z})=(\Gamma_{xy}^{*}\Pi_{x}^{-})({\bf z})

(iv) For any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}

(2.6.7)

\Gamma_{xy}^{*}1=1,~\Gamma_{xy}^{*}{\bf z}_{\bf g}={\bf z}_{\bf g},

and

(2.6.8)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\gamma}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}(x-y)^{{\bf m}-{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}&{\rm if}~\gamma=\delta_{\bf n},~{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right..

(v) For any
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
and any
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
one has
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0
,

(2.6.9)

\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp},~\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}\Longrightarrow(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0,

and for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
and any
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
one has
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0
,

(2.6.10)

\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0},~\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\Longrightarrow(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0.

In particular, for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
and any
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}
one has
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0
,

(2.6.11)

\gamma\in\mathcal{M},~\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}\Longrightarrow(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0.

Moreover, if
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
are such that
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\neq 0
then
{\bf m}:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\gamma({\bf n}_{i}){\bf n}_{i}
,
{\bf k}:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\beta({\bf n}_{i}){\bf n}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
satisfy
m_{i}\leq k_{i}
for
i=0,1,2,3
,
W_{\beta}
is naturally isomorphic to
W_{\gamma}
, and

(2.6.12)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=A_{\beta}^{\gamma}(x-y)^{{\bf k}-{\bf m}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}^{W_{\gamma}},

where
A_{\beta}^{\gamma}\neq 0
is a combinatorial numeric factor.

(vi) For every
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
fix the norm on
W_{\beta}
as in Proposition
2.4.2
. Denote by
|\cdot|
the induced operator norms on
{\rm Hom}(W_{\beta},W_{\gamma})
,
\gamma,\beta\in{\bf Mult}
. Then for every compact set
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, every
\rho>0
, and every
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
there exists a constant
D_{{\mathfrak{K}},\beta}^{\gamma}(\rho)\geq 0
such that

(2.6.13)

|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}|\leq D_{{\mathfrak{K}},\beta}^{\gamma}(\rho)|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}}.

(vii) Let

P_{W}:\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\to\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}W_{\beta}=:W,

and

P_{T}:{\bf T}=\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\to\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}=T

be the natural projection operators. Then for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
the linear maps
\Gamma_{xy}^{W}:=P_{W}\Gamma_{xy}P_{W}
(resp.
\Gamma_{xy}^{T}:=P_{T}\Gamma_{xy}P_{T}
for
\Gamma_{xy}
acting on
\bf T
) are automorphisms of
W
(resp.
T
) which form a group called the structure group of the regularity structure
T
, and for any
x,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has

(2.6.14)

\Gamma_{xy}^{W}\Gamma_{yz}^{W}=\Gamma_{xz}^{W},~\Gamma_{xy}^{T}\Gamma_{yz}^{T}=\Gamma_{xz}^{T}.

Proof.

(i) Properties (
2.6.2
), (
2.6.3
) and (
2.6.4
) follow from the definition of
\Gamma_{xy}
and from (
2.5.20
), (
2.5.21
) and (
2.5.24
), respectively, for
F_{x}
and
F_{x}^{-1}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

(ii) We have by the definition of
\Gamma_{xy}

\Gamma_{xy}\Gamma_{yz}=F_{x}^{-1}F_{y}F_{y}^{-1}F_{z}=F_{x}^{-1}F_{z}=\Gamma_{xz}.

Obviously,
\Gamma_{xx}
is the identity transformation by the definition.

(iii) By (
2.5.33
) we have

{\bf\Pi}=\Pi_{x}F_{x}^{-1}=\Pi_{y}F_{y}^{-1},

which is equivalent to the first relation in (
2.6.6
). The last two relations in (
2.6.6
) follow from (
2.5.32
) in a similar way.

(iv) (
2.6.7
) follows immediately from (
2.5.3
), and by (
2.5.9
)

(2.6.15)

(F_{x}^{-1})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\gamma}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}x^{{\bf m}-{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}&{\rm if}~\gamma=\delta_{\bf n},~{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right..

Together with (
2.5.9
) this yields

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\gamma}=\sum_{{\bf k}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}(F_{x}^{-1})_{\delta_{\bf k}}^{\gamma}(F_{y})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\delta_{\bf k}}.

If
\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
the right hand side of the last expression is zero by (
2.6.15
), and if
\gamma=\delta_{\bf n}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
we get using (
2.5.9
) and (
2.6.15
)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{{\bf k}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}(F_{x}^{-1})_{\delta_{\bf k}}^{\delta_{\bf n}}(F_{y})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\delta_{\bf k}}=

=\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf k}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|\leq|{\bf k}|\leq|{\bf m}|\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt{{\bf k}\choose{\bf n}}x^{{\bf k}-{\bf n}}{{\bf m}\choose{\bf k}}(-y)^{{\bf m}-{\bf k}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}.

Observing that in the last expression

{{\bf k}\choose{\bf n}}{{\bf m}\choose{\bf k}}={{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}{{\bf m}-{\bf n}\choose{\bf k}-{\bf n}}

and introducing the new variable of summation
{\bf l}:={\bf k}-{\bf n}
we obtain

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\delta_{\bf n}}={{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf l}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
0\leq|{\bf l}|\leq|{\bf m}-{\bf n}|\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt{{\bf m}-{\bf n}\choose{\bf l}}x^{\bf l}(-y)^{{\bf m}-{\bf n}-{\bf l}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}=

={{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}(x-y)^{{\bf m}-{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}},

where at the last step we used the binomial formula. This completes the proof of (
2.6.8
).

(v) and (vi) Fix a compact subset
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
. Property (
2.6.11
) follows from (
2.6.9
) and (
2.6.10
).

If
\gamma=0
then
[\gamma]=0
, and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
. Thus property (
2.6.10
) is void, and (
2.6.9
) follows from (
2.5.8
) with
F=\Gamma_{xy}
and from the first formula in (
2.6.7
) which imply

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{0}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{\rm Id}_{W_{0}}&{\rm if}~\beta=0\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right..

The last formula also implies (
2.6.13
) in the case when
\gamma=0
and
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
is arbitrary.

We prove (
2.6.9
), (
2.6.10
) and (
2.6.13
) by induction over
|\beta|
. We follow the arguments in §1.18 of
[
5
]
.

\beta=0
is the only possible for which
|\beta|=\alpha
is minimal possible. In this case
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}
, so that properties (
2.6.9
) and (
2.6.10
) are void.

Now assume that for some
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
the statements of (v) and (vi) are true for all
\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
and all
\beta^{\prime}\in{\bf Mult}
with
|\beta^{\prime}|<|\beta|
. As we already observed in the beginning of the proof of parts (v) and (vi), the statements are true for
\gamma=0
and for any
\beta
. Therefore we can assume that
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}\neq 0
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
.

Lemma 2.6.2
.

For any multi–index
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}\neq 0
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
and any algebra endomorphism
F^{*}
of
\mathbb{R}[[{\bf z}]]
such that
F^{*}{\bf z}_{\bf g}={\bf z}_{\bf g}
one has

(2.6.16)

(F)_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt(F)_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}\ldots(F)_{\beta_{p}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{p}}},

where we assume that an empty sum is zero and an empty product is 1.

Moreover, if
p>1
or
l>0
, and
F^{*}
satisfies (
2.5.20
) then in the non–zero terms in the sum in (
2.6.16
)
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|
,
i=1,\ldots,p
.

Proof.

Firstly, iterating (
2.5.29
) and using
F^{*}{\bf z}_{\bf g}={\bf z}_{\bf g}
yields (
2.6.16
).

Since
(F)_{0}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}=0
,
i=1,\ldots,p
by (
2.5.20
) as
|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|=|{\bf m}_{i}|\geq 0>|0|=\alpha
, we may assume that
\beta_{i}\neq 0
,
i=1,\ldots,p
in (
2.6.16
).

From definition (
2.1.5
) it follows that
|\cdot|-\alpha
is a linear function on
{\bf Mult}
achieving its minimum equal to zero at
\beta=0
, and hence for each non–zero term in (
2.6.16
) we have

(2.6.17)

|\beta|-\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\beta_{i}|-\alpha)+|l\delta_{\bf g}|-\alpha,~|\beta_{i}|-\alpha\geq 0,~i=1,\ldots,p.

Recall that
\beta_{i}\neq 0
,
i=1,\ldots,p
, and hence by Lemma
2.3.1

|\beta_{i}|-\alpha>0
,
i=1,\ldots,p
. Observe also that
|l\delta_{\bf g}|-\alpha=l(\alpha+1)\geq 0
. Therefore, if
p>1
or
l>0
, by (
2.6.17
)
|\beta_{i}|-\alpha<|\beta|-\alpha
,
i=1,\ldots,p
, or
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|
,
i=1,\ldots,p
.

∎

Note that by part (i) of this proposition Lemma
2.6.2
holds for
F=\Gamma_{xy}
.
Now if
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}\neq 0
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
then
l\neq 0
, and hence by the previous lemma for all
\beta_{i}
in the right hand side of (
2.6.16
) with
F=\Gamma_{xy}
one has
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|
. So by the induction assumption
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{i}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}\neq 0
only if
\beta_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
, and estimates (
2.6.13
) hold for all such
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{i}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}
. In all these cases

(2.6.18)

\beta_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp},~[\beta_{i}]\geq-1,~i=1,\ldots,p,

and the triangle inequality along with estimates (
2.6.13
) for
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{i}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}
in each term of sum (
2.6.16
) with
F=\Gamma_{xy}
yield for
x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}}

|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}|\leq C_{\beta}^{\gamma}\hskip-34.14322pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-34.14322pt|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}|\ldots|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{2}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{p}}}|\leq

\leq D_{{\mathfrak{K}},\beta}^{\gamma}(\rho)|x-y|^{\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\beta_{i}|-|{\bf m}_{i}|)}=D_{{\mathfrak{K}},\beta}^{\gamma}(\rho)|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}

for some
C_{\beta}^{\gamma}>0
and
D_{{\mathfrak{K}},\beta}^{\gamma}(\rho)\geq 0
as by (
2.6.17
)

|\beta|=\sum_{i=1}^{p}|\beta_{i}|+|l\delta_{\bf g}|-p\alpha,

and similarly from (
2.1.5
)

|\gamma|=\sum_{i=1}^{p}|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|+|l\delta_{\bf g}|-p\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{p}|{\bf m}_{i}|+|l\delta_{\bf g}|-p\alpha,

so

\sum_{i=1}^{p}(|\beta_{i}|-|{\bf m}_{i}|)=|\beta|-|\gamma|.

This proves the induction step for (vi) in the case when
\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
.

Note also that
[\gamma]=l-p
by the definition and that by the linearity of the function
[\cdot]
one has

(2.6.19)

[\beta]=l-p+\sum_{i=1}^{p}([\beta_{i}]+1),

where
\beta_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,p
are introduced in (
2.6.16
).
Therefore if
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
then
l-p\geq 0
, and this fact together with (
2.6.18
) imply that all terms in the right hand side of (
2.6.19
) are non–negative, so
[\beta]\geq 0
, and
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
. This establishes the induction step for (v) in the case when
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
.

If
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
then by the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

(2.6.20)

\gamma=\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}+\delta_{{\bf m}_{2}},~{\bf m}_{1},{\bf m}_{2}\in\mathbb{N}^{4},

or

(2.6.21)

\gamma=2\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}+\delta_{{\bf m}_{2}}+\delta_{{\bf m}_{3}},~{\bf m}_{1},{\bf m}_{2},{\bf m}_{3}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}.

In both cases
[\gamma]=l-p=-1
. Therefore by (
2.6.19
) and by (
2.6.18
) either
[\beta]\geq 0
, i.e.
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, or
[\beta]=-1
if
[\beta_{i}]=-1
for all
i
. In the latter case by (
2.6.18
)
\beta_{i}=\delta_{{\bf k}_{i}}
for some
{\bf k}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
and for all
i
. So that for
\gamma
as in (
2.6.20
) or in (
2.6.21
)

\beta=\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{{\bf k}_{1}}+\delta_{{\bf k}_{2}},~{\bf k}_{1},{\bf k}_{2}\in\mathbb{N}^{4},

or

\beta=2\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{{\bf k}_{1}}+\delta_{{\bf k}_{2}}+\delta_{{\bf k}_{3}},~{\bf k}_{1},{\bf k}_{2},{\bf k}_{3}\in\mathbb{N}^{4},

respectively. In both cases
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, and, as
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta_{{\bf k}_{i}}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}={{\bf k}_{i}\choose{\bf m}_{i}}(x-y)^{{\bf k}_{i}-{\bf m}_{i}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf k}_{i}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}}
, we obtain in this case that by (
2.6.16
) with
F=\Gamma_{xy}
that
W_{\beta}\simeq W_{\gamma}
, and

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=A_{\beta}^{\gamma}(x-y)^{{\bf k}-{\bf m}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}^{W_{\gamma}},~{\bf m}:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\gamma({\bf n}_{i}){\bf n}_{i},~{\bf k}:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\beta({\bf n}_{i}){\bf n}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4},

where
{\bf m}
and
\bf k
satisfy
m_{i}\leq k_{i}
for
i=0,1,2,3
, and
A_{\beta}^{\gamma}\neq 0
is a combinatorial numeric factor. This proves (
2.6.12
).

We also established the induction step for (v) in the case when
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
.

It remains to justify the induction step for (v) and (vi) in the case when
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
. For this purpose we rewrite the second relation in (
2.6.6
) in terms of components using (
2.5.17
), (
2.5.8
) and (
2.5.27
). This yields

\Pi_{y\beta}(w)=\sum_{\eta\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\eta}(w)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta},w\in\mathbb{R}^{4},

where
\circ
stands for the composition of the linear maps
\Pi_{x\eta}(w):W_{\eta}\to V
and
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}:W_{\beta}\to W_{\eta}
defined for each
x,y,w\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

Since by (
2.4.11
) for any
\delta\in{\bf Mult}
one has
\Pi_{x\delta}=0
for
\delta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
and by part (i) of this proposition
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}=0
for
|\eta|>|\beta|
, we obtain from the last formula

(2.6.22)

\Pi_{y\beta}(w)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\eta|\leq|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\eta}(w)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}+\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf m}}(w)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\eta|\leq|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\eta}(w)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}+\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}(w-x)^{{\bf m}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf m}}}^{V}\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}},

where at the last step we used (
2.4.8
).

By the induction step for (v) proved for
\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
we have
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}=0
for
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
. Also, by (
2.4.11
) we obtain
\Pi_{x\beta}=0
for
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
. Thus by (
2.6.22
) in this case

\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}(w-x)^{{\bf m}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf m}}}^{V}\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=0

if
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
, and, since the monomials
(w-x)^{{\bf m}}
are linearly independent for different
{\bf m}
, we deduce that
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=0
for all
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
if
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
. This establishes the induction step for (v) and for
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
and completes the proof of (v).

To justify the induction step for (vi) in the case when
\gamma=\delta_{\bf n}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
we apply
\partial^{\bf n}
with respect to
w
to (
2.6.22
) and evaluate the obtained expression at
w=x
. After rearranging terms and dividing by
{\bf n}!
we obtain

{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{y\beta})(x)-\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\eta|\leq|\beta|\end{array}}\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{x\eta})(x)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}.

Note that by (
2.4.24
) one has
\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{x\eta})(x)=0
for any
\eta\in{\bf Mult}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
such that
|{\bf n}|<|\eta|
, and hence the previous expression takes the form

{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{y\beta})(x)-\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\eta|\leq\min{\{|{\bf n}|,|\beta|\}}\end{array}}\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{x\eta})(x)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}.

By Lemma (
2.3.1
) the sum in the right hand side of the last expression is finite. Hence, using the triangle inequality in the right hand side of it, estimate (
2.4.22
) for the first term in the right hand side and estimates (
2.6.13
) for
|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}|
with
\eta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
|\eta|\leq|\beta|
which hold by the induction assumption and by the induction step already proved for
\beta
and any
\eta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\supset\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, we obtain

(2.6.23)

|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}|\leq\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}|\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{y\beta})(x)|+\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\eta|\leq\min{\{|{\bf n}|,|\beta|\}}\end{array}}|\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{x\eta})(x)||(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\eta}|\leq

\leq\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}|x-y|^{|\beta|-|{\bf n}|}+\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\eta|\leq\min{\{|{\bf n}|,|\beta|\}}\end{array}}|\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{x\eta})(x)|D_{{\mathfrak{K}},\beta}^{\eta}(\rho)|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\eta|},~x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}}.

Since
\Pi_{x\eta}\in\mathcal{O}_{\eta}=\mathcal{O}\otimes V_{\eta}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\otimes V_{\eta}
, their partial derivatives
\partial^{\bf n}(\Pi_{x\eta})(x)
are bounded on the compact subset
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and for
|\eta|\leq|{\bf n}|
one has
|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\eta|}\leq C_{\mathfrak{K}}|x-y|^{|\beta|-|{\bf n}|}
for any
x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}}
and some
C_{\mathfrak{K}}\geq 0
. Therefore (
2.6.23
) implies (
2.6.13
) for
\gamma=\delta_{\bf n}
as by (
2.1.5
)
|{\bf n}|=|\delta_{\bf n}|
in (
2.6.23
). This establishes the induction step for (vi) in the case when
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
and completes the proof of part (vi).

(vii) By (
2.6.11
)
P_{W}\Gamma_{xy}P_{W}=P_{W}\Gamma_{xy}
, and hence by part (ii) of this proposition

\Gamma_{xy}^{W}\Gamma_{yz}^{W}=P_{W}\Gamma_{xy}P_{W}P_{W}\Gamma_{yz}P_{W}=P_{W}\Gamma_{xy}\Gamma_{yz}P_{W}=P_{W}\Gamma_{xz}P_{W}=\Gamma_{xz}^{W}.

This proves the first relation in (
2.6.14
) and together with part (ii) of this proposition justifies that the linear maps
\Gamma_{xy}^{W}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
are automorphisms of
W
which form a group.

A similar claim for the linear maps
\Gamma_{xy}^{T}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and the second relation in (
2.6.14
) immediately follow from the definition of the maps
\Gamma_{xy}^{T}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

∎

In
[
5
,
35
]
arguments based on formulas (
2.6.16
) and (
2.6.22
) are called the algebraic and the three–point argument, respectively. They are common technical proof tools in the multi–index approach to regularity structures.

2.7.
Modelled distributions and the lift of the Langevin equation to an equation for modelled distributions

Estimates (
2.6.13
) are similar to those obtained in Proposition 8.27 in
[
27
]
. Together with parts (i), (iii), (vii) of Proposition
2.6.1
, (
2.4.8
), (
2.4.10
), (
2.4.11
) and Proposition (
2.4.2
) they imply that
(\Pi_{x},\Gamma_{xy}^{T})
is a smooth model for the regularity structure
T
in the sense of Hairer (see
[
27
]
, Definition 2.17).

Estimates (
2.4.22
), (
2.4.23
) and (
2.6.13
) are rather provisional as well as the definition of modelled distributions given in this section. Actually in Section
4
we shall obtain stochastic estimates which hold uniformly for all
\rho>0
, and in Section
5
we show that the models
(\Pi_{x},\Gamma_{xy}^{T})
converge in a certain sense when
\rho\to 0
in such a way that the estimates still hold after taking the limit. The proper definition of modelled distributions should be then given using the limit of the sequence of the smooth models for which global pointwise estimates similar to (
2.4.22
), (
2.4.23
) and (
2.6.13
) are obtained in Section
6
.

The purpose of this section is just to show that the lift of equation (
1.2.12
) to an equation for modelled distributions is reduced to the original equation after applying the so-called reconstruction operator (see Theorem 3.10 in
[
27
]
for its definition).

We start by defining the space of modelled distributions. For any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, let

W_{<\kappa}=\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}W_{\beta}.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to W=\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}W_{\beta}
be a function. For any
\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
we denote
f_{\gamma}(x)=(f(x))_{\gamma}:=P_{\gamma}(f(x))
, where
P_{\gamma}:\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}W_{\beta}\to W_{\gamma}
is the natural projection operator. Note that by Proposition
2.6.1
(i) for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and any
f:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to W_{<\kappa}
we have
\Gamma_{xy}^{W}f(y)\in W_{<\kappa}
. The space of modelled distributions
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
is defined as the space of all functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to W_{<\kappa}
such that for every compact set
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has

(2.7.1)

|||f|||_{\kappa,{\mathfrak{K}}}:=\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}|f_{\beta}(x)|+

+\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}},\\
x\neq y\end{array}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}\frac{|(f(x)-\Gamma_{xy}^{W}f(y))_{\beta}|}{|x-y|^{\kappa-|\beta|}}<+\infty,

where
|\cdot|
in the first term in the right hand side and in the numerator of the second term is the norm on
W_{\beta}
fixed in Proposition
2.4.2
.

As in
[
27
]
one can also define
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
as the set of equivalence classes of
W
–valued functions on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
such that a function
f
is equivalent to
g
if
f_{\beta}=g_{\beta}
for all
\beta
with
|\beta|<\kappa
, and in formula (
2.7.1
) the representative
f
with
f_{\beta}=0
for all
\beta
with
|\beta|\geq\kappa
is used. Using this definition
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa^{\prime}}(W)
can be regarded as a subspace in
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
for any
\kappa^{\prime}\geq\kappa
.

We call a modelled distribution
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
coherent if

(2.7.2)

f_{\delta_{\bf g}}=g,~{\rm and}~f_{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}}=f_{\beta_{1}}f_{\beta_{2}}

for all
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
such that
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
and
|\beta_{1}|
,
|\beta_{2}|
,
|\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}|<\kappa
, where the product
f_{\beta_{1}}f_{\beta_{2}}
is defined using multiplication (
2.3.2
) in the target space and pointwise product of functions.
These conditions imply that

(2.7.3)

f_{0}=1,

and for any
\beta=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=0}^{p}k_{i}\delta_{{\bf n}_{i}}\in\mathcal{M}
one has

(2.7.4)

f_{\beta}=g^{l}\prod_{i=0}^{p}f_{\delta_{{\bf n}_{i}}}^{k_{i}}.

We denote by
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}_{\rm coh}(W)
the subset of coherent modelled distributions in
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
.
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}_{\rm coh}(W)
is not a vector subspace in
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
. But it inherits a topology from
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
.

Since (
1.2.12
) is a system of equations, we shall also need the notion of
V
–valued modelled distributions. For any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, let

T_{<\kappa}=\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa+2\end{array}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}.

Let
{\bf f}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to T\otimes V=(\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*})\otimes V
be a function. For any
\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
we denote
{\bf f}_{\gamma}(x)=({\bf f}(x))_{\gamma}:=(P_{\gamma}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})({\bf f}(x))
(resp
{\bf f}_{\gamma}^{-}(x)=({\bf f}(x))_{\gamma}^{-}:=(P_{\gamma}^{-}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})({\bf f}(x))
), where
P_{\gamma}:{\bf T}\to V_{\gamma}
(resp.
P_{\gamma}^{-}:{\bf T}\to V_{\gamma}^{-}
) is the natural projection operator. Note that by Proposition
2.6.1
(i) for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
we have
(\Gamma_{xy}^{T}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}){\bf f}(y)\in T_{<\kappa}\otimes V
.

For all
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
fix norms on
V_{\beta}^{*}\otimes V
and on
{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\otimes V
. The space of modelled distributions
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T)
is defined as the space of all functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to T_{<\kappa}\otimes V
such that for every compact set
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has

||{\bf f}||_{\kappa,{\mathfrak{K}}}:=\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}|{\bf f}_{\beta}(x)|+\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa+2\end{array}}|{\bf f}_{\beta}^{-}(x)|+

(2.7.5)

+\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}},\\
x\neq y\end{array}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}\frac{|({\bf f}(x)-(\Gamma_{xy}^{T}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}){\bf f}(y))_{\beta}|}{|x-y|^{\kappa-|\beta|}}+

+\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}},\\
x\neq y\end{array}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa+2\end{array}}\frac{|({\bf f}(x)-(\Gamma_{xy}^{T}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}){\bf f}(y))_{\beta}^{-}|}{|x-y|^{\kappa+2-|\beta|}}<+\infty,

where
|\cdot|
in the first and in the second term in the right hand side and in the numerator of the third and of the fourth terms is the fixed norm on
V_{\beta}^{*}\otimes V
or
{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\otimes V
. Since all such norms are equivalent the definition does not depend on the choice of the norms.

One can also define
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T)
as the set of equivalence classes of
T\otimes V
–valued functions on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
such that a function
f
is equivalent to
g
if
{\bf f}_{\beta}={\bf g}_{\beta}
for all
\beta
with
|\beta|<\kappa
, and
{\bf f}_{\beta}^{-}={\bf g}_{\beta}^{-}
for all
\beta
with
|\beta|<\kappa+2
, and in formula (
2.7.1
) the representative
{\bf f}
with
{\bf f}_{\beta}=0
for all
\beta
with
|\beta|\geq\kappa
and
{\bf f}_{\beta}^{-}=0
for all
\beta
with
|\beta|\geq\kappa+2
is used. Using this definition
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa^{\prime}}(T)
can be regarded as a subspace in
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T)
for any
\kappa^{\prime}\geq\kappa
,

(2.7.6)

\mathcal{D}^{\kappa^{\prime}}(T)\subset\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T),~\kappa^{\prime}\geq\kappa.

For any
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(W)
, let

(2.7.7)

{\bf F}(f)(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}\tau_{\beta}({\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}}\otimes f_{\beta}(x))\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T),~x\in\mathbb{R}^{4},

where by
\tau_{\beta}({\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}}\otimes f_{\beta}(x))
it is meant that the second tensor factor of

\tau_{\beta}={\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}^{*}}\in{\rm End}(V_{\beta}^{*})\simeq V_{\beta}^{*}\otimes V_{\beta},

which belongs to
V_{\beta}={\rm Hom}(W_{\beta},V)
, is evaluated on
f_{\beta}(x)\in W_{\beta}
, and the first tensor factor is not affected, so that the result is an element of
V_{\beta}^{*}\otimes V
.

Similarly we define

(2.7.8)

{\bf F}^{-}(f)(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}\tau_{\beta}^{-}({\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}^{-}}\otimes f_{\beta}(x))\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa-2}(T).

Following
[
27
]
, §5 we also introduce the abstract integration map

\mathcal{I}:\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\to\bigoplus_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}}V_{\beta}^{*}

by requiring that

(2.7.9)

\mathcal{I}|_{{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm Id}_{{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}}^{V_{\beta}^{*}}&{\rm if}~\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\\
0&{\rm if}~\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}\end{array}\right.,

where
{\rm Id}_{{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}}^{V_{\beta}^{*}}:{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\to V_{\beta}^{*}
is the natural isomorphism.

For any
r>0
,
r\not\in\mathbb{N}
, let

(2.7.10)

B^{r,loc}_{\infty,\infty}:=\{f:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to\mathbb{R}:|f|_{B^{r}_{\infty,\infty}({\mathfrak{K}})}:=\max_{|{\bf n}|\leq[r]}\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}|\partial^{{\bf n}}f(x)|+

+\max_{|{\bf n}|=[r]}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}},\\
x\neq y\end{array}}\frac{|\partial^{{\bf n}}f(x)-\partial^{{\bf n}}f(y)|}{|x-y|^{\{r\}}}<+\infty~\text{\rm for any compact subset }{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}\},

be the local Hölder space of exponent
r
.

For any
s<0
define also the Besov space
B^{s,loc}_{\infty,\infty}
as the space of tempered distributions
\zeta\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
such that some
r>|s|
and for any compact subset
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}

\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 1}\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}|\zeta(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})|\lambda^{-s}<\infty.

Let
\mathcal{R}:\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T)\to B^{s,loc}_{\infty,\infty}\otimes V
be the natural extension of the reconstruction operator acting on
T
–valued modelled distributions as defined in Theorem 3.10 in
[
27
]
. As it is mentioned in Remark 3.15 in
[
27
]
, in the case when
\Pi_{x}(\varpi)\in B^{r,loc}_{\infty,\infty}
for some
0<r<1
and for all
\varpi\in T
, which is the case in our situation as
\Pi_{x}(\varpi)\in\mathcal{O}\subset B^{r,loc}_{\infty,\infty}
for any
r>0
, the reconstruction operator has a very simple form which implies the following expression for
\mathcal{R}{\bf f}
if
{\bf f}\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T)
for some
0<\kappa<1
,

(2.7.11)

(\mathcal{R}{\bf f})(x)=(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})({\bf f}(x))(x).

In fact, the right hand side of the last formula defines an element of
B^{u,loc}_{\infty,\infty}\otimes V
, where
u=\min\{r,\kappa\}
.

Indeed, by (
2.7.5
) and (
2.7.10
) for any compact subset
{\mathfrak{K}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has for all
x\in{\mathfrak{K}}

(2.7.12)

|(\mathcal{R}{\bf f})(x)|\leq C_{B}\max_{\varpi\in B}\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}|\Pi_{x}(\varpi)(x)|\sup_{x\in{\mathfrak{K}}}|{\bf f}(x)|\leq

\leq C_{B}\max_{\varpi\in B}|\Pi_{x}(\varpi)(x)|_{B^{r}_{\infty,\infty}({\mathfrak{K}})}||{\bf f}||_{\kappa,{\mathfrak{K}}},

where
B
is a basis of
T_{<\kappa}
,
C_{B}>0
,
|\cdot|
in the left hand side is a norm on
V
,
|\cdot|
in the right hand side evaluated at
{\bf f}(x)
is the norm on
T
induced by the norms on
V_{\beta}^{*}\otimes V
and
{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\otimes V
as in (
2.7.5
). For all
x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}}
we also obtain by (
2.7.5
) and (
2.7.10
)

|(\mathcal{R}{\bf f})(x)-(\mathcal{R}{\bf f})(y)|\leq|(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})({\bf f}(x))(x)-(\Pi_{x}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})({\bf f}(x))(y)|+

+|(\Pi_{y}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})((\Gamma^{T}_{yx}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}){\bf f}(x)-{\bf f}(y))(y)|\leq

(2.7.13)

\leq C_{B}^{\prime}\max_{\varpi\in B}|\Pi_{x}(\varpi)(x)|_{B^{r}_{\infty,\infty}({\mathfrak{K}})}||{\bf f}||_{\kappa,{\mathfrak{K}}}|x-y|^{r}+

+C_{B,{\mathfrak{K}}}\max_{\varpi\in B}|\Pi_{x}(\varpi)(x)|_{B^{r}_{\infty,\infty}({\mathfrak{K}})}||{\bf f}||_{\kappa,{\mathfrak{K}}}|x-y|^{\kappa}\leq

\leq C_{B}^{\mathfrak{K}}\max_{\varpi\in B}|\Pi_{x}(\varpi)(x)|_{B^{r}_{\infty,\infty}({\mathfrak{K}})}||{\bf f}||_{\kappa,{\mathfrak{K}}}|x-y|^{\min\{r,\kappa\}},

where
C_{B}^{\prime}
,
C_{B,{\mathfrak{K}}}
,
C_{B}^{\mathfrak{K}}>0
, and in the second term in the right hand side we used the fact that by (
2.4.22
) and (
2.4.23
) one has
\Pi_{x}(\varpi)(x)=0
for any
\varpi\in T_{v}
,
v>0
, and hence when applying (
2.7.5
) in the second term in the right hand side we can use that for some
C_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
C^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{K}}>0
and for all
x,y\in{\mathfrak{K}}
one also has
|x-y|^{\kappa-|\beta|}\leq C_{\mathfrak{K}}|x-y|^{\kappa}
for
|\beta|<0
and
|x-y|^{\kappa+2-|\beta|}\leq C^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{K}}|x-y|^{\kappa}
for
|\beta|-2<0
.

Estimates (
2.7.12
) and (
2.7.13
) imply that

(2.7.14)

(\mathcal{R}{\bf f})(x)\in B^{u,loc}_{\infty,\infty}\otimes V,~u=\min\{r,\kappa\}.

Now for
\kappa>2
, following the ideas of Sections 5 and 7 of
[
27
]
, we consider the following equation for
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}_{\rm coh}(W)

{\bf F}(f)(x)=(\mathcal{I}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}){\bf F}^{-}(f)(x)+

(2.7.15)

+\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|<\kappa}\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\tau_{\delta_{\bf n}}({\rm Id}_{V_{\delta_{\bf n}}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}^{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}(\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}_{m}(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))(x)-

-\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}(\Pi_{x}P_{\leq|{\bf n}|-2}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V})({\bf F}^{-}(f)(x))(x)+\partial^{\bf n}\widehat{u}_{0}(x))),

where
{\rm Id}_{V}^{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}:V\to W_{\delta_{\bf n}}
is the natural isomorphism, for any
a\in\mathbb{R}

P_{\leq a}:{\bf T}=\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}\to\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\beta|\leq a\end{array}}V_{\beta}^{*}\oplus\bigoplus_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\beta|-2\leq a\end{array}}{V_{\beta}^{-}}^{*}

is the natural projection operator, and

(2.7.16)

\widehat{u}_{0}(x)=\widehat{u}_{0}(t,\bar{x})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}K_{m}(t,\bar{x}-\bar{y})u_{0}(\bar{y})d\bar{y}

for some
u_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to V
which plays the role of an initial data.

Equation (
2.7.15
) resembles equation (7.18) in
[
27
]
.
We claim that solutions to equation (
2.7.15
) satisfy (
1.2.12
).

In this section we do not discuss the existence of solutions to equation (
2.7.15
). Actually one can show that the right hand side of (
2.7.15
) belongs to
\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T)
. We do not prove this result as we shall not need it in this paper. We only note that, for any
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
with
|{\bf n}|<2+\varepsilon
,
0<\varepsilon<1
, the
{\bf n}
-th term in the right hand side of (
2.7.15
) is a genuine function as by (
2.7.6
), (
2.7.8
) and (
2.7.14
) one has
\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f)\in B^{\varepsilon,loc}_{\infty,\infty}\otimes V
, so by the classical Schauder estimates (see e.g.
[
33
]
, Theorem 8.9.2) we obtain
\mathcal{K}_{m}\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f)\in B^{\varepsilon+2,loc}_{\infty,\infty}\otimes V
, and
\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}_{m}(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))\in B^{\varepsilon+2-|{\bf n}|,loc}_{\infty,\infty}\otimes V
for
|{\bf n}|<\varepsilon+2
. The third term in the right hand side of (
2.7.15
) is a function by the definition, and for a large class of initial data
u_{0}
the last term is smooth for
t>0
, as
\widehat{u}_{0}(t,\bar{x})
is smooth for
t>0
if, e.g.,
u_{0}
is locally integrable. In particular, if
\kappa=2+\varepsilon
,
0<\varepsilon<1
the right hand side of (
2.7.15
) is a genuine function if
t>0
.

From (
2.7.8
) and (
2.7.9
) it follows that

(\mathcal{I}\otimes{\rm Id}_{V}){\bf F}^{-}(f)(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\beta|<\kappa\end{array}}\tau_{\beta}({\rm Id}_{V_{\beta}}\otimes f_{\beta}(x))\in\mathcal{D}^{\kappa}(T),

and hence by (
2.7.7
) for any
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
the
V_{\beta}^{*}
–component of equation (
2.7.15
) is reduced to the identity
f_{\beta}=f_{\beta}
.

For
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf n}|<\kappa
the
V^{*}_{\delta_{\bf n}}
–component of (
2.7.15
) yields by (
2.4.18
) for
\Pi_{x}
, by (
2.7.7
) and by (
2.7.8
)

(2.7.17)

{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}f_{\delta_{\bf n}}(x)=\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}_{m}(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))(x)-

-\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\beta|\leq|{\bf n}|\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-})(f_{\beta}(x))(x)+\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}\widehat{u}_{0}(x).

Since by the choice of
\alpha
in Section
2.1
and by (
2.1.5
) for
\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
one has
|\beta|\not\in\mathbb{N}
, the sum over
\beta
in (
2.7.17
) is actually over
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
such that
|\beta|<|{\bf n}|
, and hence by (
2.4.12
) for such
\beta
one has
\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}=\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta}
. Thus (
2.7.17
) takes the form

(2.7.18)

{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}f_{\delta_{\bf n}}(x)=\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}\mathcal{K}_{m}(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))(x)-

-\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\beta|\leq|{\bf n}|\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x)+\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}\widehat{u}_{0}(x).

Observe that by (
2.4.18
), (
2.4.24
), (
2.7.2
), (
2.7.3
) and (
2.7.11
)

(2.7.19)

A(x):=(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}(f))(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|\leq 0\end{array}}\Pi_{x\beta}(f_{\beta}(x))(x)=

=v(x)+\Pi_{0}(x)+g\Pi_{\delta_{\bf g}}(x),~v(x):={\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf 0}}}^{V}f_{\delta_{\bf 0}}(x),

as
\delta_{\bf 0}
,
0
and
\delta_{\bf g}
are the only multi–indices with non–positive homogeneities.

Similarly, by (
2.4.18
), (
2.4.25
) and (
2.7.11
)

(2.7.20)

(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\beta|\leq 2\end{array}}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(f_{\beta}(x))(x).

Now for
{\bf n}={\bf 0}
(
2.7.18
) takes the form

(2.7.21)

v(x)=\mathcal{K}_{m}(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))(x)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\beta|\leq 0\end{array}}\Pi_{x\beta}(f_{\beta}(x))(x)+\widehat{u}_{0}(x).

Rearranging terms and using (
2.7.19
) we obtain

(2.7.22)

A(x)=\mathcal{K}_{m}(\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f))(x)+\widehat{u}_{0}(x).

By (
2.7.21
) formula (
2.7.18
) for
{\bf n}\neq{\bf 0}
takes the form

(2.7.23)

{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}f_{\delta_{\bf n}}(x)=\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\partial^{\bf n}v(x)-\frac{1}{{\bf n}!}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
0<|\beta|\leq|{\bf n}|\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x).

Note that for
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
the components
f_{\beta}
are expressed in terms of
f_{\delta_{\bf m}}
,
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf m}|<|\beta|
with the help of (
2.7.4
). Using these expressions together with (
2.7.23
) one can find all
f_{\beta}
,
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
by induction once
f_{\delta_{\bf 0}}
is known, at least when
|\beta|<2+\varepsilon
,
0<\varepsilon<1
and the right hand side of (
2.7.23
) is a genuine function as shown above, so that all products in (
2.7.4
) are defined.

To obtain a closed equation for
f_{\delta_{\bf 0}}
we observe that applying the operator
L
to (
2.7.22
) and using (
2.7.16
) and the definition of
\mathcal{K}_{m}
one gets for
t>0

(2.7.24)

LA=\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f).

We show that this equation coincides with equation (
2.4.2
) which is equivalent to (
1.2.12
).

Indeed, by (
2.7.23
) with
|{\bf n}|=1

\partial^{\bf n}v(x)={\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}f_{\delta_{\bf n}}(x)+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
0<|\beta|\leq 1\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x)=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
0<|\beta|\leq 1\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x),

and hence by (
2.7.19
)

\partial^{\bf n}A(x)=\partial^{\bf n}v(x)+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|<0\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|\leq 1\end{array}}(\partial^{\bf n}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x),

or

\partial_{i}A(x)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta|\leq 1\end{array}}(\partial_{i}\Pi_{x\beta})(f_{\beta}(x))(x),~i=1,2,3.

Substituting this expression and (
2.7.19
) into the right hand side of (
2.4.2
), recalling that
\Pi_{x0}^{-}=\xi^{\rho}
and using (
2.4.24
), (
2.4.25
), (
2.7.2
) and (
2.7.3
) we obtain

g\mathcal{A}(A,A)(x)+g^{2}\mathcal{B}(A,A,A)(x)+cA(x)+\xi^{\rho}(x)=

=g\hskip-22.76228pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1,2}\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq 0,|\beta_{1}|\leq 1\end{array}}\hskip-22.76228pt\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{x\beta_{1}},\Pi_{x\beta_{2}})(f_{\beta_{1}}(x)f_{\beta_{2}}(x))(x)+

+g^{2}\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1,2,3}\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta_{1,2,3}|\leq 0\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{x\beta_{1}},\Pi_{x\beta_{2}},\Pi_{x\beta_{3}})(f_{\beta_{1}}(x)f_{\beta_{2}}(x)f_{\beta_{3}}(x))(x)+

+\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}k\in\{1,2,3,4\},\beta_{1}\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq 0\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274ptc_{k}g^{k}\Pi_{x\beta_{1}}(f_{\beta_{1}}(x))(x)+\Pi_{x0}^{-}(f_{0}(x))(x)=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta\neq 0\end{array}}\left(\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-22.76228pt\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{x\beta_{1}},\Pi_{x\beta_{2}})(f_{\beta}(x))(x)+\right.

+\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}+2\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\\
\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{x\beta_{1}},\Pi_{x\beta_{2}},\Pi_{x\beta_{3}})(f_{\beta}(x))(x)+

\left.+\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}k\in\{1,2,3,4\},\beta_{1}\in{\bf Mult}:\\
k\delta_{\bf g}+\beta_{1}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274ptc_{k}\Pi_{x\beta_{1}}(f_{\beta}(x))(x)\right)+\Pi_{x0}^{-}(f_{0}(x))(x).

Finally by (
2.4.5
) this identity takes the form

g\mathcal{A}(A,A)(x)+g^{2}\mathcal{B}(A,A,A)(x)+cA(x)+\xi^{\rho}(x)=\sum_{\beta\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(f_{\beta}(x))(x)=

=\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:|\beta|\leq 2}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(f_{\beta}(x))(x)=\mathcal{R}{\bf F}^{-}(f)=LA,

where at the last two steps we also used (
2.4.25
), (
2.7.20
) and (
2.7.24
). The last identity confirms that
A
defined by (
2.7.19
) satisfies (
1.2.12
).

In conclusion we note that by (
2.7.19
)
f_{\delta_{\bf 0}}
, and hence all
f_{\beta}
,
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
, can be found once
A
is known.

3.
Analytic tools

In this section we recall some analytic results required for the proof of stochastic estimates for a model in Section
4
. In Section
3.3
we also obtain some results related to Malliavin (directional) derivatives for recentered maps used in this proof.

3.1.
Random variables and function spaces

Let
E
be a finite-dimensional real vector space. Fix a norm
|\cdot|
on
E
.
For any function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to E
we denote by

\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L_{\infty}}:={\rm ess}-\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}|\varphi(x)|

its (essential) supremum norm, and for any
r\in\mathbb{N}
let

\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}:=\max_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|\leq r}\left\|\partial^{\bf n}\varphi\right\|_{L_{\infty}}.

Note that we use definition (
2.1.3
) of
|{\bf n}|
in the last formula.

For any
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\mathcal{B}^{r}:=\{\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{1}):\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}\leq 1\},

and

\mathcal{B}^{r}_{s}:=\{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}x^{\bf n}\varphi(x)dx=0~{\rm for~all}~{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{\bf n}|\leq s\},

where we assume that
\mathcal{B}^{r}_{s}=\{\emptyset\}
if
s<0
.

We shall need a parabolic version of the Morrey inequality which relates the Hölder and the Sobolev seminorms as follows (see
[
2
]
, Theorem 16.10 for the most general scaled version of it,
[
48
]
, Theorem 7.1.1 for the parabolic version, and
[
19
]
, Theorem 8.2,
[
23
]
, Theorem 5.5,
[
48
]
, Theorem 6.1.1 for more elementary proofs of the usual Euclidean version).

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
be a bounded open subset. Denote by
{\rm diam}(\Omega)
the parabolic diameter of
\Omega
,
{\rm diam}(\Omega):=\sup_{x,y\in\Omega}|x-y|
. Assume that there exists
A>0
such that for all
x\in\Omega
,
0\leq r<{\rm diam}(\Omega)
one has

|\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap\Omega}\hskip-28.45274pt1dx|\geq Ar^{d}.

For instance, this condition is satisfied if the boundary of
\Omega
is of class
C^{1}
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
,
\vartheta>d
. Then for
f:\omega\to E
and for all
x,y\in\Omega

(3.1.1)

|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}\lesssim\iint_{\Omega\times\Omega}\frac{|f(z)-f(z^{\prime})|^{p}}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{d+\vartheta}}dzdz^{\prime}|x-y|^{\vartheta-d},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\Omega
,
\vartheta
and
p
.

In particular, we shall use this inequality for
\Omega=B_{R}(y)
,
R>0
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
. Due to obvious translational invariance (
3.1.1
) implies in this case by the triangle inequality

(3.1.2)

|f(x)|^{p}\lesssim|f(y)|^{p}+\iint_{B_{R}(y)\times B_{R}(y)}\frac{|f(z)-f(z^{\prime})|^{p}}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{d+\vartheta}}dzdz^{\prime},~x\in B_{R}(y),

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
R
,
p
and
\vartheta
.

We shall also need the following elementary inequality which is valid for continuously differentiable
E
–valued functions
f
on the interval
(0,\bar{\lambda}]
,
\bar{\lambda}>0
,
1<p<\infty
,
a\in\mathbb{R}
,

(3.1.3)

\sup_{0<t\leq\bar{\lambda}}|t^{-a+\frac{1}{p}}f(t)|^{p}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\bar{\lambda}}(|t^{-a}f(t)|^{p}+|t^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(t)|^{p})dt,

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
a,p,\bar{\lambda}
.

This inequality can be found in
[
4
]
, Appendix B. For completeness we give its proof. The proof is very elementary and relies on the Newton formula.

Indeed, we may assume that left hand side of (
3.1.3
) is finite, and hence
|t^{-a+1}f(t)|=|t^{-a+\frac{1}{p}+1-\frac{1}{p}}f(t)|=|t^{-a+\frac{1}{p}}f(t)|t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\to 0
when
t\to 0
as
1-\frac{1}{p}>0
. Thus if we denote
h(t):=t^{-a+1}f(t)
then we can extend
h
to a continuous function on the interval
[0,\bar{\lambda}]
in such a way that
h(0)=0
. Then, as
h
is continuously differentiable on
(0,\bar{\lambda}]
, by the Newton formula we have for
t\in(0,\bar{\lambda}]

h(t)=\int_{0}^{t}h^{\prime}(s)ds.

Since
h^{\prime}(s)=(1-a)s^{a}f(s)+s^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(s)
the previous identity and the Hölder inequality imply

|h(t)|\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{t}(|s^{a}f(s)|^{p}+|s^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(s)|^{p})ds\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim

\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{\lambda}}(|s^{a}f(s)|^{p}+|s^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(s)|^{p})ds\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}t^{1-\frac{1}{p}},

or recalling that
h(t)=t^{-a+1}f(t)
,

|t^{-a+\frac{1}{p}}f(t)|^{p}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\bar{\lambda}}(|s^{a}f(s)|^{p}+|s^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(s)|^{p})ds,

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
a,p,\bar{\lambda}
. The last inequality yields (
3.1.3
).

For an
E
–valued function
f
of variable
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
1\leq p\leq\infty
, and any measurable subset
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
we denote

\left\|f(x)\right\|_{L_{p}(\Omega,x)}:=\left(\int_{\Omega}|f(x)|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

along with the usual convention that if
p=\infty
the integral in the right hand side is replaced with the essential supremum norm.

If
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{4}
we simply write
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{4})=L_{p}
and
\left\|f(x)\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}=\left\|f\right\|_{L_{p}}
.

For an
E
–valued random variable
f
and
1\leq p\leq\infty
we define

\left\|f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}:=\mathbb{E}^{\frac{1}{p}}(|f|^{p}),

along with the usual convention that if
p=\infty
the corresponding norm is the almost sure supremum norm.

We shall call
E
–valued random distributions
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\otimes E
–valued random variables.
If an
E
–valued random distribution takes values in
C(\mathbb{R}^{4})\otimes E
we call it continuous and if it takes values in
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\otimes E
we call it smooth.

For an
E
–valued random distribution
\zeta
and a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
we mean by
\zeta(\varphi)\in E
the evaluation of
\zeta
on
\varphi
with respect to the first factor in the tensor product
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\otimes E
. We keep the same notation in the case when
\zeta
is actually continuous or smooth.

For any
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and for a family of
E
–valued random variables
f(x)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, which can be regarded as a random variable with values in the space of
E
–valued functions of
x
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, we introduce

\left\|f(x)\right\|_{pq\Omega x}:=\left\|\left\|f(x)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{q}(\Omega,x)}.

The following stochastic version of Sobolev’s inequality can be found in
[
5
]
, Section 3.5 (see
[
1
]
, Section 4.16 or
[
2
]
, Theorem 10.4 for the usual deterministic version of it; the latter reference contains the most general scaled version that we use in the case of a parabolic scaling):

Proposition 3.1.1
.

For any smooth
E
–valued random distribution
f
,
1\leq q<\infty
,
R>0
, and any
k>\frac{d}{2}
one has

\left\|f(y)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|\leq k\end{array}}R^{|{\bf n}|-\frac{d}{2}}\left\|(\partial^{\bf n}f)(x+y)\right\|_{q2B_{R}x},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
k
.

For
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>-s
,
\bar{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\bar{\lambda}>0
, any
E
–valued random distribution
\zeta
we define

(3.1.4)

\left\|\zeta\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}}:=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\zeta(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}}{\lambda^{s}}~{\rm if}~s<0,

and

(3.1.5)

\left\|\zeta\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}}:=\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\zeta(\varphi_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}+

+\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}_{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}}\left\|\zeta(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}}{\lambda^{s}}~{\rm if}~s\geq 0.

Let
\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}
be the space of all
E
–valued random distributions
\zeta
such that
\left\|\zeta\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}}<\infty
. Following the arguments in the proof of Proposition A5 in
[
7
]
one can show that this definition does not depend on the choice of
r
and
\bar{\lambda}
.

\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}
can be regarded as natural stochastic counterparts of Besov spaces (see, e.g.,
[
45
,
46
,
47
]
and Appendix A in
[
7
]
for similar deterministic versions of these spaces and their properties).
Note, however, that in the case when
E=\mathbb{R}
the subspace of
\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}
which consists of elements of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\otimes E=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
is
B_{q\infty}^{s}
in the usual Besov space notation, so that in our notation the indices
p
and
q
do not correspond to the usual indices
p
and
q
in the definition of Besov spaces.

If
s<0
then
\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}
can also be defined as the space of all
E
–valued random distributions
\zeta
such that there exists
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
with

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\varphi(x)dx\neq 0,

and

(3.1.6)

\left\|\zeta\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{pq}^{s}}^{\prime}:=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}}\frac{\left\|\left\|\zeta(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}}{\lambda^{s}}<\infty.

Moreover,
