\mu=\beta
follows from Lemma
4.8.2
, Corollary
4.8.4
and from Proposition
4.8.5
for
|\beta|<2
, and it is derived in Proposition
4.8.6
for
|\beta|>2
(see Corollary
4.8.7
for the exact statement).

Estimate (
4.5.16
) for
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
,
\mu=\beta
is obtained in Proposition
4.9.5
(see Corollary
4.9.6
for the exact statement).

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3.1
.

First note that by Lemma
2.6.1
(iv) and (v) for any smooth model estimate (
4.3.1
) holds for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
and for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
as by this lemma in the former case we can assume that
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
, and in the latter case we can assume that
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, and estimate (
4.3.1
) holds with any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
by (
2.6.8
) and (
2.6.12
), respectively.

Using this observation, (
2.4.8
), (
2.4.11
), (
2.4.10
), (
2.4.5
), Lemma
2.4.1
(i), (iii), (iv), (v), (
2.4.23
) we deduce that for any smooth model estimates (
4.3.2
), (
4.3.3
) also hold for all
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
and for any
0<\lambda<\infty
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
. For instance, if
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
then
\Pi_{0\mu}\neq 0
only if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
, and in this case by Lemma
2.4.1
(i), (iii), (iv), by already established (
4.3.1
) for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and by (
2.4.8
) we have for all
0<\lambda<\infty
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
using the Hölder inequality

(4.5.19)

\left\|\Pi_{0\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}=\left\|\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}\\
|\gamma|<|\mu|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})(\Gamma_{x0})_{\mu}^{\gamma}+\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq

\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}\\
|\gamma|<|\mu|\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\mu}^{\gamma}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}+\left\|\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}\\
|\gamma|\leq|\mu|\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-\alpha}|x|^{|\mu|-|\gamma|}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},

where at the last step we used (
4.7.8
).

The case of
\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}
with
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
is treated similarly noting that in this case
\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}\neq 0
only if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
by Lemma
2.4.1
(v).

By (
3.3.19
) and (
3.3.23
) estimates (
4.5.17
) and (
4.5.18
) trivially hold for any smooth model, for all
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
and for any
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
.

Therefore it suffices to establish estimates (
4.3.1
), (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.3
) for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, and we can assume in the proof that estimates (
4.5.14
), (
4.5.17
), (
4.5.18
), (
4.5.15
) and (
4.5.16
) hold for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
and for any
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
.

Next, using Proposition
4.1.1
we fix a smooth model for which estimates (
4.3.1
), (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.3
) will be established.

Recall that by Lemma
2.3.1
the set
\{|\beta|_{\prec}:\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\}\subset I+\frac{5}{2}\mathbb{N}
is locally finite and bounded from below, so that the set
\mathcal{M}_{N}=\{|\beta|_{\prec}:\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0},~|\beta|_{\prec}<N\}
is finite.

By checking the modified homogeneities of the elements
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\beta|<2
from the list in Lemma
4.1.2
we deduce that the condition
N\geq 17
implies

(4.5.20)

\mathcal{M}_{N}\supset\{|\beta|_{\prec}:\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0},|\beta|<2\}.

As before, let
n_{0}<n_{1}<\ldots<n_{M(N)}
be the elements of the set
\mathcal{M}_{N}
, and for
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
,
k=0,\ldots,M(N)
denote
\mathcal{M}_{k}=\{|\beta|_{\prec}:\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0},~|\beta|_{\prec}=n_{k}\}
,
\bar{\lambda}_{k}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{0}}{32^{k}}
. Fix
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
such that
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 1
.

Now for any given mollification parameter
\rho>0
consider the smooth model
(\Pi_{x},\Gamma_{xy}^{T})
which is uniquely defined as in Proposition
4.1.1
by the generalized BPHZ conditions

(4.5.21)

\mathbb{E}\Pi_{0~k\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{\bf 0}}^{-}(\psi^{\bar{\lambda}_{n_{i_{k}-1}}})=0,

where
\psi
is introduced in (
4.5.13
), and for
k\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{\bf 0}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
k=1,2,3,4
we write
|k\delta_{\bf g}+\delta_{\bf 0}|_{\prec}=n_{i_{k}}
.

We claim that for this model estimates (
4.3.1
), (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.3
) hold. The proof is by induction over the modified homogeneity.

Firstly, one can verify straightforwardly that
\mu=0
is the only element of
\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
with minimal possible modified homogeneity,
|0|_{\prec}=2-{\varepsilon}
. For
\mu=0
and arbitrary
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}=\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
estimates (
4.5.14
), (
4.5.17
), (
4.5.18
), (
4.5.15
) and (
4.5.16
) follow from the results of Section
4.4
, see Proposition
4.4.1
for (
4.5.14
), Propositions
4.4.2
and
4.4.9
for (
4.5.17
), Propositions
4.4.3
and
4.4.10
for (
4.5.18
), Proposition
4.4.7
for (
4.5.15
), and Proposition
4.4.8
for (
4.5.16
). Therefore for
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, such that
\beta\neq 0
and
|\beta|_{\prec}
is minimal possible, the induction assumptions are satisfied, and we can begin the induction procedure over the modified homogeneity the induction step of which is established in Proposition
4.5.11
. Conditions (
4.5.21
) ensure that the generalized BPHZ conditions used in the induction procedure are satisfied.

Note that by the condition
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 1
the induction step can be iterated until we exhaust the set of all multi-indices
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
with
|\beta|_{\prec}<N
, and after completing the last iteration we obtain estimates (
4.3.1
), (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.3
) for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
.

Finally we prove the last claim of the theorem. Observing that by the definition
[\beta]=-1
for all
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}\cup(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0})
we deduce that for such
\beta
one has
|\beta|=|\beta|_{\prec}
. Now from (
2.5.6
), (
4.5.20
), from formulas (
2.4.6
) and (
2.4.7
) for the homogeneities of the elements of the set
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
and from the definition of the homogeneity which implies
|\delta_{\bf n}|=|{\bf n}|
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
we infer that
\{\beta\in\mathcal{M}:|\beta|_{\prec}<N\}\supset\{\beta\in\mathcal{M}:|\beta|\leq 2\}
if
N\geq 17
.
This completes the proof.

∎

4.6.
The induction step for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}

In this section we establish the induction step for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\not\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
using the so–called algebraic argument following the terminology of
[
5
,
35
]
.

Proposition 4.6.1
.

Suppose that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
\beta\neq 0
and for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
one has

(4.6.1)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\mu}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\mu|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}>0,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
x
and
y
and
\rho
.
Then (
4.6.1
) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
1\leq p<\infty
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
.

Proof.

For
\gamma=0
the statement is void by the first identity in (
2.6.7
).

If
\gamma\neq 0
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
then
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
l>0
, and we apply
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
to (
2.6.16
) with
F=\Gamma_{xy}
and use the Hölder inequality together with (
2.6.9
) to get

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt\left\|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p_{1}}}\ldots\left\|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{r}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p_{r}}},

where
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\ldots+\frac{1}{p_{r}}=\frac{1}{p}
.

Since
l>0
, by Lemma
3.3.7
(i)
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
i=1,\ldots,r
in the right hand side of the previous formula, so we can use the induction assumption. This yields

(4.6.2)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt|x-y|^{\sum_{i=1}^{r}(|\beta_{i}|-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|)}.

By the linearity of the function
|\cdot|-\alpha
we obtain

(4.6.3)

\sum_{i=1}^{r}(|\beta_{i}|-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|)=|\beta|-|\gamma|.

Substituting this into (
4.6.2
) yields the result.

∎

Proposition 4.6.2
.

Suppose the conditions of Proposition
4.6.1
are satisfied, that
|\beta|<2
, and that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(4.6.4)

\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\mu}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\mu|-|\gamma|},~0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\rho
,
R
and
\upsilon
.

Then (
4.6.4
) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
. The constant in the inequality is independent of
\rho
,
R
and
\upsilon
.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and assume that
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

For
\gamma=0
the statement is obvious by the second identity in (
3.3.1
).

If
\gamma\neq 0
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
then
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
l>0
, and we apply
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
to (
3.3.6
) with
F={\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
and use the Hölder inequality together with (
2.6.9
) and (
3.3.19
) to get

(4.6.5)

\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\leq\hskip 199.16928pt

\leq\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k-1},\\
\beta_{k+1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp},\\
\beta_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}\right\|_{q_{1}\infty B_{R}x}\ldots\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta_{k-1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k-1}}}\right\|_{q_{k-1}\infty B_{R}x}\times

\times\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta_{k}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k}}}\right\|_{q_{k}2B_{R}x}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta_{k+1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k+1}}}\right\|_{q_{k+1}\infty B_{R}x}\ldots\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta_{r}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{r}}}\right\|_{q_{r}\infty B_{R}x},

where
\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\ldots+\frac{1}{q_{r}}=\frac{1}{q}
and in the
k
-th term
1\leq q<q_{k}<p^{*}<2
, where
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
satisfies
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

Since
l>0
, by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii)
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
i=1,\ldots,r
in the right hand side of the previous formula, so we can use (
4.6.2
) and (
4.6.4
). Since
|\beta_{i}|\geq|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|
,
i\neq k
in the
k
-th term (see (
2.6.2
)) we obtain by (
4.6.2
) that
\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta_{i}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}\right\|_{q_{i}\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim R^{|\beta_{i}|-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|}
, as well as
\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta_{k}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k}}}\right\|_{q_{k}2B_{R}x}\lesssim R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta_{k}|-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{k}}|}
by (
4.6.4
). Using these estimates in (
4.6.5
) together with (
4.6.3
) yields the result.

∎

For any
\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
we denote
|\gamma|_{p}:=\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}|{\bf n}|\gamma({\bf n})
.

Proposition 4.6.3
.

Suppose the conditions of Proposition
4.6.1
are satisfied, that
|\beta|<2
, and that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
S_{x+yx}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(4.6.6)

\left\|(S_{x+yx})^{\gamma}_{\mu}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|y|^{\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha}(|y|+R)^{|\mu|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}&{\rm if}~\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha>0\\
(|y|+R)^{|\mu|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\rho
,
R
,
\upsilon
and
y
.

Then (
4.6.6
) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
S_{x+yx}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
, and the constant in the inequality is independent of
y
,
\rho
,
R
,
\upsilon
.

Moreover, the left hand side of inequality (
4.6.6
) for
\mu=\beta
vanishes if
|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha<0
or
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})=0
.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and assume that
S_{x+yx}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

If
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})=0
then
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}
, and in this case
(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{l\delta_{\bf g}}=0
by the definition of
S_{x+yx}={\rm d}\Gamma_{x+y}-\Gamma_{x+yx}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
, by (
2.6.7
) and by (
3.3.1
) as these properties imply

(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{l\delta_{\bf g}}{\bf z}^{\beta}=(S_{x+yx}^{*}{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{l})_{\beta}=(({\rm d}\Gamma_{x+y}^{*}-{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}^{*}\Gamma_{x+yx}^{*}){\bf z}_{\bf g}^{l})_{\beta}=-({\rm d}\Gamma_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\bf g}^{l})_{\beta}=0.

In particular, (
4.6.6
) holds for
\gamma=0
.

If
\gamma\neq 0
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
then
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
l>0
, and we apply
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
to (
3.3.6
) with
F=S_{x+yx}
and use the Hölder inequality together with (
2.6.9
) and (
3.3.23
) to get

(4.6.7)

\left\|(S_{x+yx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\leq\hskip 199.16928pt

\leq\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k-1},\\
\beta_{k+1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp},\\
\beta_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt\left\|(\Gamma_{x+y0})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}\right\|_{q_{1}\infty B_{R}x}\ldots\left\|(\Gamma_{x+y0})_{\beta_{k-1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k-1}}}\right\|_{q_{k-1}\infty B_{R}x}\times

\times\left\|(S_{x+yx})_{\beta_{k}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k}}}\right\|_{q_{k}2B_{R}x}\left\|(\Gamma_{x+y0})_{\beta_{k+1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k+1}}}\right\|_{q_{k+1}\infty B_{R}x}\ldots\left\|(\Gamma_{x+y0})_{\beta_{r}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{r}}}\right\|_{q_{r}\infty B_{R}x}.

Here
\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\ldots+\frac{1}{q_{r}}=\frac{1}{q}
, in the
k
-th term
1\leq q<q_{k}<p^{*}<2
, and
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
satisfies
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

Since
l>0
, by Lemma
3.3.7
(iii)
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
i=1,\ldots,r
in the right hand side of the previous formula, so we can use (
4.6.2
) and (
4.6.6
). Since
|\beta_{i}|\geq|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|
,
i\neq k
in the
k
-th term (see (
2.6.2
)) we obtain by (
4.6.2
) that
\left\|(\Gamma_{x+y0})_{\beta_{i}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}}\right\|_{q_{i}\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim(|y|+R)^{|\beta_{i}|-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|}
,
|y|,R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
. Using these estimates in (
4.6.7
) together with estimate (
4.6.6
) for
\left\|(S_{x+yx})_{\beta_{k}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{k}}}\right\|_{q_{k}2B_{R}x}
and (
4.6.3
) we obtain (
4.6.6
) for
\mu=\beta
.

Note that for the
k
-th term in (
4.6.7
) we have by (
4.6.3
)

\sum_{i=1}^{r}|\beta_{i}|=|\beta|-|\gamma|+\sum_{i=1}^{r}|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|=|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}.

Since by (
2.6.2
) in the
k
-th term in sum (
4.6.7
) one has
|\beta_{i}|\geq|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|=|{\bf m}_{i}|\geq 0
,
i\neq k
, and
|\beta_{k}|\geq\alpha
we deduce

\alpha\leq\sum_{i=1}^{r}|\beta_{i}|=|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p},

i.e. the left hand side of inequality (
4.6.6
) for
\mu=\beta
vanishes if

|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha<0
.
This completes the proof.

∎

Since the assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
are stronger than those in Propositions
4.6.1
,
4.6.2
and
4.6.3
we immediately obtain the following corollary from them.

Corollary 4.6.4
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimates (
4.6.1
), (
4.6.4
) and (
4.6.6
) hold with
\mu
replaced by
\beta
and with the same conditions as in Propositions
4.6.1
,
4.6.2
, and
4.6.3
, respectively, and estimates (
4.5.14
), (
4.5.17
) and (
4.5.18
) hold with
\mu
replaced by
\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, and with the same other conditions as in the assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
, where
\mu
is replaced by
\beta
, and
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
.

4.7.
Induction step estimates for
\delta\Pi_{\beta}
and
\delta\Pi_{\beta}^{-}
,
|\beta|<2

This section contains the most difficult part in the proof of the induction step. As in the base case to obtain estimates for
\Pi_{\beta}
and
\Pi_{\beta}^{-}
,
|\beta|<2
using the spectral gap inequality we have to derive some estimates for their Malliavin derivatives which is done in this section. For this purpose we use the reconstruction theorem and Schauder estimates for germs several times.

In this section it is always assumed that
|\beta|<2
.
We start by establishing a coherence condition for a germ.

Lemma 4.7.1
.

Assume that (
4.6.6
) holds for
\mu=\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, where
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
S_{x+yx}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
. Suppose also that for some
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\varphi(x)dx\neq 0
and all
1\leq p<\infty
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
one has

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}\varphi}^{|\gamma|-2}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}}{\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
and
R
.

Then for all
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
the germ
F_{x}(y):=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
, where
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
, satisfies for any
0<R\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}:=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}

\left\|F\right\|_{q2R\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}\varphi}^{\alpha-1~2\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-1~|\beta|-1-2\alpha}=

(4.7.1)

=\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:|h|\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\frac{\left\|(F_{x+h}-F_{x})(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}}{\lambda^{\alpha-1}(\lambda+|h|)^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R+|h|)^{|\beta|-1-2\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

and the constant in the inequality is independent of
\rho
,
R
and
\upsilon
.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and assume that
S_{x+yx}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

First observe that by (
2.4.10
), (
2.6.6
), (
3.3.5
), and (
3.3.24
)

(F_{x+h}-F_{x})(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\circ(S_{x+hx})_{\beta}^{\gamma},

and hence by Hölder’s inequality

(4.7.2)

\left\|(F_{x+h}-F_{x})(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{q2Rx}\leq

\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x},

where
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{p}
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime}<p^{*}<2
,
1\leq p<\infty
.

By the assumption

(4.7.3)

\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim\lambda^{|\gamma|-2},~0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constant in the inequality is independent on
\rho
and
R
, and by translational invariance in law for
\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}
it is also independent of
x+h
.

Now suppose that
0<R\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
. Then by the conditions of the lemma we can use (
4.6.6
) in (
4.7.2
) for
h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|h|\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
\mu=\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime}<p^{*}<2
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
.

We now estimate each term in the sum in the right hand side of (
4.7.2
).

Observe that in (
4.6.6
) we can assume that
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})>0
, for otherwise its left hand side is zero, and
\gamma({\bf g})>0
for
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. Thus in this case

(4.7.4)

|\gamma|-|\gamma|_{p}=(\alpha+1)\gamma({\bf g})-\alpha\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})+\alpha\geq\alpha+1.

Therefore, since we always have
|\gamma|_{p}\geq 0
,

|\gamma|\geq|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha+1\geq\alpha+1,

and hence

(4.7.5)

|\gamma|-2\geq\alpha-1.

If

(4.7.6)

\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha>0,

using inequality (
4.7.5
), (
4.7.3
) and (
4.6.6
) in the case when
\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha>0
and for
h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|h|\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
\mu=\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime}<p^{*}<2
we obtain for
0<R\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}

\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}|h|^{\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha}(|h|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}=

(4.7.7)

=\lambda^{\alpha-1}\lambda^{|\gamma|-2-\alpha+1}|h|^{\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha}(|h|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}.

Now recall that for any
v,w,a,b\geq 0

(4.7.8)

v^{a}w^{b}\leq(v+w)^{a+b}.

By (
4.7.6
) and (
4.7.5
) we can apply (
4.7.8
) to the second and to the third factor in (
4.7.7
) and obtain

\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-1}(\lambda+|h|)^{\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha+|\gamma|-2-\alpha+1}(|h|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}=

(4.7.9)

=\lambda^{\alpha-1}(\lambda+|h|)^{\frac{d}{2}+\alpha}(\lambda+|h|)^{|\gamma|-|\gamma|_{p}-(\alpha+1)}(|h|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}.

Note that when using (
4.6.6
) we can only consider the case
|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha\geq 0
as otherwise by Proposition
4.6.3
the left hand side of (
4.6.6
) with
\mu=\beta
is zero. Recalling also (
4.7.4
) we can apply (
4.7.8
) to the last two factors in the right hand side of (
4.7.9
). This yields

\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim

(4.7.10)

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-1}(\lambda+|h|)^{\frac{d}{2}+\alpha}(\lambda+|h|+R)^{|\beta|-2\alpha-1}.

If

(4.7.11)

\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha\leq 0

then by (
4.7.5
), (
4.7.11
), and by the obvious inequality
|\gamma|_{p}\geq 0
we have

(4.7.12)

|\gamma|-2=|\gamma|-|\gamma|_{p}+|\gamma|_{p}-2\geq(\alpha+1)-2+|\gamma|_{p}\geq 2\alpha-1+\frac{d}{2}.

Using this inequality, (
4.7.3
) and (
4.6.6
) in the case
\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha\leq 0
for
h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|h|\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
\mu=\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime}<p^{*}<2
we obtain for
0<R\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}

\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}(|h|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}=

(4.7.13)

=\lambda^{2\alpha-1+\frac{d}{2}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-1-2\alpha-\frac{d}{2}}(|h|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}.

Recalling that
|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}>0
in (
4.7.2
) and using (
4.7.12
) we can apply (
4.7.8
) to the second and to the third factor in (
4.7.13
) and obtain

\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim

(4.7.14)

\lesssim\lambda^{2\alpha-1+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+|h|+R)^{|\beta|-2\alpha-1}.

Finally note that
\alpha+\frac{d}{2}>0
, and hence we can rewrite (
4.7.14
) as follows

\left\|(S_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-1}(\lambda+|h|)^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+|h|+R)^{|\beta|-2\alpha-1}.

Substituting this inequality and (
4.7.10
) into (
4.7.2
) we obtain (
4.7.1
).
This completes the proof

∎

Now using the coherence condition established in the previous lemma we can obtain the following result.

Lemma 4.7.2
.

Assume that the conditions of Lemma
4.7.1
are satisfied. Then for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>1-\alpha
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
the germ
F_{x}(y):=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(y)-\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
, where
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
, satisfies

(4.7.15)

\left\|F\right\|_{pq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}Rr}^{2\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-1~|\beta|-1-2\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{q}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{2\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-1}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-1-2\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

and the constant in the inequality is independent of
\rho
,
\upsilon
and
R
.

Proof.

Note that by examining the list of
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\beta|<2
from Lemma
4.1.2
one can deduce that the condition
\beta\neq 0
ensures that
|\beta|-1-2\alpha\geq 0
. Thus
by Lemma
4.7.1
and by Proposition
3.3.4
the germ
F_{x}
satisfies the conditions of part (i) of Corollary
3.2.6
, and the statement of this lemma immediately follows from Corollary
3.2.6
(i).

∎

Finally, using Lemma
4.7.2
we can obtain the desired estimate for the Malliavin derivative of
\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}
.

Proposition 4.7.3
.

Assume that (
4.7.15
) holds for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
, for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>1-\alpha
, for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
, and with
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
,
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
, and that (
4.6.4
) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
, and with
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
, and for arbitrary
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. Suppose also that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
one has

(4.7.16)

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}r}^{|\gamma|-2}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
.

Then for
r^{\prime}=r+3
, any
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|+\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(4.7.17)

\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}Rr}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}}\lesssim 1,

and

(4.7.18)

\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r^{\prime}}}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{y})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2-\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+|y|)^{|\beta|-\alpha+\frac{d}{2}},

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
, and the constants in the inequalities do not depend on
\upsilon
,
\rho
,
R
,
y
and
\lambda
.
In particular,

(4.7.19)

\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}\right\|_{q\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{|\beta|-2}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r^{\prime}}}\frac{\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}}{\lambda^{|\beta|-2}}\lesssim 1.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and assume that
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

First note that by (
2.4.10
), (
3.3.5
), and (
3.3.20
) the germ
F_{x}
defined in Lemma
4.7.2
can be written in the form

F_{x}(y)=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma},

and hence, taking into account that by translational invariance in law
\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
does not depend on
x
, by the triangle and by the Hölder inequalities we obtain

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\leq\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R+|y|},x)}\leq

\leq\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R+|y|},x)}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{q_{1}2B_{R+|y|}x}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q_{2}}},

where
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
,
1\leq q<q_{1}<p^{*}<2
,
1\leq q_{2}<\infty
.

Now, for
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
|y|+R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
, by (
4.7.15
), (
4.6.4
) and (
4.7.16
) we can estimate the right hand side of the last inequality as follows

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\lesssim\lambda^{2\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-1}(\lambda+R+|y|)^{|\beta|-2\alpha-1}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}(R+|y|)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}=

=\lambda^{\alpha-2}\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}+1}(\lambda+R+|y|)^{|\beta|-2\alpha-1}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{\alpha-2}\lambda^{|\gamma|-\alpha}(R+|y|)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+R+|y|)^{|\beta|-\alpha+\frac{d}{2}},

where the last inequality is obtained by applying (
4.7.8
) to the second and the third factor in each term of the sum. This is possible since by the definition of
\alpha
one has
\alpha+\frac{d}{2}+1>0
, by the definition of the homogeneity
|\beta|-2\alpha-1\geq 0
for
\beta\neq 0
,
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
as
\beta({\bf g})\neq 0
, and
|\gamma|-\alpha\geq 0
,
|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}>0
by the choice of the limits in the sum over
\gamma
.

For
y=0
we obtain (
4.7.17
) from the previous inequality, and for
R=\lambda
we get from it for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}

\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q2B_{\lambda}x}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|y|)^{|\beta|-\alpha+\frac{d}{2}},~0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime},~|y|+\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}.

Note that for
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r^{\prime}}
and
|{\bf n}|\leq 3
we have
\partial^{\bf n}\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
, and we obtain from the previous estimate

\left\|\partial^{\bf n}\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q2B_{\lambda}x}=\lambda^{-|{\bf n}|}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}((\partial^{\bf n}\varphi)^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q2B_{\lambda}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2-|{\bf n}|}(\lambda+|y|)^{|\beta|-\alpha+\frac{d}{2}},~0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime},~|y|+\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}.

Now (
4.7.18
) follows from the last estimate and Proposition
3.1.1
with
k=3
,
R=\lambda
applied to
f(y)=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{y})
, and (
4.7.19
) is (
4.7.18
) with
y=0
. This completes the proof.

∎

From Proposition
4.7.3
we also immediately obtain estimates for the Malliavin derivative of
\Pi_{0\beta}
using Schauder estimates.

Proposition 4.7.4
.

Assume that the conditions of Proposition
4.7.3
hold and that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}\geq 2
.
Then for any
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
r^{\prime}\geq r+3
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(4.7.20)

\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}\right\|_{q\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}r^{\prime}}^{|\beta|}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r^{\prime}}}\frac{\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}}{\lambda^{|\beta|}}\lesssim 1,~\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}\geq 1,

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
, and the constant in the inequality is independent of
\upsilon
and
\rho
.

Proof.

Since
\Pi_{0\beta}
depends on
\xi
polynomially, one obviously has by (
2.4.9
) using the notation as in (
3.2.9
)

\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(y)=(\mathcal{K}\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-})(y)-\sum_{{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:|{{\bf n}}|<|\beta|}\partial^{{\bf n}}(\mathcal{K}\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-})(0)\frac{y^{{\bf n}}}{{{\bf n}}!}=(\mathcal{K}\delta\Pi_{0\beta})_{0}^{|\beta|}(y).

Observe also that
r^{\prime}\geq r+3>1-\alpha+3=\frac{9}{2}+{\varepsilon}>||\beta||
as
||\beta||<2
, where
||\beta||
is the modulus of
|\beta|
.
Hence, the statement follows from (
4.7.19
), and from (
3.2.5
) (resp. (
3.2.9
)) with
x=0
,
F=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}
if
|\beta|<0
(resp. if
|\beta|>0
).

∎

Since the assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
are stronger than those in Proposition
4.6.3
, which implies (
4.6.6
), and, as for
|\beta|<2
one has
2-\alpha=2\frac{1}{2}+{\varepsilon}>|\beta|
, the other conditions in Lemmas
4.7.1
,
4.7.2
and in Propositions
4.7.3
,
4.7.4
are also weaker than the assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
, we immediately obtain the following corollary from them.

Corollary 4.7.5
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimates (
4.7.17
), (
4.7.18
), (
4.7.19
) hold with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.7.3
, and estimate (
4.7.20
) holds with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.7.4
.

The remaining results in this section will be used in Section
4.9
to establish the induction step estimates for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
.

Lemma 4.7.6
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
(
4.6.4
) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

Then the following statements are true.

(i) If for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has for some
r\in\mathbb{N}

(4.7.21)

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}r}^{|\gamma|-2}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
, then for any
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
the germ

F_{x}(y):=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

satisfies

(4.7.22)

\left\|F\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}Rr}^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}~|\beta|-\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
R
,
\rho
and
\upsilon
.

(ii) If for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
one has for some
r\in\mathbb{N}

(4.7.23)

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}r}^{|\gamma|}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\gamma|}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
, then for any
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
the germ

F_{x}(y):=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

satisfies

(4.7.24)

\left\|F\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}Rr}^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}~|\beta|-\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
R
,
\rho
and
\upsilon
.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and assume that
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

(i) By (
2.4.10
), (
3.3.20
) and (
3.3.5
) we can rewrite the definition of
F_{x}
as follows

F_{x}(y)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma},

Note that by the translational invariance in law
\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
does not depend on
x
. Hence by (
4.6.4
), (
4.7.21
), by the triangle and by the Hölder inequalities we have for
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{q}
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime}<p^{*}<2
,
1\leq p<\infty

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}+\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-2}\lambda^{|\gamma|-\frac{d}{2}-\alpha}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}\leq

(4.7.25)

\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}+\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-2}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-2}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where to obtain (
4.7.25
) from the preceding expression we applied (
4.7.8
) to the last two factors in each term of the sum over
\gamma
. Estimate (
4.7.25
) justifies (
4.7.22
).

(ii)
By (
2.4.11
)
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}
in the sum in the definition of
F_{x}
.
By (
3.3.8
)
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}=0
for
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf n}|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}
. Thus for such
{\bf n}

(4.7.26)

({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=({\rm d}\Gamma_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})_{\beta}=0,~|{\bf n}|\geq 2-{\varepsilon},

and using also (
3.3.20
) and (
3.3.5
) we can rewrite the definition of
F_{x}
as follows

F_{x}(y)=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma},

Note that by the translational invariance in law
\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
does not depend on
x
. Hence by (
4.6.4
), (
4.7.23
), by the triangle and by the Hölder inequalities we have for
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{q}
,
1\leq q^{\prime}<q<p^{*}<2
,
1\leq p<\infty

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
2-{\varepsilon}\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}+\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-\frac{d}{2}-\alpha}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}\leq

(4.7.27)

\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}+\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\end{array}}\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where to obtain (
4.7.27
) from the preceding expression we applied (
4.7.8
) to the last two factors in each term of the sum over
\gamma
. Estimate (
4.7.27
) justifies (
4.7.24
). This completes the proof.

∎

The next lemma is a preparation for the proof of Proposition
4.7.8
.

Lemma 4.7.7
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
estimate (
4.6.4
) (resp. (
4.6.6
)) holds for
\mu=\beta
, and for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
(resp. for all
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
),
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
(resp.
S_{x+yx}
) defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
. Suppose also that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>4-\alpha
(
4.7.21
) holds, and that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}\geq 1
.

Then for any
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
the germ

F_{x}(y):=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

satisfies

(4.7.28)

\left\|F\right\|_{q2\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}Rr}^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}~|\beta|-\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
, and the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\upsilon
,
\rho
and
R
.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and assume that
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
and
S_{x+yx}
are defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

Consider the germ

G_{x}(y):=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=

=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Checking the list of multi–indices
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|<2
from Lemma
4.1.2
shows that the condition
|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}
for multi–indices
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is equivalent to
|\gamma|<2
. From this observation and (
2.4.9
) we deduce that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
the function

F_{x}(y)
is obtained from
(\mathcal{K}G)_{x}(y)
by subtracting a polynomial in
y
of parabolic degree less than
2
(or equivalently less than
2-{\varepsilon}
). By (
3.3.9
) this polynomial must be the Taylor polynomial of
(\mathcal{K}G)_{x}(y)
at
x
of parabolic degree
1
. Thus
F=(\mathcal{K}G)^{2-{\varepsilon}}
, and we can apply (
3.2.10
) with
b=-{\varepsilon}
,
r>4-\alpha
to get

(4.7.29)

\left\|F\right\|_{q2\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}B_{R}r}^{2-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim\left\|G\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}B_{R}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}.

Now we estimate
\left\|G\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}B_{R}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}
for
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
. We split the sum in the definition of
G
as follows

G_{x}(y):=\large\left(\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(y)-\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\large\right)+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

and apply Lemma
4.7.2
to the expression in the large brackets and Lemma
4.7.6
to the remaining sum over
\gamma
. This yields for
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
with the help of the triangle inequality

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|G_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\lesssim\lambda^{2\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-1}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-1-2\alpha}+\lambda^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\large\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+R}\large\right)^{\alpha+1}+\lambda^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}.

For
\lambda\leq R
we obtain from the last estimate

(4.7.30)

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|G_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2+\frac{d}{2}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}=\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}.

If
\lambda\geq R
we write by (
3.3.20
)

G_{x}(y)=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma},

and hence obtain by the triangle and the Hölder inequalities using translational invariance in law of
\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|G_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\leq\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{q^{\prime}2B_{R}x},

where
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{q}
,
1\leq q^{\prime}<q<p^{*}<2
,
1\leq p<\infty
.

Using (
4.7.18
) in the first term in the right hand side and (
4.6.4
), (
4.7.21
) in the sum over
\gamma
we obtain for
R\leq\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
with the help
\frac{R}{\lambda}\leq 1
,
\frac{d}{2}-|\beta|+\alpha=-|\beta|+2-{\varepsilon}>0
and
-|\gamma|+2-{\varepsilon}>0

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|G_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2-\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}R^{\frac{d}{2}}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}\leq

\leq\lambda^{|\beta|-2}R^{\frac{d}{2}}+\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\large\left(\frac{R}{\lambda}\large\right)^{-|\gamma|+2-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}\large\left(\frac{R}{\lambda}\large\right)^{\frac{d}{2}-|\beta|+\alpha}+\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}\lesssim\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}.

The last inequality and (
4.7.30
) imply that for
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
one has

\left\|G\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}B_{R}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim R^{|\beta|-\alpha}.

Now by (
4.7.29
) for
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
and
0<\lambda\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}

\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}\lesssim\lambda^{2-{\varepsilon}}R^{|\beta|-\alpha}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}

as
|\beta|-\alpha\geq 0
. This proves (
4.7.28
).

∎

From the previous lemma and from Lemma
4.7.6
(ii) we immediately obtain the following statement.

Proposition 4.7.8
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
estimates (
4.6.4
) (resp. (
4.6.6
)) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
(resp.
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
),
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
(resp.
S_{x+yx}
) defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
. Suppose also that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>4-\alpha
(
4.7.21
) and (
4.7.23
) hold, and that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}\geq 1
.

Then for any
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
the germ

F_{x}(y):=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(y)-\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

satisfies

(4.7.31)

\left\|F\right\|_{q2\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}Rr}^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}~|\beta|-\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
, and the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\upsilon
,
\rho
and
R
.

Proof.

We can write

F_{x}(y)=\large\left(\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\large\right)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in{\bf Mult}:\\
|\gamma|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

and then estimate
\left\|F\right\|_{q2\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}Rr}^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}~|\beta|-\alpha}
using the triangle inequality in the right hand side, the previous lemma for the first term in the large brackets and Lemma
4.7.6
(ii) for the last sum over
\gamma\in{\bf Mult}
,
|\gamma|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}
. This yields the desired estimate.

∎

4.8.
The induction step for
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}

In this section we establish the induction step estimates for
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
.

We firstly consider the case when
|\beta|<2
when an estimate for
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
is obtained using the spectral gap inequality. We start with an estimate for the expectation of
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
which appears in the right hand side of the spectral gap inequality. To estimate the expectation we shall have to impose a BPHZ condition in a special way (compare the following proposition and its proof with Proposition 5.2 in
[
25
]
and with its proof).

Proposition 4.8.1
.

Let
\omega\in\mathcal{D}
be an even function supported in
B_{\frac{1}{6}}
and satisfying the first condition in (
4.5.12
). Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\beta|<2
,
\beta\neq 0
the recentered map
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
satisfies the generalized BPHZ condition with respect to the function
\psi^{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}>0
, where
\psi:=\omega^{2}\ast\omega
, and
\omega^{2}
is
\omega
rescaled by
2
. Suppose also that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
one has

(4.8.1)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
and
\rho
, and that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
and
|\gamma|<|\beta|
one has

(4.8.2)

\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\omega^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
.
Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}

(4.8.3)

|\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{x}))|\lesssim{{\varepsilon}_{n}}^{|\beta|-2},

where
{\varepsilon}_{n}=\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}2^{-n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and the constant in the inequality does not depend on
n
,
x
and
\rho
.

Proof.

First note that from the definition of
\psi
it follows that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\psi(x)dx=1
, and

(4.8.4)

{\rm supp}(\psi)\subset B_{\frac{1}{2}}\subset B_{1}.

We also have the following identity

\psi^{\frac{1}{2}}-\psi=\omega\ast(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2}),

and
{\rm supp}(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})\subset B_{\frac{2}{3}}
.

Using the previous identity we can write for
n\in\mathbb{N}

\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n+1}}-\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}=\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\ast(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{n}},

and hence

(4.8.5)

\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}=\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{0}}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{k+1}}-\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{k}})=\psi^{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}\ast(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}.

By translational invariance in law it suffices to establish (
4.8.3
) for
x=0
. Using (
4.8.5
) and the BPHZ condition
\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}})=0
we obtain

\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}))=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}_{x}))(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)dx=

=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}_{x})\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma})(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)dx,

where at the last step we used (
2.6.6
).

Since by the translational invariance in law
\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}_{x}))
does not depend on
x
and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)dx=0

we can rewrite the last identity as follows

\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}))=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}_{x})\circ(\Gamma_{x0}-{\rm Id})_{\beta}^{\gamma})(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)dx.

Now by (
2.6.2
) and (
2.6.4
) we can further reduce this identity to

\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}))=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}_{x})\circ(\Gamma_{x0}-{\rm Id})_{\beta}^{\gamma})(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)dx.

Since
(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
is supported in
B_{\frac{2\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{3}}
the domain of integration is reduced to
B_{\frac{2\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{3}}
, and using the Hölder inequality in probability with Hölder conjugate exponents
p
,
p^{\prime}
,
1<p,p^{\prime}<\infty
we can estimate the terms in the sum with the help of the assumptions of the proposition as follows

|\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}))|\leq

\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\int_{B_{\frac{2\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{3}}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\omega^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{\prime}}}|(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)|dx\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\int_{B_{\frac{2\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{3}}}{\varepsilon}_{k}^{|\gamma|-2}|x|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}|(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}(x)|dx=

=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}{\varepsilon}_{k}^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|-2}|y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}(\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega^{2})(y)dy\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}{\varepsilon}_{k}^{|\beta|-2}=\frac{{\varepsilon}_{n}^{|\beta|-2}-1}{{\varepsilon}_{1}^{|\beta|-2}-1}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n}^{|\beta|-2},

where we also used the substitution of variables
x=R_{{\varepsilon}_{k}}y
in the integral in the
k
-th term in the sum over
k
. This completes the proof.

∎

Now we can apply the spectral gap inequality to estimate
\mathbb{L}_{p}
–norms of
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
.

Lemma 4.8.2
.

Assume that the conditions of Propositions
4.7.3
and
4.8.1
are satisfied. Then for any
1\leq p<\infty
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}

(4.8.6)

\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{{\varepsilon}_{n}}^{|\beta|-2},

where
{\varepsilon}_{n}=\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}2^{-n}=\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}2^{-(n+2)}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and the constant in the inequality does not depend on
n
,
x
and
\rho
.

Proof.

By translational invariance in law it suffices to consider the case
x=0
, and by the Hölder inequality in probability one can restrict to the case when
p>2
.

We shall use spectral gap inequality (
3.1.7
) for
f=\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{0})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq|\mathbb{E}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{0}))|+\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2}):\\
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1\end{array}}\left\|\delta_{\upsilon}\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}},

where
1\leq q<p^{*}<2<p
, and
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{*}}=1
.

Now we can apply (
4.8.3
) in the first term and (
4.7.19
) in the second one to obtain from the previous estimate for
n\in\mathbb{N}

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{{\varepsilon}_{n}}^{|\beta|-2}

which completes the proof.

∎

Next, we shall derive more general estimates for
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
depending on the base point
x
and for any value of the rescaling parameter
\lambda
in the domain
0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
. We start with the following useful lemma.

Lemma 4.8.3
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\lambda>0
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
and for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
and for
\gamma=\beta
one has

(4.8.7)

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda}))\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}.

Suppose also that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
1\leq p<\infty
one has

(4.8.8)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}>0,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
and
\rho
.
Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}

(4.8.9)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\lambda
,
x
and
\rho
.

Proof.

By (
2.6.6
), (
2.6.2
), (
2.6.3
) and (
2.6.4
) we can write

\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\circ(\Gamma_{x0})^{\gamma}_{\beta}+\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda}).

Thus by the Hölder inequality in probability we get with the help of (
4.8.7
) and (
4.8.8
)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}+\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|\leq|\beta|\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}\leq|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}|x|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where at the last step we used (
4.7.8
). This completes the proof.

∎

Corollary 4.8.4
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{{\varepsilon}_{n}}^{|\gamma|-2},

and

\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{{\varepsilon}_{n}}^{|\beta|-2},

where
{\varepsilon}_{n}=\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}2^{-n}=\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}2^{-(n+2)}
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}>0
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and the constants in the inequalities do not depend on
n
and
\rho
.

Suppose also that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
1\leq p<\infty
one has

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
and
\rho
.

Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}

(4.8.10)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha-2}({\varepsilon}_{n}+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x
,
n
and
\rho
.

Proof.

The proof is obtained by applying the previous lemma to
\varphi=\psi
,
\lambda={{\varepsilon}_{n}}=\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}2^{-n}=\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}2^{-(n+2)}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
.

∎

Now the desired induction step estimate (
4.5.15
), where
\mu=\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
, in full generality in the case when
|\beta|<2
follows from Lemma
4.8.2
, the previous corollary and from the next proposition.

Proposition 4.8.5
.

Let
{\varepsilon}_{n}=\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}2^{-n}=\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}2^{-(n+2)}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, for some
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}>0
,
\psi=\omega^{2}\ast\omega
, and
\omega\in\mathcal{D}
an even function supported in
B_{\frac{1}{6}}
and satisfying (
4.5.12
) with some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>2-\alpha
.

Assume that some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}

(4.8.11)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha-2}({\varepsilon}_{n}+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x
,
n
and
\rho
.

Suppose also that for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
and for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
and
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
one has

(4.8.12)

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda}))\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{|\gamma|-2},

and that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
1\leq p<\infty
one has

(4.8.13)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constants in the inequalities do not depend on
x,y
,
\lambda
,
\rho
and
\varphi
.

Then for any
1\leq p<\infty
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
one has

(4.8.14)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
,
x
and
\rho
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.8.1
we introduce

(4.8.15)

\check{\psi}:=\psi^{\frac{1}{2}}-\psi^{2}.

By (
4.8.4
), by the definition of
\psi=\omega^{2}\ast\omega
and by the second property in (
4.5.12
) we infer

(4.8.16)

{\rm supp}(\check{\psi})\subset B_{1},~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}x^{\bf m}\check{\psi}(x)dx=0,~0\leq|{\bf m}|\leq r-1,~{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}.

We shall also need the function

\sigma:=\psi^{2}\ast\psi.

The first property in (
4.5.12
) and the definition of
\psi=\omega^{2}\ast\omega
imply

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sigma(x)dx=1,

and hence
\sigma
can be used as a mollifier, so that for
{\varepsilon}_{k}:=\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}2^{-k}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
we have

(4.8.17)

\varphi=\lim_{k\to\infty}\varphi\ast\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}

Using (
4.8.15
) we can write for
n\in\mathbb{N}

\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{n+1}}-\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}=\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\ast(\psi^{\frac{1}{2}}-\psi^{2})^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}=\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{n}},

and hence

(4.8.18)

\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}=\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}+\sum_{m=n}^{k-1}(\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{m+1}}-\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{m}})=\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}+\sum_{m=n}^{k-1}\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}.

Thus by (
4.8.17
) and by the previous presentation for
\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{k}}
we can write for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}

(4.8.19)

\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{0})=(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}\ast\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}})(0)=\lim_{k\to\infty}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}\ast\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\sigma^{{\varepsilon}_{k}})(0)=

=(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}\ast\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}})(0)+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}(\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}\ast\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}})(0)=

=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{z})(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}})(-z)dz+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}_{z})(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}})(-z)dz.

By (
4.8.4
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
one has
{\rm supp}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}})\subset B_{{\varepsilon}_{n-2}}\subset B_{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}
and by the first property in (
4.8.16
)
{\rm supp}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}})\subset B_{{\varepsilon}_{n-2}}\subset B_{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}
for
m\geq n
.
Using these observations, the Minkowski and the Hölder inequalities we have for
1\leq p<\infty

(4.8.20)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\int_{B_{{\varepsilon}_{n-2}}}\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{z})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}dz\left\|\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}+

+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\int_{B_{{\varepsilon}_{n-2}}}\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}_{z})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}dz\left\|\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}.

Now, since
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}\leq 1
, we have

(4.8.21)

\left\|\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{nd}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\left\|\psi\right\|_{L_{1}}\lesssim 2^{nd},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
and
\omega
.

By Lemma 9.2 in
[
13
]
we also obtain

(4.8.22)

\left\|\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{n(r+d)-mr}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}\left\|\check{\psi}\right\|_{L_{1}}\lesssim 2^{n(r+d)-mr},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
,
r
and
\omega
.

Using the last two estimates and (
4.8.10
) in (
4.8.20
) we obtain

(4.8.23)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq 2^{nd}\int_{B_{{\varepsilon}_{n-2}}}{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha-2}({\varepsilon}_{n}+|z|)^{|\beta|-\alpha}dz+

+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}2^{n(r+d)-mr}\int_{B_{{\varepsilon}_{n-2}}}{\varepsilon}_{m}^{\alpha-2}({\varepsilon}_{m}+|z|)^{|\beta|-\alpha}dz\lesssim

\lesssim 2^{nd-nd}{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha-2+|\beta|-\alpha}+{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha-2+|\beta|-\alpha}\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}2^{n(r+d)-mr-m(\alpha-2)+n(\alpha-2)-nd}\lesssim

\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{|\beta|-2}\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}2^{-(m-n)(r+\alpha-2)}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{|\beta|-2},

where the series in the last expression converges due to the condition
r+\alpha-2>0
and
the constant in the inequality does not depend on
n
,
\varphi
and
\rho
.

Now for any
0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be the largest possible such that
{\varepsilon}_{n-1}\geq\lambda
, so that
{\varepsilon}_{n}\leq\lambda
, and for
\lambda^{\prime}:={\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{-1}\lambda
we have
\frac{1}{2}\leq\lambda^{\prime}\leq 1
.

Also if
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
then
\varphi^{\lambda^{\prime}}\in C\mathcal{B}^{r}
, where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\lambda^{\prime}
and
\varphi
.

Since by the definition
\varphi^{\lambda}=(\varphi^{\lambda^{\prime}})^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}
we obtain from the last observation and from (
4.8.23
) that

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{0}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{|\beta|-2}\leq\lambda^{|\beta|-2},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
and
\rho
.

Finally, the last estimate, (
4.8.12
), (
4.8.13
) and Lemma
4.8.3
imply (
4.8.14
). This completes the proof.

∎

Next, in the case when
|\beta|>2
we obtain the induction step estimate (
4.5.15
), where
\mu=\beta
, and
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
. This is done by applying the reconstruction theorem for germs.

Proposition 4.8.6
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
|\beta|>2
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}>0
one has

(4.8.24)

\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}r}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\frac{|\Pi_{x\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda}))|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}}\lesssim 1

for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
, where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
.

Suppose also that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
1\leq p<\infty
one has

(4.8.25)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
and
\rho
.
Then for any
1\leq p<\infty
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>2-\alpha
,
0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}

(4.8.26)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
,
x
and
\rho
.

Proof.

Let
F_{x}:=\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
. Then for
\varphi
as in (
4.8.24
) and any
R>0
we have by (
2.4.10
), (
2.6.2
), (
2.6.3
), (
2.6.4
), and (
2.6.6
)

\left\|(F_{x+h}-F_{x})(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}=\|\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\hskip-14.22636pt\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\circ({\rm Id}-\Gamma_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\leq

\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x+h\gamma}^{-}(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p^{\prime}\infty B_{R}x}\left\|(\Gamma_{x+hx})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{p^{\prime\prime}\infty B_{R}x},

where
\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{p}
,
1\leq p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}<\infty
, and at the last step we used the Hölder inequality.

Now using (
4.8.24
) and (
4.8.25
) we obtain for
|h|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}

\left\|(F_{x+h}-F_{x})(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}|h|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}\lambda^{|\gamma|-2}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where at the last step we used (
4.7.8
).

Thus

\left\|F\right\|_{p\infty R\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}\varphi}^{\alpha-2~|\beta|-2~0}=\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:|h|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}}\frac{\left\|(F_{x+h}-F_{x})(\varphi_{x+h}^{\lambda})\right\|_{p\infty B_{R}x}}{\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|h|)^{|\beta|-\alpha}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
R
. Hence by (
2.4.25
) with
{\bf n}={\bf 0}
and by Corollary
3.2.6
(ii) we obtain for
r>2-\alpha

\left\|F\right\|_{p\infty 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}Rr}^{|\beta|-2~0}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{|\beta|-2}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
R
. Finally, the last estimate and Lemma
4.8.3
imply (
4.8.26
). This completes the proof.

∎

The assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
imply those in Lemma
4.8.2
for
|\beta|<2
(see Corollary
4.7.5
), and together with the results of Lemma
4.8.2
they imply the assumptions in Corollary
4.8.4
. In turn, the results of Corollary
4.8.4
together with the assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
