imply those in Proposition
4.8.5
if
|\beta|<2
. The assumptions of Proposition
4.5.11
are also stronger that those in Proposition
4.8.6
if
|\beta|>2
. Therefore the induction step estimate (
4.5.15
), where
\mu=\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
, follows from Proposition
4.8.5
if
|\beta|<2
and from Proposition
4.8.6
if
|\beta|>2
. More precisely, we have the following corollary which implies the induction step estimate for
\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}
in Proposition
4.5.11
.

Corollary 4.8.7
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimate (
4.8.14
) holds with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.8.5
if
|\beta|<2
, and estimate (
4.8.26
) holds with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.8.6
if
|\beta|>2
. In particular, the induction step statement for the estimate (
4.5.15
), where
\mu=\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
, in Proposition
4.5.11
holds.

Finally we can obtain a preliminary estimate for
\Pi_{x\beta}
using Lemma
4.8.2
, Corollary
4.8.4
, Propositions
4.8.5
,
4.8.6
and Schauder estimates for germs. The proper induction step estimate for
\Pi_{x\beta}
will be obtained in the next section as it is based on the induction step estimates for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{\beta}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
derived there.

Proposition 4.8.8
.

Assume that
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
and that the conditions of Lemma
4.8.2
and of Corollary
4.8.4
are satisfied if
|\beta|<2
, and that the conditions of Proposition
4.8.6
are satisfied if
|\beta|>2
. Suppose also that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}\geq 1
. Then for any
r>\max\{2-\alpha,|\beta|\}
,
1\leq p<\infty
one has

(4.8.27)

\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}r}^{|\beta|}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\frac{\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\beta|}}=

=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}\frac{\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\beta|}}\lesssim 1,

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\rho
.

Proof.

If
|\beta|<0
, by translational invariance in law, the estimate in the statement follows from Lemma
4.8.2
, Corollary
4.8.4
, Proposition
4.8.5
and from (
3.2.5
) with
b=|\beta|-2
and
F_{x}=\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
as in this case by (
2.4.9
)

\Pi_{x\beta}=\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
.

If
|\beta|>0
, by translational invariance in law, the estimate in the statement follows from Lemma
4.8.2
, Corollary
4.8.4
, Proposition
4.8.5
for
|\beta|<2
or from Proposition
4.8.6
for
|\beta|>2
, and from (
3.2.9
) with
b=|\beta|-2
,
F_{x}=\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
along with the definition of
\Pi_{x\beta}
in (
2.4.9
).

∎

The arguments in the discussion before Corollary
4.8.7
, along with the fact that the inequality
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}\geq 1
is also ensured by the assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
, and Proposition
4.8.8
immediately lead to the following statement.

Corollary 4.8.9
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimate (
4.8.27
) holds with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.8.8
.

4.9.
The induction step for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}

In this section we complete the proof of the induction step statement by establishing estimates for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
. We start with
the following auxiliary result which can be found in
[
6
]
, Section 5.5, proof of Lemma 3.15.

Lemma 4.9.1
.

For any
N>0
, and any
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
with
|{\bf m}|\leq N
there exists a function
\phi\in\mathcal{D}
supported in
B_{\frac{1}{4}}
such that for
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
0\leq|{\bf n}|\leq N

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(-x)^{\bf n}\phi(x)dx=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&{\rm if}~{\bf n}={\bf m}\\
0&{\rm otherwise}\end{array}\right..

We shall also need the following lemma which will be used several times in the proofs in this section.

Lemma 4.9.2
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{2k}})\cap\mathcal{B}^{r}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
k>0
. Then for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
with
|z|\leq\frac{1}{2k}
the function
\tilde{\phi}(y):=\phi(y+z)
is an element of
\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{k}})\cap\mathcal{B}^{r}
, and
\left\|\tilde{\phi}\right\|_{C^{r}}=\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r}}
is independent of
z
.

Proof.

It follows from the triangle inequality in the form
|y|\leq|z|+|y+z|
that for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
with
|z|\leq\frac{1}{2k}
and
|y+z|\leq\frac{1}{2k}
,
k>0
one has
|y|\leq\frac{1}{k}
. Thus the function
\tilde{\phi}(y)
is supported in
B_{\frac{1}{k}}
as
\phi\in\mathcal{D}
is supported in
B_{\frac{1}{2k}}
.

The remaining part of the statement is obvious by the translational invariance of the
C^{r}
–norm.

∎

In Propositions
4.9.3
,
4.9.7
,
4.9.8
below we shall obtain the induction step estimates for
(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
, respectively. These proofs are similar and use a trick called the three–point argument in
[
5
]
.

Proposition 4.9.3
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
, estimates (
4.6.1
) hold for
\mu=\beta
, for all
1\leq p<\infty
and all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
. Suppose also that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}\geq 1
and that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
, and for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>{\rm max}\{2-\alpha,|\beta|\}
(
4.7.23
) holds and (
4.8.27
) also holds.

Then for any
1\leq p<\infty
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
0<|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
one has

(4.9.1)

\left\|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\delta_{\bf n}|},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
and
\rho
.

Proof.

Note that by translational invariance in law it suffices to consider the case
y=0
.

By (
2.6.6
), (
2.4.11
), (
2.6.2
), (
2.6.3
) and (
2.6.4
) we have

\Pi_{0\beta}(y)=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma}+\Pi_{x\beta}(y)=

=\Pi_{x\beta}(y)+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}.

Using the definition
\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)=(y-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}
we can rewrite the previous identity as follows

\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}(y-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=

=\Pi_{0\beta}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma}-\Pi_{x\beta}(y).

Now we multiply this identity by the function
\phi^{\lambda}_{x}(y)
and integrate with respect to
y
over
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where
\phi
is given in Lemma
4.9.1
with
N=|\beta|_{\prec}
and arbitrary fixed
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf m}|<N
. This yields

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf m}}}(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=\Pi_{0\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\circ(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Thus using the Hölder inequality we obtain

(4.9.2)

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}+\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{\prime}}}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{\prime\prime}}},

where
\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{p}
,
1<p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}<\infty
.

Now assume that
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
.

By Lemma
4.9.2
, if
\frac{|x|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
then for any
r\in\mathbb{N}

\phi^{\lambda}_{x}=\tilde{\phi}^{\lambda}_{0}
, where
\tilde{\phi}(y):=\phi(y+R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}x)\in C_{r}\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
C_{r}\geq 0
, and
\left\|\tilde{\phi}\right\|_{C^{r}}
is independent of
x
and
\lambda
.
Therefore for
|x|=\frac{\lambda}{2}
we can apply (
4.8.27
) in the first and in the second terms. We can also use (
4.6.1
), (
4.7.23
) to estimate the factors in the terms of the sum over
\gamma
. This way we obtain

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|}+\lambda^{|\beta|}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}|x|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}\lambda^{|\gamma|}\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|}+(|x|+\lambda)^{|\beta|},

where at the last step we used (
4.7.8
) in the sum.
Now (
4.9.1
) is obtained from this estimate by substituting
\lambda=2|x|
.

∎

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
, which ensure, in particular, that (
4.7.23
) holds, from Corollaries
4.6.4
,
4.8.9
and from Proposition
4.9.3
we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 4.9.4
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimate (
4.9.1
) holds with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.9.3
, and estimate (
4.5.14
) holds for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
with
\mu
replaced by
\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
, and with the same other conditions as in the assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
, where
\mu
is replaced by
\beta
, and
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
.

At this point, based on the result of the previous proposition, we can finally justify the induction step estimate for
\Pi_{0\beta}
.

Proposition 4.9.5
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
estimates (
4.6.1
) hold for
\mu=\beta
, for all
1\leq p<\infty
and all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}
. Suppose also that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}\geq 1
and that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
, and for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>{\rm max}\{2-\alpha,|\beta|\}
(
4.7.23
) holds and (
4.8.27
) also holds.

Then for any
1\leq p<\infty
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{4}
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}

(4.9.3)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
,
x
and
\rho
.

Proof.

By (
2.6.6
), (
2.6.2
), (
2.6.3
) and (
2.6.4
) we can write for
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}

\Pi_{0\beta}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\circ(\Gamma_{x0})^{\gamma}_{\beta}+\Pi_{x\beta}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda}).

Thus by the Hölder inequality in probability we get for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}
with the help of (
4.7.23
), (
4.8.27
) and (
4.6.1
)

\left\|\Pi_{0\beta}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\left\|(\Gamma_{x0})^{\gamma}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}+\left\|\Pi_{x\beta}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}:\\
\alpha\leq|\gamma|\leq|\beta|\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}\leq|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\lambda^{|\gamma|}|x|^{|\beta|-|\gamma|}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\beta|-\alpha},

where at the last step we used (
4.7.8
). This completes the proof.

∎

From Corollary
4.9.4
and Proposition
4.9.5
we immediately derive the following statement.

Corollary 4.9.6
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimate (
4.9.3
) holds with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.9.5
, and estimate (
4.5.16
) holds with
\mu
replaced by
\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
, and with the same other conditions as in the assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
, where
\mu
is replaced by
\beta
, and
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
.

As promised, to complete the proof of the induction step we proceed with the induction step estimates for
({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
(S_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
.

Proposition 4.9.7
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
estimate (
4.6.4
) (resp. (
4.6.6
)) holds for
\mu=\beta
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
(resp.
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
),
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
(resp.
S_{x+yx}
) defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
. Suppose also that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>4-\alpha
(
4.7.21
) and (
4.7.23
) hold, and that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}\geq 1
.

Then for any
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{4}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(4.9.4)

\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\delta_{\bf n}|},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
R
,
\upsilon
,
\rho
.

Proof.

By (
2.4.11
), (
3.3.20
) we have

\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}.

Using the germ

F_{x}(y):=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(y)-\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}

and the definition
\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)=(y-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}
we can rewrite the previous identity as follows

\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}(y-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=

=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(y)-F_{x}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Now we multiply this identity by the function
\phi^{\lambda}_{x}(y)
and integrate with respect to
y
over
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where
\phi
is given in Lemma
4.9.1
with
N=|\beta|_{\prec}
and arbitrary fixed
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf m}|<N
. This yields

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf m}}}({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})-F_{x}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Thus

(4.9.5)

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\leq\left\|\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}+\left\|F_{x}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{q^{\prime}\infty B_{R}x}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{q^{\prime\prime}2B_{R}x},

where
\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{q}
,
1\leq q^{\prime}<\infty
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime\prime}<p^{*}<2
, and
\upsilon
satisfies
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

Now assume that
0<\lambda\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}
,
R=\frac{\lambda}{2}
.

By Lemma
4.9.2
, if
\frac{|x|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
then for any
r\in\mathbb{N}

\phi^{\lambda}_{x}=\tilde{\phi}^{\lambda}_{0}
, where
\tilde{\phi}(y):=\phi(y+R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}x)\in C_{r}\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
C_{r}\geq 0
, and
\left\|\tilde{\phi}\right\|_{C^{r}}
is independent of
x
and
\lambda
.
Therefore for
|x|\leq R
we can apply Proposition
4.7.4
, conditions of which are guaranteed by the conditions of this proposition, in the first term. We can also use (
4.7.31
), applicability of which is ensured by the conditions of this proposition as well, to estimate the second term and (
4.6.4
), (
4.7.23
) to estimate the factors in the terms of the sum over
\gamma
. This way we obtain

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim|\lambda|^{|\beta|}R^{\frac{d}{2}}+\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}\lambda^{|\gamma|}.

Now (
4.9.4
) is obtained from this estimate by substituting
\lambda=2R
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4.9
.

The proof of Proposition
4.4.9
is similar to that of Proposition
4.9.7
and even simpler as, if
\beta=0
, from the definition of the modified homogeneity it follows that the sum over
\gamma
in the right hand side of (
4.9.5
) is empty. To estimate the first and the second terms in the right hand side of (
4.9.5
) when
\beta=0
we use Proposition
4.4.5
for the first term and (
4.4.3
) for the second one. The remaining arguments are the same as in the proof of Proposition
4.9.7
.

∎

Proposition 4.9.8
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|<2
estimate (
4.6.1
) holds for all
1\leq p<\infty
and all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
, and estimates (
4.6.1
), (
4.6.4
) (resp. (
4.6.6
)) hold for
\mu=\beta
, for all
1\leq p<\infty
and all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
(resp.
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2}
),
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
and
{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
(resp.
S_{x+yx}
) defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
. Suppose also that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>4-\alpha
(
4.7.21
) and (
4.7.23
) hold, and that
\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}}{2}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}\geq 1
.

Then for any
R\in\mathbb{R}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
0<R,|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{4}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
1\leq q<p^{*}<2
,
S_{x+yx}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(4.9.6)

\left\|(S_{x+yx})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|y|^{\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|+\alpha}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}&{\rm if}~\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|+\alpha>0\\
(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-|{\bf n}|+\frac{d}{2}}&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
R
,
\upsilon
,
\rho
and
y
.

Proof.

By (
2.4.11
), (
3.3.24
) we have

\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(z)\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+y\gamma}(z)\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=

=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+y\gamma}(z)\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+y\delta_{\bf n}}(z)\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}.

Using the germ

F_{x}(z):=\delta\Pi_{0\beta}(z)-\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(z)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma},

property (
2.6.6
) and the definitions of
\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(z)=(z-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}
and
S_{x+yx}={\rm d}\Gamma_{x+y}-\Gamma_{x+yx}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
we can rewrite the previous identity as follows

\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}(z-x-y)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+y\delta_{\bf n}}(z)\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=

=F_{x}(z)-F_{x+y}(z)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+y\gamma}(z)\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Now we multiply this identity by the function
\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y}(z)
and integrate with respect to
z
over
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where
\phi
is given in Lemma
4.9.1
with
N=|\beta|_{\prec}
and arbitrary fixed
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf m}|<N
. This yields

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf m}}}(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=F_{x}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})-F_{x+y}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x+y\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\circ(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Thus

(4.9.7)

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}\left\|(S_{x+yx})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\leq\left\|F_{x}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}+\left\|F_{x+y}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x+y\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q^{\prime}\infty B_{R}x}\left\|(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{q^{\prime\prime}2B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\left\|F_{x}(\tilde{\phi}^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}+\left\|F_{x}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{q2B_{R+|y|}x}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x+y\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y})\right\|_{q^{\prime}\infty B_{R}x}\left\|(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{q^{\prime\prime}2B_{R}x},

where
\tilde{\phi}(z):=\phi(z+R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}y)
,
\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{q}
and
1\leq q^{\prime}<\infty
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime\prime}<p^{*}<2
, and
\upsilon
satisfies
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
.

Now assume that
0<\lambda\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{2}
,
0<R\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{4}
.
By Lemma
4.9.2
, if
\frac{|y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
then for any
r\in\mathbb{N}

\phi^{\lambda}_{x+y}=\tilde{\phi}^{\lambda}_{x}
, and
\tilde{\phi}(z)=\phi(z+R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}y)\in C_{r}\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
C_{r}\geq 0
, and
\left\|\tilde{\phi}\right\|_{C^{r}}
is independent of
y
and
\lambda
.
Therefore for
|y|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{4}
we can use (
4.7.31
), applicability of which is ensured by the conditions of this proposition, to estimate the first and the second terms and (
4.6.6
), (
4.7.23
) to estimate the factors in the terms of the sum over
\gamma
. This way we obtain

(4.9.8)

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}\left\|(S_{x+yx})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}+\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}(\lambda+R+|y|)^{|\beta|-\alpha}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha>0\\
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})\neq 0\end{array}}|y|^{\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}\lambda^{|\gamma|}+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha\leq 0\\
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})\neq 0\end{array}}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}\lambda^{|\gamma|}.

Now observe that for
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
with
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})\neq 0

|\gamma|=(\alpha+1)\gamma({\bf g})+\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})(|{\bf n}|-\alpha)+\alpha>\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})|{\bf n}|=|\gamma|_{p}

as
(\alpha+1)\gamma({\bf g})+\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})(-\alpha)+\alpha>0
in this case.
Using this observation we obtain from (
4.9.8
) for
\lambda=2|y|

(4.9.9)

\left\|(S_{x+yx})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim|y|^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}+

+|y|^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha>0\\
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})\neq 0\end{array}}\large\left(\frac{|y|}{|y|+R}\large\right)^{|\gamma|-|\gamma|_{p}}+

+|y|^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha\leq 0\\
\sum_{{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}}\gamma({\bf n})\neq 0\end{array}}\large\left(\frac{|y|}{|y|+R}\large\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|-\alpha}\lesssim

\lesssim|y|^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|}(|y|+R)^{|\beta|-\alpha}

as in the last sum
-\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|-\alpha>-\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha\geq 0
.

Now the first case in (
4.9.6
) is obtained from this estimate if
\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf m}|+\alpha>0
.

If
\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|+\alpha\leq 0
one can obtain a better estimate as in the second case in (
4.9.6
). This condition implies
|{\bf n}|\geq\frac{d}{2}+\alpha=2-{\varepsilon}
, and hence by (
4.7.26
)

({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=({\rm d}\Gamma_{x}^{*}{\bf z}_{\delta_{{\bf n}}})_{\beta}=0,~|{\bf n}|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}.

Thus

(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=(\Gamma_{x+yx}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}(\Gamma_{x+yx})_{\gamma}^{\delta_{\bf n}}({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

For
\gamma=\delta_{\bf m}
,
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
in this sum one must have by (
4.7.26
) in the non–zero terms
|{\bf m}|<2-{\varepsilon}
. On the other hand in this case
(\Gamma_{x+yx})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=0
by (
2.6.2
) as
|{\bf n}|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}
, and conditions (
2.6.2
), (
2.6.9
) together with (
3.3.5
) and (
3.3.20
) imply that the expression for
(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}
is reduced to

(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|{\bf n}|\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}(\Gamma_{x+yx})_{\gamma}^{\delta_{\bf n}}({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Thus we obtain by (
4.6.1
) and by Proposition
4.6.2
for
0<R,|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}}{4}

\left\|(S_{x+yx})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|{\bf n}|\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\left\|(\Gamma_{x+yx})_{\gamma}^{\delta_{\bf n}}\right\|_{q^{\prime}\infty B_{R}x}\left\|({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\right\|_{q^{\prime\prime}2B_{R}x}\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|{\bf n}|\leq|\gamma|<|\beta|+\frac{d}{2}\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}|y|^{-|{\bf n}|+|\gamma|}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|\gamma|}\lesssim

\lesssim(|y|+R)^{\frac{d}{2}+|\beta|-|{\bf n}|},

where
\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{q}
,
1<q^{\prime}<\infty
,
1\leq q<q^{\prime\prime}<p^{*}<2
,
\upsilon
satisfies
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
, and at the last step we used (
4.7.8
). This completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4.10

The proof of Proposition
4.4.10
is similar to that of Proposition
4.9.8
and even simpler as, if
\beta=0
, the case
\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|+\alpha\leq 0
is trivial by (
3.3.24
). Indeed, in this case

(S_{x+yx})_{0}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=0
since
|{\bf n}|=|\delta_{\bf n}|_{\prec}\geq|0|_{\prec}=2-{\varepsilon}=\frac{d}{2}+\alpha
.

If
\frac{d}{2}-|{\bf n}|+\alpha>0
from the definition of the modified homogeneity it follows that the sum over
\gamma
in the right hand side of (
4.9.7
) is empty. To estimate the first and the second terms in the right hand side of (
4.9.7
) when
\beta=0
we use (
4.4.3
). The remaining arguments are the same as in the proof of Proposition
4.9.8
.

∎

As we mentioned when describing the strategy of the proof of Proposition
4.5.11
, in Sections
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
the conditions in each statement depend on the results obtained in the preceding statements only and on the induction assumptions imposed in Proposition
4.5.11
. Therefore Propositions
4.9.7
and
4.9.8
imply the following corollary.

Corollary 4.9.9
.

Under the induction step assumptions in Proposition
4.5.11
estimates (
4.5.17
) and (
4.5.18
) hold for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
and with
\mu
replaced by
\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
and with the same conditions as in Proposition
4.5.11
.

5.
Convergence of models

In this section we show that the smooth models
(\Pi_{x}(\rho),\Gamma_{xy}^{T}(\rho))
converge in a certain sense when
\rho\to 0
and the mollification is removed. The proof of this statement is based on a Cauchy property for models which we state in the next section.

5.1.
The Cauchy property for models

The proof of the Cauchy property for models closely follows the same steps as the proof of Theorem
4.3.1
. We begin by stating this property in this section.

For any function
F(\rho)
of a real variable
\rho>0
we denote
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}F:=F(\rho)-F(\rho^{\prime})
. In the statement below we keep the notation of Theorem
4.3.1
.

Theorem 5.1.1
.

For
\rho>0
, fixed
N\geq 17
and
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{0}}{32^{M(N)}}\geq 1
let
(\Pi_{x}(\rho),\Gamma_{xy}^{T}(\rho))
be the smooth models defined in Theorem
4.3.1
.

Then for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma,\mu\in\mathcal{M}
the following inequality holds

(5.1.1)

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\mu}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}|x-y|^{|\mu|-|\gamma|},

for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}
if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
,
k=0,\ldots,M(N)
.
The left hand side of this inequality is zero if
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
.

Also, for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma,\mu\in\mathcal{M}
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
r>{\rm max}\{2-\alpha,|\mu|\}
the following inequalities hold

(5.1.2)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},

(5.1.3)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{0\mu}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}
if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
,
k=0,\ldots,M(N)
. The left hand sides of inequalities (
5.1.2
) and (
5.1.3
) are equal to zero if
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
.

The constants in inequalities (
5.1.1
), (
5.1.2
) and (
5.1.3
) do not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
,
x
,
y
and
\rho
,
\rho^{\prime}
.

This theorem is proved by induction over the modified homogeneity similarly to Theorem
4.3.1
. The proof will be presented in Section
5.3
. In the next section we obtain the desired estimates in the case of the base of this induction when
\mu=0
.

5.2.
The base case

In the base case when
\mu=0
the proof of estimates in Theorem
4.3.1
differs considerably from the proofs of analogous statements in Section
4.4
. We start with the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

For any
0\leq\nu\leq 1
,
1\leq p<\infty
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
the function

\phi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\eta(y)\varphi(x-R_{\nu}y)dy-\varphi(x).

satisfies

\left\|\phi\right\|_{L_{p}}\lesssim\nu~\max_{i=0,1,2,3}\left\|\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|_{L_{p}},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\eta
.

Proof.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\eta(y)=1
we can write

\phi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\eta(y)(\varphi(x-R_{\nu}y)-\varphi(x))dy.

By the Newton formula and by the chain rule we have

\left|\varphi(x-R_{\nu}y)-\varphi(x)\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{dt}\varphi(x-tR_{\nu}y)dt\right|\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\varphi(x-tR_{\nu}y)\right|dt\leq

\leq\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{3}\left|\frac{\partial\varphi(x-tR_{\nu}y)}{\partial x_{i}}\right|\left|(R_{\nu}y)_{i}\right|dt\leq\nu\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{3}\left|\frac{\partial\varphi(x-tR_{\nu}y)}{\partial x_{i}}\right|\left|y_{i}\right|dt,

where at the last step we used the fact that
0\leq\nu\leq 1
, so that
\nu^{2}\leq\nu
, and hence for
R_{\nu}y=(\nu^{2}y_{0},\nu y_{1},\nu y_{2},\nu y_{3})
each component satisfies
\left|(R_{\nu}y)_{i}\right|\leq\nu\left|y_{i}\right|
.

Now from the previous estimate we obtain using the Minkowski inequality and translational invariance of the
L_{p}
–norm

\left\|\phi\right\|_{L_{p}}\leq\nu\int_{0}^{1}\left\||\eta(y)|\sum_{i=0}^{3}\left\|\frac{\partial\varphi(x-tR_{\nu}y)}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\left|y_{i}\right|\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4},y)}dt\leq

\leq\nu~\max_{i=0,1,2,3}\left\|\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|_{L_{p}}\left\||\eta(y)|\sum_{i=0}^{3}\left|y_{i}\right|\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4},y)}\lesssim\nu~\max_{i=0,1,2,3}\left\|\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x_{i}}\right\|_{L_{p}},

where at the last step we used the fact that
\eta\in\mathcal{S}
. This completes the proof.

∎

The remaining statements in this section have counterparts in Section
4.4
. As in that section we start with estimates for Malliavin derivatives.

Proposition 5.2.2
.

For any
1\leq q<2
,
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
and
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{q}(L_{2})
satisfying
\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1
one has

(5.2.1)

\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}\right\|_{q\bar{\lambda}_{0}1}^{\alpha-2}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{1}}\frac{\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}}{\lambda^{\alpha-2}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon},

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
and the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
\rho\geq\rho^{\prime}
. Let
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}
.

Consider first the case when
\nu:=\frac{\rho}{\lambda}\geq 1
. Then under the assumption
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}
by (
4.4.2
) we have for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{1}

\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq\left\|\delta\Pi_{00}^{-}(\rho)(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}+\left\|\delta\Pi_{00}^{-}(\rho^{\prime})(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim

(5.2.2)

\lesssim\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\leq\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\left(\frac{\rho}{\lambda}\right)^{\varepsilon}=({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha-2}.

If
\nu=\frac{\rho}{\lambda}\leq 1
by the definition of
\Pi_{00}^{-}(x)=\xi^{\rho}(x)=\xi(\eta^{\rho}_{x})
we have for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{1}

(5.2.3)

\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}=\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon+\upsilon-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq

\leq\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon)(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}+\left\|(\upsilon-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}.

Now we estimate the first term in in the right hand side. The second one is estimated in a similar way.

We proceed as in (
4.4.2
) using the Hölder and the Young convolution inequalities, and the assumption

\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1

which yields

\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon\right)(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\left\|\eta^{\rho}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})-\varphi^{\lambda}(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq

\leq\left\|\eta^{\rho}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})-\varphi^{\lambda}(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}=\left\|\phi^{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}=\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)},

where

\phi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\eta(y)\varphi(x-R_{\nu}y)dy-\varphi(x).

Now since
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{1}
we have by Lemma
5.2.1
with
p=2

(5.2.4)

\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon)(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\nu\leq\nu^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}=\rho^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha-2},

as for
i=0,1,2,3

\left\|\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x_{i}}(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\lesssim 1
, and
\nu\leq\nu^{e}
.
Substituting the last estimate and a similar estimate with
\rho
replaced by
\rho^{\prime}
into (
5.2.3
) we obtain

\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha-2}

This estimate together with (
5.2.2
) imply the statement of this proposition.
This completes the proof.

∎

Now, as in Proposition
4.4.5
we proceed with an estimate
\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}
using Schauder estimates for
\mathcal{K}
.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

For any
1\leq q<2
,
\bar{\lambda}_{0}\geq 1
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\geq 1
and
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{q}(L_{2})
satisfying
\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1
one has

\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}\right\|_{q\bar{\lambda}_{0}r}^{\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{\left\|\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}}{\lambda^{\alpha}}\lesssim({\rm\max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon},

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
and the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
and
r
.

Proof.

Since by (
2.4.4
)
\Pi_{00}
depends on
\xi
polynomially, one obviously has from (
2.4.4
)

\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}=\mathcal{K}\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}.

Observe also that
r\geq 1>|\alpha|={\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}
.
Hence, the estimate in the statement follows from (
5.2.1
), and from (
3.2.5
) with
x=0
, and
F=\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}
.

∎

The next statement is an analogue of Proposition
4.4.6
for differences of germs.

Proposition 5.2.4
.

For any
\bar{\lambda}_{0}\geq 1
,
R>0
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\geq 2
,
1\leq q<2
,
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{q}(L_{2})
with
\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1
the germ

F_{x}(y):=\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}(y)-\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(\Pi_{x\gamma}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{0}^{\gamma})

satisfies

(5.2.5)

\left\|F\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}_{0}Rr}^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\left\|F_{x}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(B_{R},x)}}{\lambda^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon},

where
\delta=\delta_{\upsilon}
, and the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
and
r
.

Proof.

By (
3.3.8
)

F_{x}(y)=\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}(y)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<2-{\varepsilon}\end{array}}\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)\circ({\rm d}\Gamma_{x})_{0}^{\delta_{\bf n}}).

From this observation and (
3.3.9
) we deduce that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}

F_{x}(y)
is obtained from
\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}(y)
by subtracting its Taylor polynomial at
x
of parabolic degree
1
. Thus
F_{x}=(\delta\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00})_{x}^{2-{\varepsilon}}=(\mathcal{K}(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}))_{x}^{2-{\varepsilon}}
, and we can apply (
3.2.10
) with
b=-{\varepsilon}
,
r>2-{\varepsilon}
to get

(5.2.6)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}F\right\|_{q2\bar{\lambda}_{0}B_{R}r}^{2-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{q2~2\bar{\lambda}_{0}B_{R}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}\leq

\leq\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{q2~2\bar{\lambda}_{0}\mathbb{R}^{4}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{q2}^{-{\varepsilon}}},

where at the last step we used definition (
3.1.4
).

Now we estimate the right hand side using definition (
3.1.6
) of the space
\mathbb{B}_{q2}^{-{\varepsilon}}
according to which

(5.2.7)

\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{q2}^{-{\varepsilon}}}\lesssim\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\frac{\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}}{\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}},

where
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}
is such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\varphi(x)dx\neq 0
. Thus we obtain

(5.2.8)

\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{q2~2\bar{\lambda}_{0}B_{R}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathbb{B}_{q2}^{-{\varepsilon}}}\lesssim

\lesssim\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\frac{\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}}{\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}}.

As
1\leq q<2
we can proceed further by the Minkowski inequality,

(5.2.9)

\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}\right\|_{q2~2\bar{\lambda}_{0}B_{R}r}^{-{\varepsilon}}\lesssim\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\frac{\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}}{\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}}.

Now assume without loss of generality that
\rho\geq\rho^{\prime}
, and as before
0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}_{0}
.
If
\nu:=\frac{\rho}{\lambda}\geq 1
we use the fact that
{\varepsilon}>0
, the triangle inequality and (
4.4.7
) to obtain

(5.2.10)

\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq

\leq\left\|\left\|\upsilon^{\rho}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}+\left\|\left\|\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim 1\leq\nu^{\varepsilon}=({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{-{\varepsilon}}.

Now consider the case when
\frac{\rho}{\lambda}\leq 1
. Then we proceed from (
5.2.9
) by the triangle inequality as follows

(5.2.11)

\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}=\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon+\upsilon-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq

\leq\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon)(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}+\left\|\left\|(\upsilon-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}.

We shall estimate the first term in the right hand side of the previous inequality. The second one can be estimated in a similar way. We have by the the Young convolution inequality

(5.2.12)

\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon)(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\left\|\eta^{\rho}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})-\varphi^{\lambda}(x)\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}=

=\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\left\|\eta^{\rho}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})-\varphi^{\lambda}(x)\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\leq\left\|\phi^{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}=\left\|\phi\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4})},

where

\phi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\eta(y)\varphi(x-R_{\nu}y)dy-\varphi(x),

and we also used the fact that

\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1.

Now since
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{1}
we have by Lemma
5.2.1
with
p=1

\left\|\phi\right\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\lesssim\nu
. Thus from (
5.2.12
) we deduce

\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon)(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim\nu\leq\nu^{\varepsilon}\leq({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{-e}.

This estimate and a similar estimate with
\rho
replaced by
\rho^{\prime}
together with (
5.2.11
) yield

\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{-e}.

Finally from the last estimate and (
5.2.10
) we deduce that

\left\|\left\|(\upsilon^{\rho}-\upsilon^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{-e}

for all
\rho,\rho^{\prime}>0
and
0<\lambda\leq 2\bar{\lambda}_{0}
, where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
and
r
. Using this estimate, (
5.2.6
) and (
5.2.8
) we obtain the desired estimate. This completes the proof.

∎

Next, as in Proposition
4.4.8
we can get an estimate for
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}^{-}
with the help of the spectral gap inequality.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

For any
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
, and any
1\leq p<\infty

(5.2.13)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}^{-}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}_{0}0}^{\alpha-2}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{0}}\frac{\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{\alpha-2}}=

=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{0}}\frac{\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{\alpha-2}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
and
p
.

Proof.

By translational invariance in law it suffices to consider again the case
x=0
, and by the Hölder inequality in probability one can restrict to the case when
p>2
. Also, the equality in the right hand side of (
4.4.8
) is obvious as
\Pi_{x0}^{-}=\xi^{\rho}
does not depend on
x
.

We shall use spectral gap inequality (
3.1.7
) for
f=\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{0}
.
Since the underlying Gaussian measure is centered,
\mathbb{E}(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0}))=\mathbb{E}((\xi^{\rho}-\xi^{\rho^{\prime}})(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0}))=0
, and the spectral gap inequality takes the form

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2}):\\
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1\end{array}}\left\|\delta_{\upsilon}\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq

\leq\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2}):\\
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1\end{array}}\left\|\delta_{\upsilon}\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}},

where
1\leq q\leq p^{*}<2<p
,
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{*}}=1
, and at the last step we used the Hölder inequality in probability.

Thus by Proposition
5.2.2
with
q=p^{*}
for
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}
one has

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha-2},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
and
p
. This implies (
5.2.13
).

∎

Finally, using Schauder estimates we can obtain an estimate for
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}
similarly to Proposition
4.4.8
.

Proposition 5.2.6
.

For any
\bar{\lambda}_{0}\geq 1
,
r\geq 1
and any
1\leq p<\infty

(5.2.14)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}\right\|_{p\bar{\lambda}_{0}r}^{\alpha}=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{\alpha}}=

=\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{00}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{\alpha}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon},

where the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
,
p
and
r
.

Proof.

By translational invariance in law, the estimate in the statement follows from (
5.2.13
) and from (
3.2.5
) with
b=|0|-2=\alpha-2
and
F_{x}=\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}^{-}
, as in this case by (
2.4.9
)

\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}=\mathcal{K}\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{x0}^{-}
.

∎

The last two propositions in this section will be only used in the next induction step in the proof of Theorem
5.1.1
.

Proposition 5.2.7
.

For any
1\leq q<2
,
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
,
0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}
and
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{q}(L_{2})
with
\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1
one has

(5.2.15)

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{0}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}R^{\frac{d}{2}+\alpha-|\delta_{\bf n}|},

where
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon
, and the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
. The left hand side of the inequality vanishes if
|{\bf n}|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}
.

The vanishing property follows from Proposition
4.4.2
.
The proof of estimate (
5.2.15
) is similar to the proof of Proposition
4.4.9
.

Proposition 5.2.8
.

For any
1\leq q<2
,
\bar{\lambda}_{0}>0
,
R\in\mathbb{R}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
0<R,|y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{0}
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
and
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{q}(L_{2})
with
\left\|\left\|\upsilon(x)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4},x)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{q}}\leq 1
one has

(5.2.16)

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}S_{x+yx})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{0}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}|y|^{\frac{d}{2}+\alpha-|\delta_{\bf n}|},

where
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}S_{x+yx}
is defined with the help of
\upsilon
, and the constant in the inequality only depends on
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
. The left hand side of the inequality vanishes if
|{\bf n}|\geq 2-{\varepsilon}
.

The vanishing property follows from Proposition
4.4.3
.
The proof of estimate (
5.2.16
) is similar to the proof of Proposition
4.4.10
.

5.3.
The induction step statement

The induction step statement in the proof of Theorem
5.1.1
resembles Proposition
4.5.11
used in the proof of Theorem
4.3.1
.

Let
\omega\in\mathcal{D}
and
\psi
be the functions fixed Section
4.5
.

Proposition 5.3.9
.

Let
\rho,\rho^{\prime}>0
and
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{8}\geq 1
, and
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
\beta\neq 0
. Suppose that the conditions of Proposition
4.5.11
are satisfied for the recentered maps and the structure group maps of the smooth model as in Proposition
4.5.11
with both mollification parameters
\rho,\rho^{\prime}>0
, and that for all
1\leq p<\infty
,
\gamma,\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
one has

(5.3.17)

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma_{xy}\right)^{\gamma}_{\mu}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}|x-y|^{|\mu|-|\gamma|},~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
x
and
y
and
\rho
.

Assume also that for any
1\leq p<\infty
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
, and any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
for some (or for any)
r>{\rm max}\{2-\alpha,|\mu|\}
one has

(5.3.18)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim

\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},~x\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},~0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

and

(5.3.19)

\left\|\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{0\mu}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim

\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\lambda^{\alpha}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},~x\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},~0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

where the constants in the inequalities do not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
,
x
and
\rho
.

If
|\beta|<2
, assume in addition that the recentered map
\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\rho)
,
\Pi_{0\beta}^{-}(\rho^{\prime})
satisfy the generalized BPHZ condition with the function
\psi^{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}
, and that for all
1\leq q<2
,
\gamma,\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
q<p^{*}<2
,
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
and
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}S_{x+yx}
defined with the help of
\upsilon\in\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}(L_{2})
such that
\left\|\left\|\upsilon\right\|_{L_{2}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p^{*}}}\leq 1
one has

(5.3.20)

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x})^{\gamma}_{\mu}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}R^{\frac{d}{2}+|\mu|-|\gamma|},~0<R\leq\bar{\lambda}_{\prec},

and

(5.3.21)

\left\|(\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}S_{x+yx})^{\gamma}_{\mu}\right\|_{q2B_{R}x}\lesssim({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}\times

\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|y|^{\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha}(|y|+R)^{|\mu|-|\gamma|+|\gamma|_{p}-\alpha}&{\rm if}~\frac{d}{2}-|\gamma|_{p}+\alpha>0\\
(|y|+R)^{|\mu|-|\gamma|+\frac{d}{2}}&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.,~0<R,|y|\leq\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{2},

where the constants in the inequalities are independent of
\rho
,
R
,
\upsilon
and
y
.

Then if
|\beta|<2
(resp.
|\beta|\geq 2
) estimates (
5.3.17
), (
5.3.18
), (
5.3.19
), (
5.3.20
) and (
5.3.21
) (resp. (
5.3.17
), (
5.3.18
), (
5.3.19
)) hold with
\mu
replaced by
\beta
,
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}}{32}
, and under the same conditions as in the assumptions, where
\mu
is replaced by
\beta
, and
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
is replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
.

Strategy of the proof.

The strategy of the proof of this proposition is the same as that of Proposition
4.5.11
: it is based on analogues of statements in Sections
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
for the differences
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma_{xy}
,
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}
,
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\Pi_{0\mu}
,
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}{\rm d}\Gamma_{x}
,
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}S_{x+yx}
. In the proof we can can assume that Proposition
4.5.11
is already proved, and estimates (
4.5.14
), (
4.5.15
), (
4.5.16
), (
4.5.17
) and (
4.5.18
) for
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
,
\mu=\beta
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
hold.

Then one can prove the same statements as in Sections
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
, where in the statements all functions of
\rho
are replaced with
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}
applied to these functions and the right hand sides in all estimates are multiplied by
({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}
.

The order of the statements remains the same as in Sections
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
, and the arguments in the proofs are also almost the same if we make the following simple observation. All estimates, arguments and identities which appear in the proofs in Sections
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
remain valid verbatim if we apply
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}
to their right hand sides and left hand sides using the following formal rules.
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(1)=({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}
,
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}
is
\mathbb{R}
–linear, commutes with all norms and satisfies the product rule: for any functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
of
\rho>0

(5.3.22)

\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(F_{1}\ldots F_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}F_{1}(\rho^{\prime})\ldots F_{i-1}(\rho^{\prime})\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(F_{i})F_{i+1}(\rho)\ldots F_{n}(\rho),

where the meaning of the product
F_{1}\ldots F_{n}
depends on the context. E.g., for real valued functions, like norms of recentered maps, this is the usual product, for the recentered maps and the structure group maps it is an appropriate composition
\circ
, etc. Applicability of these formal rules stems from the fact that all expressions in Sections
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
depend on products of functions of
\rho
estimates for which are already known, and in each statement the estimates for the differences of the functions at different points
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
, which are used in the proof, are known either by the induction assumption or by the assumption in the statement. So to estimate differences of products of such functions at different points
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
one should use the same telescoping trick as in the proof of the product rule for differentiation. Then to estimate a norm of an expression like
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(F_{1}\ldots F_{n})
in (
5.3.22
) one can use the known estimates for the factors in the products in the right hand side. Note that in each such product only one difference
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(F_{i})
appears, and hence the final estimate will only contain one factor
({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}
in the right hand side, as by the induction assumption only estimates of differences
\Delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}(F_{i})
can give factors
({\rm max}\{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\})^{\varepsilon}
.

Estimates (
5.3.17
), (
5.3.20
) and (
5.3.21
) for
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
,
\mu=\beta
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
can be established similarly to Propositions
4.6.1
,
4.6.2
and
4.6.3
, respectively, and for
\mu=\beta
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
they can be obtained similarly to Propositions
4.9.3
,
4.9.7
, and
4.9.8
, respectively.

Estimate (
5.3.18
) for
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
,
\mu=\beta
can obtained using the telescoping trick and similarly to estimate (
4.5.15
) in Lemma
4.8.2
, Corollary
4.8.4
, Proposition
4.8.5
for
|\beta|<2
, and to Proposition

for
|\beta|>2
.

Estimate (
5.3.19
) for
\bar{\lambda}_{\prec}
replaced by
\bar{\lambda}
,
\mu=\beta
can be obtained similarly to Proposition
4.9.5
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1.1
.

Firstly, as in the proof of Theorem
4.3.1
note that by Lemma

(iv) and (v) estimate (

) holds for all

and for all

with any

, and by (

), (

By (

and for any

.

Therefore it suffices to establish estimates (

), (

) and (

) for the model defined in Theorem
4.3.1
, and for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, since for
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
the left hand sides of these inequalities are equal to zero. We can also assume in the proof that estimates (
5.3.17
), (
5.3.18
), (

), (

) and (

) hold for all

and for any

.

We claim that for the model defined in Theorem
4.3.1
estimates (
5.1.1
), (

) and (
5.1.3
) hold for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
. The proof, which is similar to that of Theorem

, is by induction over the modified homogeneity.

Firstly, recall that
\mu=0
is the only element of
\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
with minimal possible modified homogeneity,
|0|_{\prec}=2-{\varepsilon}
. For
\mu=0
and arbitrary

and

for (

and

. Conditions (

Note that by the condition
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 1
, where

is the same as in the proof of Theorem

, the induction step can be iterated until we exhaust the set of all multi-indices

with

with

and
