∎

5.4.
Convergence of models

In this section we confirm that the smooth models
(\Pi_{x}(\rho),\Gamma_{xy}^{T}(\rho))
converge in a certain sense when
\rho\to 0
. To this end we have to define a space of models where this convergence takes place. In view of the definition of
(\Pi_{x}(\rho),\Gamma_{xy}^{T}(\rho))
and of Theorems
4.3.1
and
5.1.1
this task naturally leads to a wider class of models which we are going to introduce now.

We keep the notation used in Theorem
4.3.1
.
By a model
(\Pi_{x},\Gamma_{xy}^{T})
of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 1
for the regularity structure with model space
T
we mean a family of random variables
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
and
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
defined for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\beta|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
with values in
\mathcal{S}_{\beta}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\otimes V_{\beta}
,
{\mathcal{S}_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{-}:=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\otimes V_{\beta}^{-}\simeq\mathcal{S}_{\beta}^{\prime}
, and
{\rm Hom}_{\mathbb{R}}(W_{\beta},W_{\gamma})
, respectively, and such that the following properties are satisfied for all
x,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

(1)

\Pi_{x\delta_{{\bf n}}}(y)=(y-x)^{{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}^{V}.

(2)

If
\beta\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\beta|_{\prec}<N
then
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}\in\mathbb{P}_{|\beta|-2}\otimes V_{\beta}^{-}
is defined by formula (
2.4.5
), where
\Phi_{\beta}^{-}
is replaced by
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
, and
\Phi_{\beta_{i}}
are replaced by
\Pi_{x\beta_{i}}
, and
\beta_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
i=1,2,3
in this case.

(3)

\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}=0~{\rm for}~\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime},

\Pi_{x\beta}=0~{\rm for}~[\beta]<0,~\beta\neq\delta_{{\bf n}},~{{\bf n}}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}.

(4)

For all
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\beta|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0~{\rm if}~|\gamma|>|\beta|~{\rm or}~|\gamma|_{\prec}>|\beta|_{\prec},

and
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\beta}={\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}
.

(5)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{0}=\delta_{\beta}^{0}{\rm Id}_{W_{0}},~(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf g}}=\delta_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf g}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{\bf g}}}.

(6)

For all
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\beta|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N

(\Gamma_{xz})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}\leq|\delta|_{\prec}\leq|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|\leq|\delta|\leq|\beta|\end{array}}(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta}^{\gamma}(\Gamma_{yz})_{\beta}^{\delta}.

(7)

For any multi–indices
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\beta|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
,
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}\neq 0\in\mathcal{M}
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
l>0
one has

(5.4.1)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\in\mathcal{M}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{p}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\\
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|,i=1,\ldots,p\\
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec},i=1,\ldots,p\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}\ldots(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{p}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{p}}},

where we assume that an empty sum is zero and an empty product is 1.

Moreover, in the non–zero terms in the sum in the right hand side of (
5.4.1
)
[\beta_{i}]<[\beta]
,
i=1,\ldots,p
.

(8)

(5.4.2)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\delta_{\bf m}}^{\gamma}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{{\bf m}\choose{\bf n}}(x-y)^{{\bf m}-{\bf n}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\delta_{{\bf m}}}}^{W_{\delta_{{\bf n}}}}&{\rm if}~\gamma=\delta_{\bf n},~{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right..

(9)

\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp},~\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}\Longrightarrow(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0,

\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0},~\beta\not\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\Longrightarrow(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=0.

(10)

For
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
the only nonzero
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}\neq 0
occur if for
{\bf m}:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\gamma({\bf n}_{i}){\bf n}_{i}
,
{\bf k}:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\beta({\bf n}_{i}){\bf n}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
one has
m_{i}\leq k_{i}
for
i=0,1,2,3
,
W_{\beta}
is naturally isomorphic to
W_{\gamma}
, and in this case
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
is given by formula (
2.6.12
),

(5.4.3)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=A_{\beta}^{\gamma}(x-y)^{{\bf k}-{\bf m}}{\rm Id}_{W_{\beta}}^{W_{\gamma}}.

(11)

For all
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\beta|_{\prec}<N

(5.4.4)

\Pi_{y\beta}=\Pi_{x\beta}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta}\circ(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta}_{\beta},

(5.4.5)

\Pi_{y\beta}^{-}=\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta}^{-}\circ(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta}_{\beta}.

(12)

For any
h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\beta|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N

\Pi_{x\beta}(y)=_{\rm law}\Pi_{x+h\beta}(y+h),~\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}(y)=_{\rm law}\Pi_{x+h\beta}^{-}(y+h),

(\Gamma_{x+hy+h})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=_{\rm law}(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

(13)

If for
\mu\in{\bf Mult}
we denote by
r_{\mu}
the minimal possible natural number such that
r>{\rm max}\{2-\alpha,|\mu|\}
,
\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{M}_{k}\cup\{\beta\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:|\beta|_{\prec}<N\}
then for all
1\leq p<\infty

(5.4.6)

\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{Np}:=\max_{0\leq k\leq M}\max_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N\\
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}\end{array}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\\
x\neq y\\
|x-y|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}\end{array}}\frac{\left\|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{|x-y|^{|\mu|-|\gamma|}}<\infty,

(5.4.7)

\left\|\Pi^{-}\right\|_{Np}:=\max_{0\leq k\leq M}\max_{\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\mu|-2}}=

=\max_{0\leq k\leq M}\max_{\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\mu|-2}}<\infty,

and

(5.4.8)

\left\|\Pi\right\|_{Np}:=\max_{0\leq k\leq M}\max_{\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\mu|}}=

=\max_{0\leq k\leq M}\max_{\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}}\frac{\left\|\Pi_{0\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{0})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}}{\lambda^{|\mu|}}<\infty.

Note that the identities in the left hand sides of the last two formulas follow from item (12) of this definition.

Remark 5.4.1
.

By (5), (8), (9), (10) for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
and
\mu=0
one has the deterministic bound

(5.4.9)

\max_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N\end{array}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\\
x\neq y\end{array}}\frac{|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\mu}|}{|x-y|^{|\mu|-|\gamma|}}<\infty.

Indeed, by properties (8), (9) and (10) in the definition of models of level
N
if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
then we can assume that
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
in the right hand side of (
5.4.6
), and if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
then we can assume that
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
. Hence the deterministic bound follows from (
5.4.2
) and (
5.4.3
) in these cases.

If
\mu=0
the bound trivially follows from (4) and the first property in (5).

By (1), (2) and (3) for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
one has also the deterministic bounds

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{0<\lambda<\infty}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}}\frac{|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})|}{\lambda^{|\mu|-2}}<\infty,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{0<\lambda<\infty}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}}\frac{|\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})|}{\lambda^{|\mu|}}<\infty.

Remark 5.4.2
.

Properties (5) and (7) imply that for any multi–indices
\beta,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\beta|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec},|\gamma_{1}|_{\prec},|\gamma_{2}|_{\prec}<N
,
\gamma=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\neq 0\in\mathcal{M}
, one has

(5.4.10)

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in\mathcal{M}:\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\\
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|,i=1,2\\
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec},i=1,2\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{p}}^{\gamma_{2}},

where we assume that an empty sum is zero and an empty product is 1. Clearly, iterating (
5.4.10
) and using (5) one can obtain (
5.4.1
) as in the proof of identity (
2.6.16
). We prefer to use (
5.4.1
) in the definition of models of level
N
to keep it transparent for future references.

Observe that arguments similar to those in the proof of Lemma
4.8.3
and Proposition
4.9.5
imply that actually estimates in item (13) in the previous definition lead to the following estimates which hold for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x|\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}
,
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}_{k}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\prime}
,
k=0,\ldots,M(N)
, and any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r_{\mu}}
:

(5.4.11)

\left\|\Pi_{0\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha-2}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},

(5.4.12)

\left\|\Pi_{0\mu}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha}(\lambda+|x|)^{|\mu|-\alpha},

where the constants in the inequalities do not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
, and
x
.

Now we can introduce a space where the convergence of models
(\Pi_{x}(\rho),\Gamma_{xy}^{T}(\rho))
takes place.
Let
{\rm Md}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
be the set of all models of level
N
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
with the model space
T
. If
(\Pi,\Gamma^{T})
,
(\Pi^{\prime},{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{T})
are two such models we introduce a family of pseudo–distances
{\rm d}_{Np}
,
1\leq p<\infty
between them by

{\rm d}_{Np}((\Pi,\Gamma^{T}),(\Pi^{\prime},{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{T}))=\left\|\Gamma-\Gamma^{\prime}\right\|_{Np}+\left\|\Pi^{-}-{\Pi^{\prime}}^{-}\right\|_{Np}+\left\|\Pi-\Pi^{\prime}\right\|_{Np}.

Equip
{\rm Md}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
with a topology defined by the family of pseudo–distances
{\rm d}_{Np}
,
1\leq p<\infty
, where a sequence of models in
{\rm Md}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
converges if it converges with respect to all pseudo–distances
{\rm d}_{Np}
,
1\leq p<\infty
. Denote by
\overline{{\rm Md}}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
the corresponding topological space obtained by the metric identification. Its topology is defined by the family of metrics corresponding to
{\rm d}_{Np}
,
1\leq p<\infty
. We denote these new metrics by the same symbols. When we speak about a model in the space
\overline{{\rm Md}}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
we mean its equivalence class in
\overline{{\rm Md}}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
.

Note that the models in Theorems
4.3.1
,
5.1.1
belong to
{\rm Md}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
, where
N\geq 17
. This follows from the estimates obtained in Theorem
4.3.1
, from the definition of the recentered maps in Section
2.4
(see especially properties (
2.4.8
), (
2.4.10
), (
2.4.11
) and Lemma
2.4.1
(iii)), from the properties of the structure group maps stated in Proposition
2.6.1
(i)-(v), Lemma
2.6.2
and from (
4.2.1
), (
4.2.2
).

Now from Theorem
5.1.1
using the Cauchy criterion and from formulas (
2.4.4
)
we immediately obtain the following result

Theorem 5.4.3
.

For
N\geq 17
the models
(\Pi(\rho),\Gamma^{T}(\rho))\in\overline{{\rm Md}}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
,
\rho>0
defined in Theorem
4.3.1
converge to a model
(\Pi,\Gamma^{T})\in\overline{{\rm Md}}_{N\bar{\lambda}_{0}}
when
\rho\to 0
. This model, called a model of level
N
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
for the regularity structure with model space
T
associated to equation (
1.2.8
), also satisfies
\Pi_{x0}^{-}=\xi
,
\Pi_{x0}=\mathcal{K}\Pi_{x0}^{-}=\mathcal{K}\xi
, where the last formula should be understood in the sense of generalized functions, and by Lemma
2.2.1
and by Proposition
2.2.3
it defines a random variable with values in
\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\prime}
.

6.
Pointwise estimates for models

In this section we show that the recentered maps and the structure group maps for models of level
N
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
introduced in the previous section almost surely satisfy pointwise estimates similar to (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.11
) and (
5.4.12
).

6.1.
Preliminary pointwise estimates for recentered maps with a fixed test function

We start with poinwise estimates for the recentered maps.
As in the previous section we keep the notation used in Theorem
4.3.1
. For any
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
we also denote by
|\beta|_{-}
the corrected homogeneity which is defined by formula (
2.1.5
) where
\alpha
is replaced with
\alpha_{-}:=\alpha-{\varepsilon}_{-}
for some fixed arbitrary
{\varepsilon}_{-}
satisfying
0<{\varepsilon}_{-}<\frac{{\varepsilon}}{100}
.

By a weight we mean a function
w\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
such that
w(x)>0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,

(6.1.1)

\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\frac{1}{w^{p}({\bf m})}<\infty,

for
p>\frac{12}{{\varepsilon}_{-}}
, and for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
\left\|x-y\right\|\leq 2
or
|x-y|\leq 2

(6.1.2)

\frac{1}{w(x)}\leq C\frac{1}{w(y)}.,

where
C>0
is independent of
x
and
y
.

We shall also denote
B_{1}^{e}(x):=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:\left\|y-x\right\|\leq 1\}
.

An example of a weight is the function
w(x)=(1+\left\|x\right\|^{2})^{\frac{5{\varepsilon}_{-}}{24}}
. Indeed, condition (
6.1.1
) can be verified for it using the multi–dimensional generalization of the integral convergence test for series, and condition (
6.1.2
) follows from the obvious estimate for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
obtained with the help of the triangle inequality,

1+\left\|y\right\|^{2}\leq 1+2(\left\|x\right\|^{2}+\left\|x-y\right\|^{2})

which for
\left\|x-y\right\|\leq 2
yields

1+\left\|y\right\|^{2}\leq 9+2\left\|x\right\|^{2}\leq 9(1+\left\|x\right\|^{2}),

and for
|x-y|\leq 2
it gives

1+\left\|y\right\|^{2}\leq 1+2A^{2}+2\left\|x\right\|^{2}\leq(1+2A^{2}+2)(1+\left\|x\right\|^{2}),

where
A=\max_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{4},|z|\leq 2}\left\|z\right\|
. The last two displayed inequalities imply (
6.1.2
).

Consider a model of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
for the regularity structure with model space
T
, with recentered maps
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
and structure group maps
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
.

We shall obtain pointwise estimates for
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
and
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
which hold almost surely.

Proposition 6.1.1
.

Let
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
be the recentered maps of a model of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
. Then for any weight
w
, any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{4}})
, for all
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\beta|_{\prec}<N
,
x,h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
0\leq|h|\leq\frac{1}{4}
,
0<\lambda\leq 8
one has almost surely

(6.1.3)

|\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}({{}^{h}}\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})|\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|_{-}-2}w(x),

(6.1.4)

|\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|_{-}}w(x),

where
{{}^{h}}\varphi(z)=\varphi(z+h)
, the constants in the inequalities do not depend on
h
,
\lambda
, and
x
, and they depend on the model via quantities (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.7
) and (
5.4.8
), the dependence being linear for each of them.

Proof.

We prove (
6.1.4
). Estimate (
6.1.3
) is established in a similar way.

We shall apply the usual Kolmogorov continuity trick using a Sobolev type inequality (compare with
[
17
]
, Proof of Theorem 3.3 or Appendix B in
[
4
]
). Firstly we shall derive some preliminary estimates.

Fix
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\beta|_{\prec}<N
. Firstly we obtain a local estimate similar to (
6.1.4
).
Let
\phi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{2}})
,
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
x,y\in B_{1}(v)
, so that
|x-y|\leq 2
, and
0<\lambda\leq 8
.

For
1\leq p<\infty
we start by estimating
\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{y}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
.

If
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
we use the Taylor formula and write for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{4}

\phi_{x}^{\lambda}(z)=\frac{1}{\lambda^{d}}\phi(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-z))=\frac{1}{\lambda^{d}}\phi(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(y-z))+

+\frac{1}{\lambda^{d}}\sum_{i=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{i}\phi)(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(y-z+t(x-y)))(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y))_{i}dt=

=\phi_{y}^{\lambda}(z)+\sum_{i=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}{{}^{i}\phi}_{y}^{\lambda}(z)(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y))_{i}dt,

where, due to the condition
t\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
one has for
i=0,1,2,3
that
{}^{i}\phi(z):=(\partial_{i}\phi)(z+tR_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y))\in\mathcal{D}(B_{1})
by Lemma
4.9.2
,
\left\|{}^{i}\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}=\left\|\partial_{i}\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}
is independent of
x,y,\lambda,t
, and
\left\|{}^{i}\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}=\left\|\partial_{i}\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}\leq\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}
.

Since the condition
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
also implies
|(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y))_{i}|\leq\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}
from the previous identity and (
5.4.8
) we obtain that for all
1\leq p<\infty

\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{y}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}=\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}\Pi_{y\beta}({{}^{i}\phi}_{y}^{\lambda})(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y))_{i}dt\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq

\leq\sum_{i=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}({{}^{i}\phi}_{y}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}|(R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y))_{i}|dt\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}\sum_{i=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}\lambda^{|\beta|-1}|x-y|dt\lesssim

(6.1.5)

\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}\lambda^{|\beta|-1}|x-y|\leq\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}|x-y|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

where
0<{\varepsilon}^{\prime}<{\varepsilon}_{+}(N):=\min\{1,|\gamma|-|\delta|:\gamma,\delta\in\mathcal{M},|\gamma|>|\delta|,|\gamma|_{\prec},|\delta|_{\prec}<N\}
, the constant in the inequality is independent of
\phi
,
\lambda
,
x
and
y
, and at the last step we used that
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\left(\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\right)^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}
as
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

For
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
we can also obtain using (
5.4.4
), (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.8
) and the Hölder inequality that

(6.1.6)

\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\left\|\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\circ(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim

\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\beta|\end{array}}\left\|\Pi_{x\delta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\left\|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta}_{\beta}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\beta|\end{array}}|x-y|^{|\beta|-|\delta|}\lambda^{|\delta|}=

=\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}|x-y|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\beta|\end{array}}\left(\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\right)^{|\beta|-|\delta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}|x-y|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\phi
,
\lambda
,
x
and
y
and at the last step we used that
\left(\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\right)^{|\beta|-|\delta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{|\beta|-|\delta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}
for
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}
as
|\beta|-|\delta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}>0
by the choice of
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
.

Now from (
6.1.5
) and (
6.1.6
) we obtain for
|x-y|\leq 2
, and
0<\lambda\leq 8
,
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}

(6.1.7)

\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{y}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq

\leq\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{y}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}+\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}|x-y|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\phi
,
\lambda
,
x
and
y
.

For
|x-y|\leq 2
, and
0<\lambda\leq 8
and
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{2}
we immediately derive from (
5.4.8
)

(6.1.8)

\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{y}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}}}\lambda^{|\beta|}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}|x-y|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\phi
,
\lambda
,
x
and
y
, and we used the fact that
\left(\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\right)^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\geq(\frac{1}{2})^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}
for
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

Finally from (
6.1.7
) and (
6.1.8
) we obtain that for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2
,
0<\lambda\leq 8
,
1\leq p<\infty

(6.1.9)

\left\|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi_{y}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}|x-y|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\phi
,
\lambda
,
x
and
y
.

Now for
1\leq p<\infty
by (
6.1.9
) and (
3.1.2
) with
d+\vartheta={\varepsilon}^{\prime}p>2d
,
f(x)=\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})
and for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
x\in B_{1}(v)
,
0<\lambda\leq 8
we have

(6.1.10)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}|\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p})\lesssim\mathbb{E}|\Pi_{v\beta}(\phi_{v}^{\lambda})|^{p}+

+\iint_{B_{1}(v)\times B_{1}(v)}\frac{\mathbb{E}|\Pi_{z\beta}(\phi_{z}^{\lambda})-\Pi_{z^{\prime}\beta}(\phi_{z^{\prime}}^{\lambda})|^{p}}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p}}dzdz^{\prime}\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{p|\beta|}+\iint_{B_{1}(v)\times B_{1}(v)}\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{p{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\lambda^{p(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p}}dzdz\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}\lambda^{p(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\phi
,
\lambda
,
x
and
v
.

Next, observe that by Lemma
4.9.2
for
h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|h|\leq\frac{1}{4}
we have
{{}^{h}}\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{2}})
for
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{4}})
.
Now we use (
3.1.3
) with
f(t)=\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t})
,
a=|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
,
\bar{\lambda}=8
,
1<p<\infty
subject to
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p>2d
to estimate using Fubini’s theorem

(6.1.11)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p})\leq

\leq\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\int_{0}^{8}(|t^{-a}f(t)|^{p}+|t^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(t)|^{p})dt)\leq

\leq\int_{0}^{8}(\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}|t^{-a}f(t)|^{p})+\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}|t^{-a+1}f^{\prime}(t)|^{p}))dt.

By Sobolev’s embedding
C^{0}(B_{\frac{1}{4}})\supset W_{p}^{1}(B_{\frac{1}{4}})
,
p>4
for
F\in W_{p}^{1}(B_{\frac{1}{4}})
we have

(6.1.12)

\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}|F(h)|^{p}\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}}}(|F(h)|^{p}+\sum_{i=0}^{3}|\partial_{i}F(h)|^{p})dh.

Applying this inequality to the first and to the second terms in the right hand side of (
6.1.11
) with
F=f
and
F=f^{\prime}
, respectively, we obtain for
p>4

(6.1.13)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p})\lesssim

\lesssim\int_{0}^{8}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}}}(\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}t^{-a}(|\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t})|^{p}+\sum_{i=0}^{3}|\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\partial_{i}\varphi)_{x}^{t})|^{p}))+

+\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}t^{-a+1}(|\Pi_{x\beta}(\frac{d}{dt}({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t})|^{p}+\sum_{i=0}^{3}|\Pi_{x\beta}(\frac{d}{dt}({{}^{h}}\partial_{i}\varphi)_{x}^{t})|^{p})))dtdh.

Note that by the choice of
\varphi
and
h
we have by Lemma
4.9.2

{{}^{h}}\varphi,{{}^{h}}\partial_{i}\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{2}})
, and

(6.1.14)

\left\|{{}^{h}}\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}=\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}},\left\|{{}^{h}}\partial_{i}\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}=\left\|\partial_{i}\varphi\right\|_{C^{r}}\leq\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r+2}},~r\in\mathbb{N}.

Therefore we can estimate the terms in the right hand side of (
6.1.13
) using (
6.1.10
).

For instance, by (
6.1.10
) with
\phi={{}^{h}}\varphi
for
0<t\leq 8
we obtain for
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p>2d

(6.1.15)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}|t^{-a}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t}|^{p})=\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}|t^{-(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t}|^{p})\lesssim

\lesssim\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}}t^{-p(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}t^{p(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}=\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+2}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
h
,
t
,
v
and
\varphi
.

Similarly, since by the chain rule

\frac{d}{dt}({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t}=\frac{1}{t}({{}^{h}}\hat{\varphi})_{x}^{t},

where

{{}^{h}}\hat{\varphi}(z)=-d({{}^{h}}\varphi)(z)-2({{}^{h}}\partial_{0}\varphi)(z)z_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{3}({{}^{h}}\partial_{i}\varphi)(z)z_{i}\in\mathcal{D}(B_{\frac{1}{2}}),

we can estimate for
0<t\leq 8
,
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p>2d

(6.1.16)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}|t^{-a+1}|\Pi_{x\beta}(\frac{d}{dt}({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{t})|^{p})=

=\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}|t^{-(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})+1-1}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\hat{\varphi})_{x}^{t})|^{p})\lesssim

\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+4}}t^{-p(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}t^{p(|\beta|-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}=\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+4}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
t
,
v
and
\varphi
.

Now using (
6.1.15
) and (
6.1.16
) and similar estimates for the remaining terms in the right hand side of (
6.1.13
), which can be obtained by replacing
\varphi
with
\partial_{i}\varphi
in (
6.1.15
) and (
6.1.16
), we obtain from (
6.1.13
), noting the second inequality in (
6.1.14
),

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}(v)}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p})\lesssim\int_{0}^{8}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+6}}dtdh\lesssim

(6.1.17)

\lesssim\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+6}},

where
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p>2d
,
p>4
, and the constant in the inequality is independent of
v
and
\varphi
.

Next, we can obtain the global estimate as follows (compare the second line in the formula below with Definition 5.1 in
[
8
]
)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})\frac{1}{w(x)}|^{p})=

=\mathbb{E}(\sup_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\sup_{x\in B_{1}^{e}({\bf m})}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p}\frac{1}{w^{p}(x)})\leq

\leq C^{p}\mathbb{E}(\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\sup_{x\in B_{1}^{e}({\bf m})}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p}\frac{1}{w^{p}({\bf m})}),

where we used (
6.1.2
) with
y={\bf m}
and the fact that the balls
B_{1}^{e}({\bf m})
,
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
cover
\mathbb{R}^{4}
which follows from the inclusions
\Upsilon_{\bf m}:=\{x=(x_{0},x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:|x_{i}-m_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{2},~i=0,1,2,3\}\subset B_{1}^{e}({\bf m})
,
{\bf m}=(m_{0},m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
, and from the obvious identity
\mathbb{R}^{4}=\bigcup_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\Upsilon_{\bf m}
.

Now we proceed by the monotone convergence theorem which yields

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})\frac{1}{w(x)}|^{p})\leq

\leq C^{p}\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}^{e}({\bf m})}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p})\frac{1}{w^{p}({\bf m})},

Next, observe that from the definition of the norms
\left\|\cdot\right\|
and
|\cdot|
it follows that
B_{1}^{e}({\bf m})\subset B_{1}({\bf m})
for all
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
, and hence

(6.1.18)

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})\frac{1}{w(x)}|^{p})\leq

\leq C^{p}\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in B_{1}({\bf m})}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|^{p})\frac{1}{w^{p}({\bf m})}.

Choose
p
such that
p>\max\{4,\frac{11}{{\varepsilon}_{+}(N)},\frac{12}{{\varepsilon}_{-}}\}
and
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}=\frac{11}{p}
, so that
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}p=11>10=2d
,
p>4
and
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}=\frac{11}{p}<{\varepsilon}_{+}(N)
as required in (
6.1.17
).
Therefore we can estimate the expectations in the right hand side of (
6.1.18
) using (
6.1.17
),

\mathbb{E}(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{h\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 8}|\lambda^{-|\beta|+{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})\frac{1}{w(x)}|^{p})\lesssim\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+6}}\frac{1}{w^{p}({\bf m})}\lesssim

(6.1.19)

\lesssim\left\|\varphi\right\|_{C^{r_{\beta}+6}},

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
\varphi
, and at the last step we used (
6.1.1
) which implies
\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}}\frac{1}{w^{p}({\bf m})}<\infty
.

Inequality (
6.1.19
) obviously yields that almost surely for
x,h\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|h|\leq\frac{1}{4}
,
0<\lambda\leq 8
,

(6.1.20)

|\Pi_{x\beta}(({{}^{h}}\varphi)_{x}^{\lambda})|\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|-({\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p})}w(x),

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
h
,
\lambda
, and
x
.

Note that by the choice of
p
we have
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}=\frac{12}{p}
. Thus, since
[\beta]\geq 0
and
\frac{12}{p}<{\varepsilon}_{-}
, for
0<\lambda\leq 8
we obtain

\lambda^{|\beta|-({\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p})}=\lambda^{|\beta|-\frac{12}{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}_{-}}\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|-{\varepsilon}_{-}([\beta]+1)},

where the constants in the inequality depends on
{\varepsilon}_{-}
,
\beta
and
p
.

By the definition of the homogeneity

(6.1.21)

|\beta|_{-}=|\beta|-{\varepsilon}_{-}([\beta]+1),

so we deduce that for
0<\lambda\leq 8

\lambda^{|\beta|-({\varepsilon}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p})}\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|_{-}},

and hence with our choice of
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
and
p
inequality (
6.1.20
) gives (
6.1.4
).

By (
6.1.5
)-(
6.1.9
) the constant in (
6.1.10
) depends on the model via quantities (
5.4.6
) and (
5.4.7
), the dependence being linear for each of them. Therefore the same fact is true for the constant in (
6.1.20
) and in (
6.1.4
). This completes the proof.

∎

6.2.
Pointwise estimates for structure group maps

Poinwise estimates for the structure group maps can be obtained using induction over the modified homogeneity and pointwise analogues of Propositions
4.6.1
and
4.9.3
.
We keep the notation used in Theorem
4.3.1
and in Section
6.1
and fix
{\varepsilon}_{-}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0<{\varepsilon}_{-}<\frac{{\varepsilon}}{100}
, with the help of which the corrected homogeneity is defined.

If
w
is a weight then for
\beta\in{\bf Mult}
we define

w_{\beta}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}w&{\rm if}~\beta=0\\
w^{\beta({\bf g})}&{\rm if}~\beta\neq 0\end{array}.\right.

Consider a model of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
for the regularity structure with model space
T
, with recentered maps
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
and structure group maps
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
.

We shall obtain pointwise estimates for
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
which hold almost surely.

Theorem 6.2.1
.

Let
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\mu}^{\gamma}
be the structure group maps of a model of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
. Then for any weight
w
, for all
\mu,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\mu|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
the following inequality holds almost surely

(6.2.1)

|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\mu}|\lesssim|x-y|^{|\mu|_{-}-|\gamma|_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)w_{\gamma}^{-1}(x)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2
, the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
, and it depends on the model via quantities (
5.4.6
) and (
5.4.8
), the dependence being polynomial, without a constant term.

Remark 6.2.2
.

Note that by (
5.4.1
) for
\gamma\neq 0
one has
\mu({\bf g})\geq\gamma({\bf g})
in (
6.2.1
), and by part (5) in the definition of models in Section
5.4

(\Gamma_{xy})_{\mu}^{0}=\delta_{\mu}^{0}{\rm Id}_{W_{0}}
. Hence
w_{\mu}(x)w_{\gamma}^{-1}(x)=w^{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

First note that by (
5.4.9
) estimates (
6.2.1
) hold for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
. Indeed, as we observed in Remark
5.4.1
in this case we can assume that
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
in (
6.2.1
). But in this case
[\mu]=[\gamma]=-1
, and by (
6.1.21
)
|\mu|_{-}=|\mu|
,
|\gamma|_{-}=|\gamma|
, so (
6.2.1
) is reduced to (
5.4.9
).

Therefore it suffices to consider the case
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
. The proof now is by induction over the modified homogeneity of
\mu
.

If
\mu=0
(
6.2.1
) trivially holds by property (4) in the definition of models of level
N
as it was already observed in Remark
5.4.1
.

Now the induction step follows from the next two propositions which are analogues of Propositions
4.6.1
and
4.9.3
.

Firstly, given that (
6.2.1
) is true for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
\beta\neq 0
we show that (
6.2.1
) holds almost surely for
\mu=\beta
and for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
.

Proposition 6.2.3
.

Suppose that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|_{\prec}<N
and for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
,
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
one has almost surely

(6.2.2)

|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\mu}|\lesssim|x-y|^{|\mu|_{-}-|\gamma|_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)w_{\gamma}^{-1}(x),~x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4},~|x-y|\leq 2,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
x
and
y
.
Then (
6.2.2
) holds almost surely for
\mu=\beta
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
.

Proof.

For
\gamma=0
the statement is void by the first property in part (5) of the definition of models of level
N
in Section
5.4
.

If
\gamma\neq 0
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
then
\gamma=l\delta_{\bf g}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{{\bf m}}_{i}}
,
{\bf m}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
l>0
, and we apply the norm
|\cdot|
to (
5.4.1
), and use property (9) in the definition of models of level
N
and the multiplicative property of the norm to get

(6.2.3)

|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}|\lesssim\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\\
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec},i=1,\ldots,p\\
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|,i=1,\ldots,p\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{1}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{1}}}|\ldots|(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta_{r}}^{\delta_{{\bf m}_{r}}}|.,

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
x
and
y
.

By the induction assumption we now obtain that almost surely for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2

(6.2.4)

|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\gamma}_{\beta}|\lesssim\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta\\
|\beta_{i}|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec},i=1,\ldots,r\\
|\beta_{i}|<|\beta|,i=1,\ldots,r\end{array}}\hskip-28.45274pt|x-y|^{\sum_{i=1}^{r}(|\beta_{i}|_{-}-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|_{-})}\prod_{i=1}^{r}w_{\beta_{i}}(x),

where the constant in the inequality is independent of
x
and
y

By the linearity of the function
|\cdot|_{-}-\alpha_{-}
we obtain for all terms in the right hand side of the last inequality

(6.2.5)

\sum_{i=1}^{r}(|\beta_{i}|_{-}-|\delta_{{\bf m}_{i}}|_{-})=|\beta|_{-}-|\gamma|_{-}.

Since
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{r}+l\delta_{\bf g}=\beta
in the terms in the sum in (
6.2.4
)
we also have
\beta({\bf g})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\beta_{i}({\bf g})+l=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\beta_{i}({\bf g})+\gamma({\bf g})
for each such term. Thus

\prod_{i=1}^{r}w_{\beta_{i}}=w^{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\beta_{i}({\bf g})}=w_{\beta}w_{\gamma}^{-1}.

Substituting this expression and (
6.2.5
) into (
6.2.4
) yields the result.

∎

Next, given that (
6.2.1
) is true for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
with
|\mu|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}
for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}
,
\beta\neq 0
and for
\mu=\beta
with arbitary
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
, we show that (
6.2.1
) holds almost surely for
\mu=\beta
and for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{pp}
,
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
.

Proposition 6.2.4
.

Assume that for some
\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
\beta\neq 0
,
|\beta|_{\prec}<N
, estimates (
6.2.2
) hold for
\mu=\beta
, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{pp}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
|\gamma|_{\prec}<N
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2
.
Then for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
0<|x-y|\leq 2
,
{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf n}|<N
one has almost surely

(6.2.6)

|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta_{\bf n}}_{\beta}|\lesssim|x-y|^{|\beta|_{-}-|\delta_{\bf n}|_{-}}w_{\beta}(x),

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
x,y
.

Proof.

By properties (3), (4) and (11) in the definition of models of level
N
we have

\Pi_{y\beta}(z)=\sum_{\gamma\in{\bf Mult}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(z)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}\cup\mathcal{M}_{pp}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(z)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}+\Pi_{x\beta}(z)=

=\Pi_{x\beta}(z)+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(z)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(z)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}.

Using the definition
\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(y)=(y-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}
we can rewrite the previous identity as follows

\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}(z-x)^{\bf n}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf n}}}(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf n}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}:\\
|{\bf n}|<|\beta|_{\prec}\end{array}}\Pi_{x\delta_{\bf n}}(z)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf n}}=

=\Pi_{y\beta}(z)-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(z)\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}-\Pi_{x\beta}(z).

Now we multiply this identity by the function
\phi^{\lambda}_{x}(z)
and integrate with respect to
z
over
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where
\phi
is given in Lemma
4.9.1
with
N
being the level of the model and arbitrary fixed
{\bf m}\in\mathbb{N}^{4}
,
|{\bf m}|<N
. This yields

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}{\rm Id}^{V}_{W_{\delta_{\bf m}}}(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\delta_{\bf m}}=\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})-\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})-\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})\circ(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}.

Thus using the multiplicativity property of the norm we obtain

(6.2.7)

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}|\lesssim|\Pi_{y\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})|+|\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})|+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})||(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}|=

=|\Pi_{y\beta}({{}^{h}}\phi^{\lambda}_{y})|+|\Pi_{x\beta}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})|+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}|\Pi_{x\gamma}(\phi^{\lambda}_{x})||(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}|,

where
h=R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y)
.

Now assume that
0<\lambda\leq 8
. If
\frac{|x-y|}{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{4}
then we can apply (
6.1.4
) with
w=w_{\beta}
, and
h=R_{\frac{1}{\lambda}}(x-y)
(resp.
h=0
) in the first (resp. in the second) term. We can also use (
6.1.4
) with
h=0
,
w=w_{\gamma}
and (
6.2.2
) to estimate the factors in the terms of the sum over
\gamma
. This way we obtain from (
6.2.7
) that almost surely for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2

\lambda^{|{\bf m}|}|(\Gamma_{xy})^{\delta_{\bf m}}_{\beta}|\lesssim\lambda^{|\beta|_{-}}w_{\beta}(y)+\lambda^{|\beta|_{-}}w_{\beta}(x)+

+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}:\\
|\gamma|_{\prec}<|\beta|_{\prec}\\
|\gamma|<|\beta|\end{array}}|x-y|^{|\beta|_{-}-|\gamma|_{-}}w_{\beta}(x)w_{\gamma}^{-1}(x)\lambda^{|\gamma|_{-}}w_{\gamma}(x)\lesssim(\lambda^{|\beta|_{-}}+(|x-y|+\lambda)^{|\beta|_{-}})w_{\beta}(x),

where at the last step we used (
6.1.2
) and (
4.7.8
) in the sum.
Now (
6.2.6
) is obtained from this estimate by substituting
\lambda=4|x-y|
.

∎

The last two propositions justify the induction step which also completes the proof of
(
6.2.1
).

Note that by (
6.2.3
) and (
6.2.7
) used in the inductive algorithm, the constant in inequality (
6.2.1
) depends on the model via quantities (
5.4.6
) and (
5.4.8
), the dependence being polynomial, without constant term.
This completes the proof of Theorem
6.2.1
.

∎

6.3.
Pointwise estimates for recentered maps

Consider again a model of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
for the regularity structure with model space
T
, with recentered maps
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
and structure group maps
(\Gamma_{xy})_{\beta}^{\gamma}
.

We shall obtain pointwise estimates for
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
which hold almost surely.
We keep the notation used in Theorem
4.3.1
and in Sections
6.1
and
6.2
, and fix
{\varepsilon}_{-}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0<{\varepsilon}_{-}<\frac{{\varepsilon}}{100}
, with the help of which the corrected homogeneity is defined and denote
\alpha_{-}=\alpha-{\varepsilon}_{-}
.

Theorem 6.3.1
.

Let
\Pi_{x\beta}
,
\Pi_{x\beta}^{-}
be the recentered maps of a model of level
N\in\mathbb{R}
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
,
w
the weight used in Theorem
6.2.1
. Then for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
,
r>2-\alpha_{-}
, the following inequalities hold almost surely

(6.3.1)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{y})|\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}-2}(\lambda+|x-y|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}w_{\mu}(x),

(6.3.2)

|\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi_{y}^{\lambda})|\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}(\lambda+|x-y|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)

for all
0<\lambda<\infty
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
if
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
, and if
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
they hold for all
0<\lambda\leq 1
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2
.

The constants in inequalities (
6.3.1
) and (
6.3.2
) do not depend on
\lambda
,
\varphi
,
x
,
y
, and they depend on the model via quantities (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.7
) and (
5.4.8
), the dependence being polynomial without a constant term.

Proof.

First note that for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
one has
[\mu]=-1
, and hence
|\mu|_{-}=|\mu|
by (
6.1.21
). Therefore arguments similar to those used in the proof of (
4.5.19
) and the deterministic bounds in Remark
5.4.1
lead to the following deterministic bounds for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N

(6.3.3)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{y})|\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}-2}(\lambda+|x-y|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}},

(6.3.4)

|\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi_{y}^{\lambda})|\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}}(\lambda+|x-y|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}},

which hold for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and
0<\lambda<\infty
. By the definition of weights these bounds imply (
6.3.1
) and (
6.3.2
) for all
0<\lambda<\infty
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
if
\mu\not\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
.

Therefore it suffices to establish (
6.3.1
) and (
6.3.2
) for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\geq 0}
. We shall prove (
6.3.1
). The other inequality is obtained in a similar way.

Let
\psi=\omega^{2}\ast\omega
,
\omega^{2}
is
\omega
rescaled by
2
, and
\omega
is fixed in (
4.5.12
) with
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>2-\alpha_{-}
.
Observe arguments similar to those used in the proof of (
4.5.19
) or Lemma
4.8.3
, with the
\mathbb{L}_{p}
–norm replaced by
|\cdot|
, and estimates (
6.2.1
),
(
6.1.3
) with
\varphi=\psi
,
h=0
imply that almost surely we have for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2
,
0<\lambda\leq 8

(6.3.5)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{\lambda}_{y})|\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}-2}(\lambda+|x-y|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}w_{\mu}(x).

Indeed, by (
5.4.5
)

(6.3.6)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{\lambda}_{y})|\leq|\Pi_{y\mu}^{-}(\psi^{\lambda}_{y})|+\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}<|\mu|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|<|\mu|\end{array}}|\Pi_{y\delta}^{-}(\psi^{\lambda}_{y})||(\Gamma_{yx})^{\delta}_{\mu}|.

Now we get almost surely using (
6.2.1
) and (
6.1.3
) with
\varphi=\psi
,
h=0
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
|x-y|\leq 2
,
0<\lambda\leq 8
,
w=w_{\mu}
,

(6.3.7)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{\lambda}_{y})|\lesssim\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}\leq|\mu|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|\leq|\mu|\end{array}}\lambda^{|\delta|_{-}-2}|x-y|^{|\mu|_{-}-|\delta|_{-}}w_{\delta}(y)w_{\mu}(x)w_{\delta}^{-1}(y).

Finally, by (
6.1.2
)

w_{\delta}(y)w_{\mu}(x)w_{\delta}^{-1}(y)\lesssim w_{\delta}(x)w_{\mu}(x)w_{\delta}^{-1}(x)=w_{\mu}(x),

and hence (
6.3.7
) takes the form

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{\lambda}_{y})|\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}-2}\sum_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\delta\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\delta|_{\prec}\leq|\mu|_{\prec}\\
|\delta|\leq|\mu|\end{array}}\lambda^{|\delta|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}|x-y|^{|\mu|_{-}-|\delta|_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)\lesssim

\lesssim\lambda^{\alpha_{-}-2}(\lambda+|x-y|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}w_{\mu}(x),

where at the last step we used (
4.7.8
). This proves (
6.3.5
).

Now we proceed as in the proof of Proposition
4.8.5
replacing the
\mathbb{L}_{p}
–norms by
|\cdot|
and repeating the other arguments verbatim.

Let
{\varepsilon}_{n}=2^{-(n+1)}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We use presentation (
4.8.19
) with
0
replaced by
x
, arbitrary
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
and
{\varepsilon}_{n}=2^{-(n+1)}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{x})=

=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{z})(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}})(x-z)dz+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}_{z})(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}})(x-z)dz.

By (
4.8.4
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
one has
{\rm supp}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}})\subset B_{\frac{3}{2}{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\subset B_{2}
, and by the first property in (
4.8.16
)
{\rm supp}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}})\subset B_{\frac{3}{2}{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\subset B_{2}
for
m\geq n
.
By these observations we have from the previous presentation for
\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{x})
,

(6.3.8)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{x})|\leq\int_{B_{\frac{3}{2}{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}(x)}|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}_{z})|dz\left\|\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}+

+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\int_{B_{\frac{3}{2}{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}(x)}|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\psi^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}_{z})|dz\left\|\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}\ast\check{\psi}^{{\varepsilon}_{m}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}.

Using estimates (
4.8.21
), (
4.8.22
) and (
6.3.5
) in (
6.3.8
) we obtain

(6.3.9)

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}_{x})|\leq 2^{nd}\int_{B_{\frac{3}{2}{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}(x)}{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha_{-}-2}({\varepsilon}_{n}+|z-x|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)dz+

+\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}2^{n(r+d)-mr}\int_{B_{\frac{3}{2}{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}(x)}{\varepsilon}_{m}^{\alpha_{-}-2}({\varepsilon}_{m}+|z-x|)^{|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)dz\lesssim

\lesssim(2^{nd-nd}{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha_{-}-2+|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}+{\varepsilon}_{n}^{\alpha_{-}-2+|\mu|_{-}-\alpha_{-}}\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}2^{n(r+d)-mr-m(\alpha_{-}-2)+n(\alpha_{-}-2)-nd})w_{\mu}(x)\lesssim

\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{|\mu|_{-}-2}\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}2^{-(m-n)(r+\alpha_{-}-2)}w_{\mu}(x)\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{|\mu|_{-}-2}w_{\mu}(x),

where the series in the last expression converges due to the condition
r+\alpha_{-}-2>0
and
the constant in the inequality does not depend on
n
,
\varphi
.

Now for any
0<\lambda\leq 1
, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be the largest possible such that
{\varepsilon}_{n-1}=2^{-n}\geq\lambda
, so that
{\varepsilon}_{n}\leq\lambda
, and for
\lambda^{\prime}:={\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{-1}\lambda
we have
\frac{1}{2}\leq\lambda^{\prime}\leq 1
.

Also if
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}
then
\varphi^{\lambda^{\prime}}\in C\mathcal{B}^{r}
, where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\lambda^{\prime}
and
\varphi
.

Since by the definition
\varphi^{\lambda}=(\varphi^{\lambda^{\prime}})^{{\varepsilon}_{n-1}}
we obtain from the last observation and from (
6.3.9
) that

|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi_{x}^{\lambda})|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{n-1}^{|\mu|_{-}-2}w_{\mu}(x)\leq\lambda^{|\mu|_{-}-2}w_{\mu}(x),

where the constant in the inequality does not depend on
\lambda
and
\varphi
.

Finally, the last estimate, which holds for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}
,
|\mu|_{\prec}<N
, (
6.2.1
) and arguments similar to those used in the proof of (
6.3.5
), with
\psi
replaced by
\varphi
, imply (
6.3.1
).

By (
6.3.6
), (
6.3.8
), by Proposition
6.1.1
, and by Theorem
6.2.1
the constant in (
6.3.1
) depends on the model via quantities (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.7
) and (
5.4.8
), the dependence being polynomial without a constant term. This completes the proof.

∎

Since by Theorem
4.3.1
for the smooth models defined in that theorem the quantities (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.7
) and (
5.4.8
) introduced in part (13) of the definition of models of level
N
do not depend on the mollification parameter
\rho
, and by Proposition
6.1.1
and by Theorems
6.2.1
,
6.3.1
the constants in pointwise estimates (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.2
) and (
6.2.1
) may depend on
\rho
only via (
5.4.6
), (
5.4.7
) and (
5.4.8
), we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 6.3.2
.

The recentered maps and the structure group maps of the smooth model of level
N
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
for the regularity structure with model space
T
defined in Theorem
4.3.1
satisfy estimates (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.2
) and (
6.2.1
) with arbitrary weight
w
, and the constants in inequalities (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.2
) and (
6.2.1
) in this case do not depend on the mollification parameter
\rho
.

As a consequence of Theorem
5.4.3
and of the definition of models of level
N
we also obtain another corollary.

Corollary 6.3.3
.

The recentered maps and the structure group maps of the model of level
N
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
for the regularity structure with model space
T
associated to equation (
1.2.8
) and defined in Theorem
5.4.3
satisfy estimates (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.2
) and (
6.2.1
) with arbitrary weight
w
.

The last two corollaries, arguments similar to those presented before Corollary
6.3.2
in conjunction with Theorems
5.1.1
,
5.4.3
and with the usual telescoping trick as in the proof of Proposition
5.3.9
yield the following statement which is a pointwise counterpart of Theorem
5.4.3
.

Corollary 6.3.4
.

Let
\Pi_{x\mu}(\rho)
,
\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\rho)
,
\Gamma_{xy}(\rho)
be the recentered maps and the structure group maps of the smooth model of level
N
at scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
satisfying
\bar{\lambda}_{M(N)}\geq 8
for the regularity structure with model space
T
defined in Theorem
4.3.1
, and
\Pi_{x\mu}
,
\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}
,
\Gamma_{xy}
the recentered maps and the structure group maps of the model of level
N
at the scale
\bar{\lambda}_{0}
for the regularity structure with model space
T
associated to equation (
1.2.8
) and defined in Theorem
5.4.3
. Then for any weight
w
and
r>2-\alpha_{-}
we have almost surely

\max_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\mu,\gamma\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\mu|_{\prec},|\gamma|_{\prec}<N\end{array}}\sup_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:\\
|x-y|\leq 2\end{array}}\frac{|(\Gamma_{xy}-\Gamma_{xy}(\rho))^{\gamma}_{\mu}|}{|x-y|^{|\mu|_{-}-|\gamma|_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)w_{\gamma}^{-1}(x)}\to 0~{\rm when}~\rho\to 0,

\max_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\mu\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\mu|_{\prec}<N\end{array}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 1}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{|\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})-\Pi_{x\mu}^{-}(\rho)(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})|}{\lambda^{|\mu|_{-}-2}w_{\mu}(x)}\to 0~{\rm when}~\rho\to 0,

\max_{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\mu\in\mathcal{M}:\\
|\mu|_{\prec}<N\end{array}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}}\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 1}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{r}}\frac{|\Pi_{x\mu}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})-\Pi_{x\mu}(\rho)(\varphi^{\lambda}_{x})|}{\lambda^{|\mu|_{-}}w_{\mu}(x)}\to 0~{\rm when}~\rho\to 0.
